## Working history and source boundary

**Exact question now under attack.**  The maintained target is tolerance-
dependent: produce one `c>0` such that for every `eta>0` an exact punishment-
floor path has endpoint payoff distance at most `eta` and contains an
individual edge of charge at least `c`.  The path, edge, roots, and endpoints
may all vary with `eta`.  Sections 25A--29 and Euler's review are boundary
data: they validate the aggregate-block compiler and show that neither one
paid row, fixed behavioral block hazard, nor literal replication supplies the
requested exact edge.

**Logical branch framing.**  This producer is used under an instantiated
positive-minimum terminal-semantic frontier.  Such a frontier already rules
out any uniform-equilibrium payoff, whereas a nonempty admissible payoff-near-
return family compiles to one.  Consequently the maintained consumer is,
pointwise, a proof that the full separated paid-row antecedent is impossible;
it is not a request to construct a family coexisting consistently with that
antecedent.  Local tables below test proposed implications only.  They do not
refute the consumer unless they instantiate the global positive-minimum
frontier and separated endpoint, which none does.

**Current source-level boundary.**  Section 31 extracts the exact consequence
of global minimum provenance: a full mover reset transfers a fixed amount of
terminal debt to opponents.  Section 34 applies the existing constrained
reset-face minimizer and upgrades this to an exact semantic point `R` with
`d_m(R)=0`, full opponent transfer, and only the all-Continue cap--Nash root.
If `R` returns to the minimum fiber it omits `m` from positive debt (strict
support drop still needs the separate flat/no-entry hypotheses); otherwise it
is the canonical off-minimum cap barrier.  The paid row remains attached to
the literal full-reset stopping law, not to `R`.

**Honest status and kill criterion.**  The local incidence route is killed by
the independently reviewed Section 30/Corollary 30A regression.  The global
minimum does create the nonlocal debt excursion (Sections 31 and 34), but it
does not make it chronological.  Sections 32--33 show that cap replacement or
arbitrarily accurate payoff lifts can erase all exact charge, and Section 35
shows that even the semantic pair plus terminal coalition law does not port a
fixed paid witness: the latent opponent stopping law matters.  All these
claims are independently reviewed.  The exact remaining gap is therefore to
retain/re-extract one executable paid block at the constrained reset
minimizer, or convert the loss of stopping-law provenance into an exact floor-
admissible excursion whose payoff returns within arbitrary tolerance.  A
purely semantic or terminal-law minimization cannot do this.  Abandon this
line if a complete source-compatible reset shuttle shows that every such
law-retaining selection either stays a fixed distance above the constrained
minimum or loses all exact floor charge; no such full-source table is known.
Section 36 now supplies the first positive half of that shuttle: along the
actual one-dimensional observer-witness port, the paid row is retained and
total debt has an exact convex minimizer/premium dichotomy.  The remaining
obstruction is no longer law retention on this first port; it is either
restored mover debt, or a port minimizer at its high-witness endpoint, and in
both cases conversion of the scalar debt account into an exact floor edge is
still open.  Section 37 adds the exact chronology correction: actual prefixing
does preserve the paid row, scaled by opponent survival.  The mismatch is
instead between the actual prescribed tail `U` needed for that prefix and the
cap tail `B` at which floor-safe Nashification is available.  New Section 38
identifies that seam exactly.  A fixed cap--Nash root transports to `U` iff
every continuing coordinate has enough cap slack to pay
`opponent survival * debt` and every genuinely mixed coordinate pays zero of
that toll.  In particular a sure-Quit cap root transports automatically and
has the same successor at `U`; without sure Quit, every active coordinate of
a transported root must lie on the zero-debt face.  This exact criterion is
proved below and independently reviewed.  The named fixed-
tail positive-absorption theorem then dispatches every reset point whose
literal prescribed payoff is floor-safe and lies below some singleton owner
reward.  The actual surviving source seam is therefore a below-floor
prescribed tail or a wholly non-instant behavioral cap debt, followed in
either case by the still-missing exact payoff re-entry.  In the floor-safe
uniform-singleton-gap subarm, the named fixed-tail estimate already supplies
the required uniform charge `delta/(delta+2M)`; only re-entry remains there.
Section 40 now closes every resulting sure-Quit root except one sharply
identified unique-sure-quitter collision-premium case: the same root becomes
a charge-one self-loop at its successor whenever the quitter's collision-
Continue payoff does not exceed its exact scaled Quit payoff.  Section 41
rules out the other naive re-entry: normalizing and repeating one wholly mixed
root reaches an exact self-loop only when the original edge was already a
payoff self-loop.  The remaining universal move must therefore alter the
outsider root in the unique-sure premium case or alter at least one product
root along the wholly mixed chronology.  Section 42 makes the first demand
strictly stronger: an absorbed outsider face can have a unique mixed Nash
completion with positive premium, so freezing the sure owner and merely
reselecting the outsider Nash root is not a universal repair.  The exact
remaining gap is a source-matched root-changing edge that desaturates or
changes the owner/support while retaining floor admissibility and a fixed
charge, followed by payoff re-entry.

Conjecture-closing thesis: assume, toward contradiction, one fixed positive
`HasTerminalExploitabilityGap reward gamma`.  Let `P` be the coordinatewise
behavioral punishment floor.  Every forced-owner outsider-Nash completion has
owner refusal at least `gamma` by
`quittingAtomicBlockerBalance_le_neg_of_terminalExploitabilityGap`.  The paid
first-disagreement limit supplies the opposite end of one such separated
transition.  The proposed universal producer is a chronological refusal
transport: each forced refusal either produces a positive exact
floor-admissible Nash--Bellman edge, or transfers the sure-Quit anchor while
paying a fixed signed amount into a bounded floor-state account.  Finiteness
then forces a charged return (or an arbitrarily charged bounded prefix), which
the checked positive-cycle/capacity consumers contradict under the same
terminal gap.  This is a thesis, not a proved producer.

Precise universal obligation now under attack: prove one source-independent
one-step transport lemma from an arbitrary forced-owner outsider-Nash row
`q`, tail `v>=P`, and balance `B(q)<=-gamma`.  Its conclusion must be either
(i) a positive exact Nash--Bellman predecessor above `P`, or (ii) a new forced
owner and boxed tail together with a forward signed-account increment bounded
below by a fixed function of `gamma` and the reward bound.  State equality is
not required, but every step must retain exact endpoint Nash for all players,
both floor inequalities, and literal absorption charge.  A static root-face
or certificate-language separation does not change this obligation.

Kill criterion: abandon this route if an exact finite reward table shows that
the forced-refusal transfer can complete a label cycle while, for every choice
of boxed tails above `P`, every attempted owner-Nashifying transition either
loses the floor by a scale-independent amount or has zero charge, with no
telescoping signed account surviving the cycle.  Equivalently, if the smallest
active transfer cycle defeats the one-step lemma even after arbitrary
coordinatewise tail compensation, the paid/atomic data do not support this
universal thesis and the route must pivot rather than add another static
certificate.

Current status: two exact two-player examples show that a paid row alone gives
the wrong one-step data in either temporal orientation.  I also prove an
abstract bounded-length compact exactification lemma: uniformly bounded
asymptotically admissible loops with a fixed positive first-edge charge yield
an exact positive admissible return.  This is an ordinary-mathematics reduction,
not a producer from the existing frontier data and not checked in Lean.
Section 7 records a failed actual-data implication: the full-reset mover's
vanishing debt cannot exclude it as the dispatched outsider because the
outsider deviation is evaluated only after the observer has also been
replaced.  Section 8 audits directed debt transport: its exact telescope is
useful only after one supplies an exact return of a scalar debt coordinate,
and the paid-row carrier supplies no such return hypothesis.  Section 9 gives
a sharper compact target than a full finite return: positive exact admissible
edges whose current and tail *payoff annotations* asymptotically close already
produce a positive exact self-loop.  Section 10 shows why literal
compactification still fails: the natural sure-Quit root at the
first-disagreement date has charge one but a uniformly positive endpoint-Nash
residual in both temporal orientations.  Section 11 quantifies the required
Nashifying displacement and identifies a sufficient missing datum: a
continuous uniformly absorbing exact-Nash selection on a forward-invariant
compact convex payoff region.  Section 12 shows that a set-valued Kakutani
replacement cannot use Nash roots or Nash payoffs directly because their
fibers are already nonconvex in a two-player quitting root game.  It isolates
a weaker viable convex carrier with one designated positive quit coordinate.
Section 13 gives a minimal exact obstruction to deriving that carrier from a
paid seed: in the earlier orientation one outsider best-response correction
can reverse the observer's Quit incentive, while in the later orientation the
carrier fails already at the seed.  Section 14 audits the actual
source/full-replacement construction.  Its named fields retain one selected
mover reset, convergence, normalized debt directions, and the mover's small
debt at that one-reset endpoint.  They do not retain debt or best-response
stability after the observer or outsider is also replaced, and support-rank
descent is available only on the minimum-debt fiber, whereas the paid arm is
the off-minimum terminal exit.  Thus neither the needed two-reset stability nor
an acyclic anchor-transfer rank is supplied by the actual adapter.
Section 15 revisits the section 13 four-cycle nonperturbatively.  Simultaneous
mixing has a unique interior product-Nash root with positive absorption charge
`2/3`.  This is a genuine Nashification mechanism that sequential corrections
miss.  More strongly, these roots form a continuous invariant selection on
tails `(z,0)`, `2/3<=z<=1`, with successor map `T(z)=2/(3-z)`.  Its boundary
fixed point `(1,0)` is an exact positive floor-admissible self-loop of charge
`1/2`.  Thus the abstract paid-like regression contains the desired mechanism;
the remaining gap is producing its four face signs, invariant payoff interval,
spectator safety, and floors from the actual paid source.
Section 16 recovers one previously missing sign from the actual source.  Along
a diagonal vanishing-endpoint-error paid subsequence, compactness gives a
forced-observer-Q limit row with nonnegative atomic blocker balance and
outsider defect at least the terminal gap.  In contrast, the checked theorem
`quittingAtomicBlockerBalance_le_neg_of_terminalExploitabilityGap` forces
balance at most minus the gap at every outsider-Nash completion of that same
forced-Q face.  Hence actual paid data already supplies an exact separated
forced-face transition.  The unresolved topological input is the opposite
owner-Continue face (or an equivalent degree condition), followed by payoff
closure and floors.
Section 17 completely closes the root-production step when there is exactly
one outsider.  Binary affinity gives a dichotomy: the dispatched action is
still optimal on the owner-Continue face, yielding a pure transferred-anchor
Nash root, or its sign reverses, yielding an interior mixed Nash root.  With
the punishment-value vector as tail this is a positive exact
floor-admissible edge.  It does not supply the finite return required by the
existing consumer, and the conjecture-facing case has a higher-dimensional
outsider cube.
Section 18 gives the smallest exact obstruction with two outsiders.  A
three-player quitting table with punishment vector `(1,1,1)` realizes the
linear gap field
`(y-2z,z-2x,x-2y)`.  It has the same paid-limit/forced-completion sign
separation as Section 16, but its only product Nash root over that tail is
all-Continue.  Thus forced-face separation and exact punishment semantics do
not imply a charged root once the outsider cube has dimension two.  The table
is not an actual terminal-gap counterexample (three-player existence is
checked); it is an exact no-go for the extracted static adapter alone.
Section 19 restores the full conjecture-facing quantifiers.  At the behavioral
punishment floor `P`, an ordinary mixed Nash row either has positive charge and
therefore gives a positive exact floor-admissible edge, or it is all-Continue.
In the latter case the terminal gap selects a player with singleton payoff at
least `gamma`, endpoint Nash makes that singleton payoff exactly punishment
tight, and the checked owner-solo dichotomy forces a joining obstruction at
every positive solo rate.  Thus the universal residual obligation is not an
unoriented outsider cube: it is specifically the checked multi-owner support-
enlargement seam.  The Section 18 table confirms the distinction.  Although
it has no positive Nash root at `P`, raising the tail to its singleton payoff
vector produces an exact positive stationary self-loop; hence it does not
survive the universal-joining branch and does not trigger the dynamic route's
kill criterion.
Section 20 sharpens the zero-charge branch without another model assumption.
For a punishment-tight owner `o`, a positive solo hazard at tail `P` is an
exact endpoint Nash row unless some outsider's floor-adjusted insertion gain
is positive.  If every positive solo rate fails, finiteness and the limit
`p downarrow 0` force a distinct punishment-tight outsider `j` with strictly
positive singleton-collision increment
`r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j`.  Therefore absence of any positive floor edge makes
this strict collision relation serial on the nonempty tight set, and hence
produces a periodic tight collision cycle.  This is a universal finite
support reduction.  It does not yet turn the label cycle into a Bellman-state
return; that chronology/account conversion is now the only live thesis step.
Section 21 computes the missing chronological orientation exactly.  At a solo
phase of owner `o`, a punishment-tight inactive player `j` requires future
tail excess at least `p/(1-p)` times its collision increment.  Therefore a
positive collision successor cannot be the immediately following owner in a
singleton chronology: that next owner's active equality pins the needed tail
coordinate back to `P_j`.  Reverse adjacency is possible only when the joint
collision payoff does not exceed `P_j`; a strong collision instead forces
current excess at least `p(r({o,j})_j-P_j)`.  Hence the cycle margin is not an
additive chronological credit by itself.  The universal account must carry
labelled tail excess and show that strong-collision funding cannot be erased
around the full gap/preemption geometry.
Section 22 gives the exact quantitative dispatch on every selected tight-cycle
edge.  Its collision increment splits as the sum of a solo-preemption gap and
a strong-collision excess above the successor's punishment floor.  Therefore
an edge of margin at least `kappa` carries either a `kappa/2` preemption edge
or a `kappa/2` strong current-excess forcing inequality.  The cycle is thus a
finite colored preemption/forcing object, not an unpriced collision graph.
The remaining producer must telescope the forcing edges across the intervening
preemption runs; the existing full-gap preemption cycle supplies localization,
not that telescope.

Next concrete question: on the tight collision cycle, classify each selected
edge as weak (`r({o,j})_j<=P_j`) or strong.  Can reverse weak edges be assembled
into an exact floor-admissible singleton segment, while every strong edge pays
an unavoidable labelled tail-excess increment that survives until `j` becomes
active?  The signed account must also include the full-gap preemption cycle;
otherwise Section 18's tail compensation remains a falsifier.  If the excess
can always be erased before activation without an invariant loss, apply the
kill criterion.  The separate atom chronology and radial routes are exhausted
at their source interfaces and are cross-linked in their owned notes.

## Exact question

Fix a finite player type `I`, a quitting reward table on every nonempty quitting
coalition, and data occurring in the paid arm of finite support-rank exit:

- a terminal-exploitability witness and its extracted stopping-law frontier;
- an active mover and a full-reset endpoint cluster whose total semantic debt
  is strictly above the frontier minimum;
- a distinct observer and a fixed `g > 0`; and
- along all sufficiently large ranks, a paid first-disagreement row for the
  observer on the full-reset behavioral profile, with receiving pure-time
  payoff minus source pure-time payoff at least `g`.

Find one constant `c>0` such that for every `eta>0` the actual source data
produce a finite exact punishment-floor path whose endpoint payoff vectors
are coordinatewise `eta`-close and which contains one literal edge of
absorption charge at least `c`.  The path, its endpoints, its roots, its
length, and the charged edge may all depend on `eta`; only `c` is fixed.  This
is the current `QuittingPositiveAdmissiblePayoffNearReturnFamily` target.

A fixed exact return to one charged edge's own tail remains a sufficient
stronger special case, not the maintained requirement.  Exactness and the
punishment floor are not weakened to approximate edges, limit-only returns,
or bounded-controller strategies.

## Why it could matter

This is exactly the open producer arrow `PAID-FIRST-DISAGREEMENT-ROW` to
`POSITIVE-ADMISSIBLE-PAYOFF-NEAR-RETURN` in `docs/FRONTIER.md`.  The checked
theorem
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_admissiblePath_payoffNearReturns`
consumes this varying exact family and yields a uniform-equilibrium payoff.
Its existence does not itself produce the paths from a paid row.

## Work

### 1. Exact interface mismatch

A paid first-disagreement row stores two deterministic quit times for one
observer against one receiving behavioral profile.  Its exact identity is

`payoff(receiving witness) - payoff(source witness) = liveMass * reachedGain`,

and it supplies the lower bounds `g <= liveMass * reachedGain` and
`g <= 2 M liveMass`, where `M` is the uniform reward bound.  Thus `g > 0`
forces positive opponent survival to the disagreement date and positive reached
gain.  It says nothing directly about simultaneous optimality of the receiving
root for every player.

By contrast, an admissible edge with predecessor `current` and tail `tail`
requires both:

1. the exact Bellman equality
   `current.value = RootSuccessorPayoff(tail.value, current.root)`; and
2. exact root Nash against the endpoint `tail.value` for every player.

It also requires both payoff states to dominate the behavioral punishment
floor.  A return is a finite directed path of further exact edges from
`current` back to `tail`.  Therefore the positive payoff difference in a paid
row is not itself an admissible edge charge: the latter is the probability that
someone quits at one exact Nash root.

### 2. Elementary consequences retained from the paid identity

Let `L` be the row live mass, `R` its reached gain, and `M` the reward bound.
For a paid row with `g > 0`, its checked inequalities imply

- `L > 0`;
- `R > 0`; and
- if `M > 0`, `L >= g/(2M)`.

Indeed `L` is a probability and hence nonnegative.  From `g <= L R` and
`g > 0`, neither factor can vanish and `R` cannot be nonpositive.  The last
bound is the division of `g <= 2ML` by the positive number `2M`.

These facts guarantee that the strategic difference is reached with a fixed
positive mass.  They still do not imply that the root at that reached history
is Nash for the other players, nor any directed return.

### 3. Two exact orientation examples

Both examples have players `A` and `B`, observer `A`, no randomization, and
fixed gain `g = 1`.  Unlisted coordinates and rewards are zero.  They concern
the row interface alone; neither is claimed to be a positive-minimum frontier.

#### Later-receiving: paid gain with zero reached-root charge

Set A's terminal rewards to

- `r_A({A}) = -1`,
- `r_A({B}) = 0`, and
- `r_A({A,B}) = 0`.

Let B Continue forever.  Compare A's source witness `Quit at 0` with the
receiving witness `Never Quit`.  At the first disagreement, the opponent live
mass is `L = 1`.  Quitting now pays A minus one, while nobody ever quitting has
the terminal convention payoff zero.  Thus the paid identity is exactly
`1 = 1 * (0 - (-1))`, with
`receivingEarlier = false`.

But every root of the receiving profile is the all-Continue root, so its
one-stage absorption charge is exactly zero at every date.  Hence even a
maximal paid edge and full live mass do not lower-bound either current or
future absorption along the strategically preferred receiving trajectory.  A
paid later witness may simply avoid a bad Quit rather than reach a good later
terminal event.

#### Earlier-receiving: unit charge with a unit outsider defect

Set

- `r({A}) = (1,0)`,
- `r({B}) = (0,0)`, and
- `r({A,B}) = (1,1)`.

Let B Continue forever.  Compare A's source witness `Never Quit` with the
receiving witness `Quit at 0`.  The source payoff to A is zero and the
receiving payoff is one, so again `g = L = R = 1`, now with
`receivingEarlier = true`.  The receiving root has absorption charge one.

Nevertheless it is not root Nash: B obtains zero by Continue and one by also
Quitting at date 0.  Its outsider one-coordinate defect is exactly one.  Thus
the orientation which automatically supplies positive charge can fail exact
Nash by the same fixed scale.

Together the examples isolate the one-step dilemma:

- later receiving can select the strategically correct action for the
  observer while supplying no current absorption; and
- earlier receiving supplies certain current absorption while another player
  can have a fixed endpoint deviation.

The integrated temporal-dispatch theorem strengthens the second statement
under a positive terminal gap: after choosing the receiving pure-time witness
with sufficiently small endpoint error, some outsider has a reached endpoint
deviation of weighted size at least
`theta = gamma * g / (2M) > 0`.  Since `0 <= L <= 1`, the unweighted root gain
is also at least `theta`.  Because A Quits surely, the all-Continue probability
is zero before and after the outsider changes action, so this gain is
independent of the continuation payoff vector.  Consequently this literal
receiving root cannot converge to an exact endpoint-Nash root while `theta`
stays fixed.  A producer must detour to a different root rather than merely
change the tail value.

### 4. Bounded-length compact exactification

Here is a self-contained topological reduction which relaxes the exact return
at every finite rank.

**Lemma (bounded approximate loop exactification).**  Let `X` be a compact
metric space, let `R` be a closed subset of `X × X`, and let
`q : X × X → ℝ` be continuous.  Fix `delta > 0` and a natural number `K`.
Suppose there are errors `epsilon_n -> 0`, primary pairs `(a_n,b_n)`, and
return strings

`b_n = z_(n,0), z_(n,1), ..., z_(n,l_n)` with `l_n <= K`,

such that

1. the distance from `(a_n,b_n)` to `R` is at most `epsilon_n`;
2. `q(a_n,b_n) >= delta`;
3. the distance from every return pair
   `(z_(n,j),z_(n,j+1))` to `R` is at most `epsilon_n`; and
4. `dist(z_(n,l_n),a_n) <= epsilon_n`.

Then `R` contains a closed directed walk of length at most `K + 1` whose
first edge has charge at least `delta`.

**Proof.**  There are only `K + 1` possible values of `l_n`, so pass to a
subsequence on which `l_n = l` is constant.  Compactness of the finite product
`X^(l+2)` gives a further subsequence on which `a_n`, `b_n`, and every
`z_(n,j)` converge, say to `a`, `b`, and `z_j`.  We have `z_0 = b` from the
exact initial identity and `z_l = a` from condition 4.  If points `u_n`
converge to `u` and their distances to a closed set `R` tend to zero, then
`u` belongs to `R`; applying this to the primary and every return pair gives

`a R b R z_1 R ... R z_l = a`.

Continuity of `q` gives `q(a,b) >= delta > 0`.  This is the required exact
positive closed walk.  (For `l = 0`, the endpoint condition gives `a = b` and
the primary edge itself is a positive self-loop.)

#### Quitting-game specialization

Take `X` to be the full punishment-floor admissible state space.  It is a
closed subset of the canonical compact Nash--Bellman box: the additional
conditions are finitely many closed coordinate inequalities.  Take `R` to be
the exact Nash--Bellman predecessor graph.  Closedness of the boxed graph is
the checked theorem `isClosed_quittingNashBellmanEdgeGraph`; restricting it to
the floor subset remains closed.  The absorption charge is continuous in the
root simplex coordinate.

Therefore a paid-row producer need not construct a literal exact return at
each rank.  It is sufficient to construct, with one uniform length bound:

- a primary edge whose Bellman and Nash defects tend to zero and whose
  absorption charge is bounded below by one fixed `delta > 0`;
- a return string whose per-edge Bellman and Nash defects tend to zero; and
- a closing state error tending to zero.

Passing to the compact limit yields a `QuittingPositiveAdmissibleReturn`, and
the existing checked consumer then produces a uniform-equilibrium payoff.
The outstanding game-facing work is precisely the bounded approximate detour
from the paid source data.

More explicitly, write an admissible state as `(v,p)`, where `v` is its payoff
coordinate and `p` its stored simplex root, and write `root(p)` for the product
Bool root.  For errors `epsilon_n -> 0`, it is enough that every proposed edge
from tail `t_n` to current `c_n` satisfy

- `||c_n.v - quittingRootSuccessorPayoff(reward,t_n.v,root(c_n.p))||_infty
  <= epsilon_n`;
- `IsεQuittingRootEndpointNash(reward,t_n.v,epsilon_n,root(c_n.p))`;
- both states lie in the canonical box and dominate the punishment floor; and
- the primary edge has absorption mass at least a common `delta > 0`.

Impose the same first two conditions on each edge of a return string of length
at most `K`, and require its last state to approach the primary tail.  On a
convergent subsequence, continuity turns the Bellman residual into equality and
the endpoint inequalities into their zero-error versions.  This is precisely
an exact edge in the relation used by
`quittingGame_exists_uniformPayoff_of_positive_admissible_return`.

This formulation matters for the paid frontier because its selected pure-time
witnesses are approximate at every finite rank.  Exactifying only after taking
a compact limit is legitimate provided literal return length does not escape.

### 5. Why the length bound is load-bearing

Compactness and approximate recurrence without a length bound do not give a
finite exact return.  Let `X` be the unit circle and let `T` be rotation by an
irrational angle.  The closed relation `R = {(x,T(x))}` has charge identically
one.  Every orbit returns arbitrarily close to its starting point, but no
nonempty finite orbit segment closes exactly.  Thus there is no directed
cycle, even though there are positive exact first edges and arbitrarily close
returns.  The return times necessarily escape to infinity.

For completeness, approximate recurrence here is elementary: among
`0, alpha, ..., N alpha` modulo one, two points fall within `1/N` along a
pigeonhole partition, so some nonzero multiple `k alpha` is within `1/N` of
zero.  Irrationality prevents any nonzero multiple from being exactly zero
modulo one.  Hence increasingly accurate return segments exist, their lengths
cannot remain bounded, and none closes.

This example blocks any argument that invokes compactness plus recurrence
without controlling literal return length or adding a separate closing
mechanism.

### 6. Quantitative bounded-loop alternative under a terminal witness

The compact argument has a useful dual form.  Retain a compact metric state
space `X`, closed relation `R`, and continuous first-edge charge `q`.  Fix
`delta > 0` and `K`.  Assume there is no exact `R`-cycle of length at most
`K + 1` whose first-edge charge is at least `delta`.  Then there is a number
`rho = rho(delta,K) > 0` such that every proposed closed loop of length at most
`K + 1` with first-edge charge at least `delta` has either

- one edge at distance at least `rho` from `R`, or
- closing error at least `rho`.

To prove this, fix each return length `l <= K` and consider all tuples
`(a,b,z_1,...,z_l)` with `q(a,b) >= delta`.  This is compact.  On it take the
maximum of the distances of the primary and return pairs from `R` and the
closing distance `dist(z_l,a)` (with the evident `l = 0` convention).  The
maximum is continuous and never zero, since a zero would be exactly a
forbidden positive closed walk.  It therefore has a strictly positive minimum.
Take the minimum over the finitely many `l <= K`; empty tuple classes are
vacuous.

For the quitting relation, a terminal-exploitability witness rules out every
positive exact admissible cycle by the checked theorem
`QuittingTerminalExploitabilityWitness.admissible_cycle_chargeSum_eq_zero`.
Consequently, for every fixed positive charge floor and return-length bound,
the witness imposes one uniform residual floor `rho > 0` on all approximate
loop attempts in the punishment-floor box.

This recasts the desired paid-row producer as a sharp contradiction target:
construct one fixed `delta > 0`, one fixed `K`, and paid-row loop attempts for
which every Bellman defect, endpoint-Nash defect, and closing error tends to
zero.  Compactness then contradicts the witness.  Conversely, if a proposed
construction has return lengths escaping or retains one residual of fixed
size, it has not crossed the producer boundary.

### 7. Failed third-player exclusion and the exact missing stability bound

Let `m` be the frontier mover and `o != m` the paid observer.  The full-reset
profile `p_n` has

`debt_m(p_n) <= lambda_n^2 -> 0`.

It is tempting to combine this with the earlier-receiving dispatch, whose
profitable player `w != o` gains a fixed
`theta = gamma * g / (2M) > 0`, and conclude `w != m`.  That implication is
invalid.  The dispatch evaluates the outsider at

`p'_n = update(p_n, o, receivingPureTimeWitness_n)`,

not at `p_n`.  Updating the observer can change the mover's complete terminal
best-response envelope and prescribed payoff.  The checked small bound on
`debt_m(p_n)` gives no bound on `debt_m(p'_n)`.

The strongest valid conclusion remains only `w != o`; the dispatched outsider
may be the original reset mover.  To prove a genuine third-player statement it
would suffice to establish the two-reset stability estimate

`debt_m(p'_n) <= debt_m(p_n) + o(1)`,

or merely `debt_m(p'_n) < theta` eventually.  Nothing inspected supplies this.
The source approximation concerns the observer's pure-time witness at the
original source profile, while the receiving approximation concerns it at
`p_n`; neither controls the mover after the observer replacement.

This is not a cosmetic mismatch.  Terminal semantic state is known to require
deleted-clock information for splicing, and changing one opponent's complete
quit-time plan can change another player's envelope by a fixed amount.  Any
future third-player or finite-label iteration must first prove a quantitative
two-reset envelope-stability lemma from actual source data.

An exact two-player regression shows that no such lemma follows from the row
and the two players' local optimality alone.  Let the players be mover `m` and
observer `o`, start from the all-`Never` profile `p`, and set mover rewards

- `r_m({m}) = 0`,
- `r_m({o}) = -1`, and
- `r_m({m,o}) = 1`.

Give the observer singleton reward `r_o({o})=1` (other observer rewards may be
zero).  At `p`, the mover has debt zero: staying at `Never` pays zero and
Quitting alone also pays zero.  The observer's exact best response is to Quit
at date zero, producing the earlier-receiving paid row of gain one.  After this
update, the mover is prescribed payoff `-1` but can Quit simultaneously for
payoff `1`, so its debt is at least two.  Thus an exact best-response update by
the observer can raise the previously optimal mover's debt from zero to two.

This regression is not itself a stopping-law frontier and therefore does not
rule out stability from stronger source geometry.  It does rule out deriving
the needed estimate from vanishing mover debt plus receiving-witness
optimality and paid-row data alone.

## 8. Directed transport is a consumer of a debt return

The literal all-Continue spine has an exact scalar account for each player,

`d_t = c_t + s_t d_(t+1)`,

where `c_t >= 0` is the coordinate cap--Nash defect and `0 <= s_t <= 1` is the
joint Continue mass.  If one additionally knows the exact return
`d_T = d_0`, then elementary telescoping gives

`d_0 (1 - product_(t<T) s_t)
  = sum_(t<T) (product_(u<t) s_u) c_t`.

This is exactly the checked content of
`QuittingTerminalSemanticPrefixChain.debt_mul_one_sub_reachedWeight_eq_sum_reached_defect_of_return`.
Its literal corollary
`exists_actualRowGain_pos_or_capDefect_le_quitOptionBudget_of_return` then
extracts either a reached actual best-endpoint gain or a positively charged cap
defect covered by the explicit continuation-option budget.

This does not close the paid-row producer.  The exact return `d_T = d_0` is a
hypothesis of every relevant return theorem, not a consequence of the prefix
chain.  A `QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow` records two deterministic quit
times, their chronology, live mass, reached payoff difference, and the paid
identity.  It records no equality between the terminal-semantic debt at the
initial profile and the debt of a later all-Continue spine.  Chronological
agreement before `start` also does not imply such equality: the debt envelope
depends on the deleted-clock continuation, not just on the prescribed
pure-time value used in the row.

Therefore directed transport sharpens the form of a possible bridge but is
not an actual-data adapter from the current paid arm.  A usable bridge would
have to prove a return (exact or a quantitatively closable approximation) from
the source/full-reset geometry independently of the paid-row carrier.

## 9. Payoff-closing positive edges exactify to a self-loop

There is a useful weakening of the requested exact finite state return.  The
simplex coordinate stored at the *tail* of a Nash--Bellman edge is operationally
irrelevant to that edge: only the tail payoff annotation and the current
root/simplex enter its Bellman and Nash equations.

**Proposition (ordinary mathematics, not checked in Lean).**  Fix `c>0`.
Suppose there is a sequence `e_n` of exact punishment-floor admissible edges
such that

- every absorption charge is at least `c`; and
- the distance between the current and tail payoff annotations tends to zero.

Then there is a positive punishment-floor admissible self-loop, hence a
`QuittingPositiveAdmissibleReturn` with a nil return path.

**Proof.**  The boxed payoff/simplex state space is compact, and the floor
condition is closed.  Pass to a subsequence on which both endpoint states of
`e_n` converge.  The closing hypothesis says that the two limiting payoff
annotations are one common vector `v`, although their stored simplex
coordinates may differ.  Closedness of the exact Nash--Bellman edge graph
gives a limiting exact edge whose current root is some `q`, whose current
payoff is `v`, and whose tail payoff is also `v`.  Form the single admissible
state `s=(v,q)`.  Replacing the limiting edge's unused tail simplex by `q`
does not change either its Bellman equation or its endpoint-Nash condition,
so it is an exact edge from `s` to `s`.  Continuity of absorption charge gives
charge at least `c>0`.  The nil path returns `s` to itself.  QED.

Equivalently, under a terminal exploitability witness there is a uniform
separation at every positive charge scale: for each `c>0` there is
`delta(c)>0` such that every exact floor-admissible edge of charge at least
`c` has current/tail payoff distance at least `delta(c)`.  Otherwise choose
edges of distance below `1/n` and apply the proposition; the resulting
positive self-loop is consumed by
`quittingGame_exists_uniformPayoff_of_positive_admissible_return`, contrary to
the witness.  This is a compact qualitative separation, not the linear
absorption-clock ballisticity already proved for the optimized dynamic tail.

The one-edge variation estimate points in the complementary direction:
every exact bounded Bellman edge has payoff displacement at most
`2 M * charge`.  Hence a payoff-return detour whose total charge tends to zero
would force the primary endpoint annotations to close, even if its final
stored simplex did not equal the primary tail simplex.  This makes
"same payoff, arbitrary simplex" a sufficient return target.  It does not
remove the main adapter gap: the paid row is not exact endpoint Nash in the
earlier orientation, and its absorption charge can vanish in the later
orientation.

## 10. The natural charged row is uniformly separated from exact Nash

The reached first-disagreement root itself is maximally absorbing: after
replacing the observer by whichever witness Quits at `start`, its observer
coordinate is pure Quit, so its joint absorption charge is one.  Nevertheless
the temporal dispatch gives a fixed root-Nash obstruction, not an approximate
edge.

Every paid row with `g>0` has `0<L<=1`, where `L` is its live mass, and reached
gain `R` satisfying

`R >= g/L >= g`.

The first inequality is the paid bound and the second uses `L<=1`.

In the **later-receiving** orientation, let `q` be the reached root after the
observer is set to the earlier source witness.  The observer's prescribed
Quit endpoint is the fixed-opponents Quit value `Q`, while following the later
receiving plan has conditional value `W`, with `W-Q=R`.  Decompose that later
plan after its first Continue action and use its shifted continuation payoff
as the observer coordinate of a Bellman tail.  Then pure Continue at `q` pays
`W`, the prescribed pure Quit pays `Q`, and the endpoint regret is `R>=g`.
Thus the natural charge-one root is not even an `epsilon` endpoint Nash root
for `epsilon<g` at its literal deleted-clock tail.

In the **earlier-receiving terminal-gap** orientation, put

`theta = gamma*g/(2M)>0`.

The checked temporal dispatch supplies an outsider and pure endpoint action
whose reached gain `D` obeys `theta <= L*D`, hence `D>=theta`.  Because the
observer Quits surely at this root, both the prescribed row and the outsider's
unilateral endpoint deviation absorb immediately.  Their payoff difference is
therefore independent of the Bellman tail annotation.  Consequently this
same charge-one root fails `epsilon` endpoint Nash for **every** tail whenever
`epsilon<theta`.

These estimates show that taking a subsequential limit of the literal reached
roots cannot yield the exact positive edge required in section 9.  In the
earlier branch, continuity and compactness even place the dispatched roots a
positive distance from the exact endpoint-Nash graph over the whole payoff
box.  In the later branch an exactification must change either the root or the
deleted-clock tail by a nonvanishing amount.  Any viable producer therefore
needs a genuine Nash-selection/correction step and a new argument showing
that this correction preserves positive charge and payoff closure.

## 11. Quantitative no-small-correction theorem

Write a product root by its quit-probability vector and put

`d_1(p,q) = sum_i |p_i-q_i|`.

For any payoff annotation in the reward box, coupling independent Bernoulli
actions gives the elementary bound

`|E_p[f]-E_q[f]| <= 2 M d_1(p,q)`

for every one-stage outcome function bounded in `[-M,M]`.  Applying it once
to the prescribed root and once to a pure unilateral endpoint gives a
`4 M d_1` modulus for the corresponding endpoint regret.

### Earlier-receiving branch

Let `q_n` be any dispatched reached root and let `w_n,a_n` be its outsider
and pure action.  Section 10 gives a raw endpoint gain at least `theta`, and
the sure-Quit observer makes this gain independent of every tail annotation
in the payoff box.  If `p` is an exact endpoint-Nash root at any such tail,
its gain from the same pure action is nonpositive.  Therefore

`theta <= 4 M d_1(p,q_n)`,

so

`d_1(p,q_n) >= theta/(4M) > 0`.

Thus no sequence of exact Nash corrections can converge back to the literal
paid roots, regardless of how its tail annotations are chosen.  This is an
exact no-small-correction theorem, not just the statement that the current
adapter omits a proof.

### Later-receiving branch

Let `x_n` be the deleted-clock tail payoff obtained by shifting the later
receiving plan past the reached row.  At `(q_n,x_n)` pure Continue beats the
observer's prescribed Quit by at least `g`.  If `(p,v)` is exact endpoint Nash,
the same coupling estimate, plus changing the all-Continue tail outcome,
gives the conservative inequality

`g <= 4 M d_1(p,q_n) + 2 ||v-x_n||_infinity`.

Hence an exactification cannot simultaneously keep the root and the literal
deleted-clock tail close.  In both orientations the paid residual must be
removed by an order-one correction.

### What additional source datum would overcome this

A precise sufficient correction packet is now visible.  Let `K` be a nonempty
compact convex subset of the floor-admissible payoff box.  Suppose actual
source data supplied a continuous choice `N(v)` of an exact endpoint-Nash
product root for every `v in K` such that

1. `quittingRootAbsorptionMass(N(v)) >= c` for one fixed `c>0`; and
2. `quittingRootSuccessorPayoff(reward,v,N(v))` belongs to `K`.

Then the continuous successor map `T(v)=Successor(v,N(v))` maps `K` to itself.
Brouwer gives `T(v*)=v*`.  The state `(v*,N(v*))` is a positive exact
floor-admissible self-loop, and the existing positive-return consumer applies
with a nil return path.

The paid row supplies neither this continuous exact-Nash selection nor an
invariant payoff region.  Generic finite-game Nash correspondences need not
have continuous single-valued selections, and the all-Continue zero-charge
branch can coexist with the paid temporal data.  The quantitative bounds above
also show that such a selection cannot be obtained by a perturbative
Nashification of the literal reached roots.  A successful actual-data producer
must instead construct a nonlocal absorbing Nash branch (or a finite exact
return after such a jump) and then prove payoff closure.

## 12. Kakutani audit: nonconvex Nash fibers and a viable carrier

Continuity of a single-valued exact-Nash selection in section 11 is stronger
than logically necessary, but simply replacing it by the Nash correspondence
does not work.

### Smallest exact nonconvexity example

Take two players `A,B`, continuation tail `(0,0)`, and terminal rows

- `r({A})=(2,1)`,
- `r({B})=(1,2)`, and
- `r({A,B})=(0,0)`.

The one-root quitting game has exactly three product Nash equilibria:

- `(A Quit, B Continue)`, with successor payoff `(2,1)`;
- `(A Continue, B Quit)`, with successor payoff `(1,2)`; and
- the fully mixed root in which both quit with probability `2/3`, with
  successor payoff `(2/3,2/3)`.

Indeed, if `B` quits with probability `y`, `A`'s Quit and Continue endpoints
are `2(1-y)` and `y`, so interior indifference gives `y=2/3`; the other
coordinate is symmetric.  The pure best-response checks are immediate.

Thus both the Nash-root fiber and its successor-payoff image are nonconvex.
For example, the midpoint `(3/2,3/2)` of the two pure Nash payoffs is not a
Nash payoff.  All three roots have positive absorption, so imposing positivity
does not repair this example.  Taking a convex hull would produce a payoff
with no product root witnessing it; a public correlated lottery is not part of
the existing behavioral root interface.

The raw positive-charge region is itself nonconvex.  For two quit
probabilities, the roots `(0,1/2)` and `(1/2,0)` both have absorption charge
`1/2`, while their midpoint `(1/4,1/4)` has charge `7/16<1/2`.  Therefore the
constraint `charge>=c` cannot simply be intersected with the ordinary convex
best-response values in Kakutani.

### Why unconstrained best-response Kakutani is insufficient

On the product of the payoff box and root simplex, the correspondence

`(v,p) |-> {Successor(v,p)} x BR(v,p)`

does have nonempty compact convex values and a closed graph.  Kakutani gives a
fixed point `v=Successor(v,p)`, `p in BR(v,p)`: an exact Nash--Bellman
self-loop.  But it may choose the all-Continue root.  Such a zero-charge
phantom fixes every annotation satisfying the singleton endpoint inequalities
and need not equal the terminal payoff of executable all-Continue play.  This
is precisely why the argument does not prove stationary terminal equilibrium
for every quitting game.

### A weaker sufficient convex carrier

A continuous single-valued selection can be replaced by the following packet.
Choose a player `o`, a constant `c>0`, a compact convex payoff region `K` in
the floor box, and a compact convex root carrier `R` contained in

`{p : p_o >= c}`.

Assume:

1. `Successor(v,p) in K` for every `(v,p) in K x R`;
2. `BR(v,p) intersect R` is nonempty for every `(v,p) in K x R`; and
3. this intersection correspondence has closed graph (hence is upper
   hemicontinuous here).

Its values are compact and convex, because ordinary binary mixed
best-response values and `R` are convex.  The correspondence

`(v,p) |-> {Successor(v,p)} x (BR(v,p) intersect R)`

is a Kakutani self-map of `K x R`.  At a fixed point, `p` is an unrestricted
product Nash root, `v` is its successor fixed point, and its absorption charge
is at least `p_o>=c`.  Hence it is a positive exact admissible self-loop and is
consumed by the existing positive-return theorem.

This carrier packet is strictly weaker than a continuous exact-Nash selection:
it permits a genuinely set-valued best-response fiber.  It also makes the
missing paid-row inputs explicit.

- The natural charged row gives only one seed with `p_o=1`; it does not give a
  convex root carrier invariant under simultaneous best responses.
- In the later-receiving orientation, Quit is strictly suboptimal for `o` at
  the literal deleted-clock tail, so condition 2 already fails at the seed if
  `R` forces positive `o`-Quit mass.
- In the earlier-receiving orientation, the observer can be made nearly
  optimal as its endpoint error tends to zero, but the dispatched outsider
  has a fixed defect.  Correcting outsiders can change the observer's envelope
  by order one, so the current two-reset data do not preserve condition 2.
- No forward-invariant convex payoff region `K` is supplied by a paid row or
  by the off-minimum endpoint cluster.

Consequently upper hemicontinuity is not the missing fact.  The hard datum is
a positive-charge **convex carrier** on which best responses remain nonempty
and successor payoffs remain invariant.  The paid row currently supplies only
a maximally charged, uniformly non-Nash point outside such a verified carrier.

## 13. Minimal obstruction to a designated-quitter carrier

The later-receiving orientation already defeats a carrier forcing positive
observer Quit mass: at the literal deleted-clock tail, Continue beats the
observer's sure Quit by at least `g`.  Thus its best-response set has no point
in `{p_o>=c}` for any `c>0` when the inequality is strict.

The earlier orientation is subtler because the receiving observer can be made
arbitrarily close to optimal.  Nevertheless observer optimality need not
survive even one outsider correction.  The following exact two-player root
game is a minimal regression.  Let `o` be the observer, `w` the outsider, use
tail `(0,0)`, and set

- `r({o})=(1,0)`,
- `r({w})=(2,-1)`, and
- `r({o,w})=(0,1)`.

At the seed `(o Quit,w Continue)`, the observer strictly prefers Quit
(`1>0`), while `w` gains one by switching to Quit.  After this unique outsider
correction, at `(o Quit,w Quit)`, the observer strictly prefers Continue,
because leaving the quitting coalition gives payoff `2` instead of `0`.
The strict best-response cycle is

`CC -> QC -> QQ -> CQ -> CC`,

where the first coordinate is `o`.  In particular, for the convex carrier
`R={p:p_o>=c}`, the best-response intersection is nonempty at the paid-like
seed but becomes empty at `QQ` for every `c>0`.

This seed is a literal earlier-receiving paid row: against the all-Continue
profile, replacing `o`'s source witness `Never` by receiving witness `Quit 0`
gains one, has live mass one, and the receiving witness is an exact best
response.  The outsider deviation is also exactly the earlier temporal
dispatch.  The example is not a stopping-law frontier and, being a finite
two-player quitting game, is covered by the integrated two-player uniform
existence theorem; no exact stationary equilibrium is asserted here.  Its
role is narrower and exact: paid row, receiving
optimality, and local outsider dispatch do not imply best-response invariance
of a designated-quitter carrier.

There are two genuinely stronger source data which would overcome this
regression.

1. **Observer-invariant correction:** prove that throughout the constrained
   outsider best-response correspondence the observer's Quit endpoint remains
   optimal, together with a uniform lower bound on its quit mass.  This is a
   root-level strengthening of the missing two-reset envelope-stability bound
   from section 7.
2. **Acyclic anchor transfer:** allow the positive-quit anchor to move from the
   observer to a correcting outsider, but supply a finite rank which strictly
   decreases on every such transfer.  A terminal anchor then supports the
   convex carrier of section 12.  The displayed four-cycle shows that no such
   rank follows from arbitrary root payoff data.

The actual paid arm currently supplies neither datum.  Its outsider may equal
the original reset mover, and no inequality controls the observer after that
outsider is corrected.  Thus the restricted-correspondence route is rigorously
blocked at exactly the same two-reset seam already visible in semantic debt,
now with a minimal finite root counterexample.

## 14. Actual-source audit: the missing datum was not erased by abstraction

I inspected the named construction one level before the paid-row interface.
The conclusion is negative but narrower than an independence counterexample:
the current checked declarations expose neither two-reset stability nor a
rank/carrier which could substitute for it.  The abstract examples above do
not refute a theorem from the full frontier hypotheses; rather, this audit
identifies the exact new adapter such a theorem would have to prove.

### 14.1 What the actual frontier retains

`QuittingPositiveMinimumDebtTangentFamily`
(`PositiveMinimumDebtTangentFamily.lean`) retains:

- one selected source sequence converging to a positive global minimum of
  total terminal semantic debt;
- one common reset scale tending to zero;
- for each active mover, one selected replacement strategy and the limiting
  normalized debt-direction column; and
- `replacement_moverDebt_le_tolerance`, which bounds the mover's debt at the
  *single* literal full-replacement profile by both the squared scale and half
  its source debt.

`FullReplacementCluster` (`MinimumFiberSupportDrop.lean`) takes a cluster
subsequence of exactly those
single-reset endpoints.  It stores convergence, coordinatewise tangent
domination, and equality in the mover coordinate.  Consequently
`FullReplacementCluster.mover_debt_eq_zero` gives zero mover debt at the
limiting single-reset cluster.  Neither the structure nor this theorem
mentions the profile obtained by subsequently replacing the paid observer by
one of the row's pure-time witnesses, still less the profile obtained after a
dispatched outsider is corrected.

The curvature decoder
`exists_paidFirstDisagreementRow_of_stoppingLawNormalizedCurvature` and its
data-preserving wrapper `QuittingStoppingLawCurvaturePaidWitness`
(`NormalizedCurvaturePaidRow.lean` and
`NormalizedCurvatureStrategicDispatch.lean`) add only two scalar observer
approximation inequalities: one at the source and one at the literal
full-replacement endpoint.  The endpoint inequality is enough for
`QuittingStoppingLawCurvaturePaidWitness.nonempty_strategicDispatch` to expose
an owner deviation or a reached outsider deviation.  It gives no bound on the
reset mover's debt after the observer update and no invariant observer
best-response statement after the outsider update.  The docstring of
`exists_eventually_curvatureStrategicDispatch` accurately records the present
boundary: its conclusion remains attached to the literal source and
full-replacement profiles and does not re-enter the outsider endpoint into the
tangent family.

Thus the exact two-reset estimate missing in section 7 has the following
form.  For the finite-rank receiving profile `r_n` and the observer-updated
profile

`z_n = update r_n observer (pureTime receivingWitness_n)`,

one needs, for example,

`debt(z_n,mover) -> 0`,

or an envelope-stability inequality strong enough to imply it.  The current
field is only `debt(r_n,mover) <= scale_n^2`.  As section 7's exact example
shows, the latter does not control the former for general quitting data.

### 14.2 Why the existing finite rank stops precisely before the paid arm

The only well-founded rank in the actual construction is
`positiveDebtSupport.card`.  The theorem
`exists_reextractedFrontier_of_minimumFiberEndpoint`
(`NormalizedCurvaturePaidRow.lean`) strictly lowers it only when the selected
full-replacement cluster remains on the minimum-total-debt fiber.  The paid
row is produced in the disjoint case

`debtSum(frontier.base) < debtSum(endpoint.cluster)`.

Accordingly,
`potentialCoDecrease_minimumFiberDeflation_or_paidFirstDisagreement` branches
between support deflation on the minimum fiber and a paid row off the minimum
fiber.  `exists_finiteSupportRankExit` recurses only through the former and
terminates immediately with the paid-row tag in the latter.  No support set,
successor frontier, or ordinal label is assigned to the observer/outsider
correction.  Therefore the existing rank cannot rule out the root-level
best-response cycle of section 13: it simply is not defined on those
transitions.

A related but distinct construction,
`QuittingSourceMatchedRadialPaidSquare` and
`exists_fixed_radialStrategicLabel_of_scaleNormalizedLiminfLower`
(`SourceMatchedRadialPaidDispatch.lean`), does retain a reset-cube square and can
stabilize the finite label “owner” or the identity of an outsider along a
subsequence.  That label is not an acyclic rank: the theorem preserves a
positive normalized legal gain, but supplies no successor profile in the
frontier, no monotone label change, no Nash--Bellman edge, and no floor-carrier
invariance.  It is also not the adapter used by the off-minimum paid arm of
`PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissibleReturnConsumer`.

### 14.3 Precise adapter deficit and status of the route

Any continuation of the paid arm must add at least one genuinely new source
theorem of one of these forms:

1. **Two-reset stability:** uniformly in the eventual paid rows, the reset
   mover's debt remains small after the observer pure-time update, followed by
   a corresponding invariant for any dispatched outsider correction.
2. **Off-minimum acyclic correction:** every positive-anchor transfer enters a
   new actual frontier/carrier with a well-founded rank that strictly drops.
   The current support-cardinality rank is confined to minimum-fiber
   re-extraction.
3. **Nonperturbative carrier jump:** construct from the endpoint cluster a
   compact convex payoff set `K` and root carrier `R`, with uniformly positive
   charge, successor invariance, and nonempty convex best-response
   intersections as in section 12.  The actual fields store neither such a
   payoff region nor such a root correspondence.

Payoff closure alone would be sufficient for the section 9 consumer once
exact positively charged floor-admissible edges have been produced: the limit
is then a positive self-loop and the existing nil-path return consumer applies.
But payoff closure does not create Nashification or a positive root charge.
The actual paid source supplies neither prerequisite.

The strongest honest status is therefore: the paid-row producer is blocked at
a precise missing adapter, not disproved.  The abstract counterexamples rule
out deriving the adapter from the row/dispatch alone; the named full frontier
declarations do not currently add the missing two-reset, acyclic, or
floor-carrier datum.

## 15. Nonperturbative joint repair closes at a boundary self-loop

The sequential correction cycle of section 13 does not imply the absence of a
charged exact Nash root.  In fact, it contains one.  Write `x` for the
observer's Quit probability and `y` for the outsider's Quit probability.  At
tail `(0,0)`, the observer's Quit-minus-Continue difference is

`Delta_o(y) = (1-y) - 2y = 1 - 3y`,

and the outsider's is

`Delta_w(x) = (2x-1) - 0 = 2x - 1`.

Thus the unique fully mixed product Nash root is

`x = 1/2,   y = 1/3`.

Its successor payoff and absorption charge are exactly

`Successor((0,0),(x,y)) = (2/3,0)`,

`charge(x,y) = 1 - (1-x)(1-y) = 2/3`.

For the second coordinate, the `w`-only loss `-1/6` and the joint-Quit gain
`1/6` cancel.  For the first, the `o`-only and `w`-only outcomes each
contribute `1/3`.  Hence this is an exact positive-charge Nashification of the
paid-like seed by a genuinely joint mixed phase.  It is nonperturbative: both
coordinates move a fixed distance from `QC`.

At tail `(0,0)` this first edge does not yet close: its exact Bellman current is
`(2/3,0)`.  Reapplying the same root game at continuation tail `(2/3,0)` gives
the interior indifference equations

`1-y = 2y + (1-y)(2/3)` for the observer and `2x-1=0` for
the outsider, giving `x=1/2`, `y=1/7`; the resulting successor is `(6/7,0)`.
This movement is not a no-return obstruction.  For every continuation tail
`(z,0)` with `0<=z<=1`, choose

`x(z)=1/2,   y(z)=(1-z)/(3-z)`.

The observer's Quit payoff is `1-y`; its Continue payoff is
`2y+(1-y)z`, so these are equal.  The outsider's difference remains
`2x-1=0`.  Hence this is an exact product-Nash root.  Its successor is

`T(z)=1-y(z)=2/(3-z)`

in the observer coordinate and zero in the outsider coordinate.  In
particular `T` maps `[2/3,1]` continuously into itself, every selected root has
charge at least `1/2`, and the unique fixed point in this interval is `z=1`.
At that boundary,

`x=1/2,   y=0,   Successor((1,0),(x,y))=(1,0)`.

This is already an exact positive Nash--Bellman self-loop of charge `1/2`.
It is also punishment-floor admissible.  The outsider can guarantee zero by
Continuing forever, and the observer can guarantee at least one against every
opponent quitting-time law: if the opponent's first-Q distribution has masses
`b_t`, quitting deterministically at `t` pays

`2 sum_(s<t)b_s + (1-sum_(s<=t)b_s) = 1 + sum_(s<t)b_s - b_t`

after including `Never` in the residual.  Some `t` has
`sum_(s<t)b_s>=b_t` (otherwise the partial sums would more than double
indefinitely), so the supremum is at least one.  Conversely all-Continue
opponents cap the observer's best reply at its solo payoff one.  Thus the two
punishment values are exactly `(1,0)`.  The self-loop state meets both floors,
and the nil return path makes it a positive admissible return in this exact
table.

This table is only a paid-like regression, not data from the positive-minimum
counterexample frontier; as a two-player quitting game it already lies under
the checked two-player existence theorem.  Its role is a positive exact model
of the nonperturbative correction the actual adapter would have to construct.

The exact mechanism generalizes.  For any binary two-player root game, suppose
the four endpoint differences satisfy

`Delta_o(0)=A>0`, `Delta_o(1)=-B<0`,

`Delta_w(0)=-C<0`, `Delta_w(1)=D>0`.

Affinity in the opponent's mixing probability gives the unique fully mixed
root

`y*=A/(A+B),   x*=C/(C+D)`.

Both coordinates are interior, hence its absorption charge is positive.  With
uniform lower bounds on the four strict face margins and uniform upper bounds
on payoff differences, they are uniformly separated from the boundary.  This
is the smallest exact joint-repair rectangle which converts the sequential
four-cycle into a nonperturbative charged Nash root.

The actual temporal dispatch does not yet construct that rectangle.  In the
earlier orientation, observer optimality at the paid seed gives the sign
`Delta_o(0)>=0` up to the endpoint error, and the selected outsider deviation
gives the positive sign corresponding to `Delta_w(1)>0` after relabelling the
outsider's dispatched pure action as Quit.  It supplies neither the reverse
outsider sign `Delta_w(0)<0` nor the observer reversal
`Delta_o(1)<0`.  It also supplies no spectator best-response inequalities,
punishment floors, or equality/return for the successor payoff.  The
dispatched outsider action may originally be Continue rather than Quit, so the
statement is invariant under relabelling its two root actions.

Consequently the section 13 cycle is not itself a no-go for nonperturbative
joint Nashification.  The sharpened source deficit is a theorem producing:

1. an alternating-sign joint-repair rectangle (or a higher-dimensional
   Poincare--Miranda analogue) with uniform strict margins;
2. best-response safety for all spectators and punishment-floor admissibility;
   and
3. payoff closure, either by a fixed successor or by a finite exact return.

The existing paid-row and strategic-dispatch fields provide two oriented face
comparisons only.  They do not provide items 1--3 as a packet.

## 16. Actual forced-Q face separation

The actual positive-minimum source supplies the observer reversal after all,
but at a nonlocally Nashified outsider row rather than after the one selected
outsider correction.

Fix `gain=g>0`, terminal gap `gamma>0`, and endpoint errors `eta_n->0` along a
diagonal subsequence of the earlier-receiving paid arm.  Let `p_n` be the
reached root after replacing the observer by its earlier pure-time witness, so
the observer Quits surely.  Write

`B(p)=quittingAtomicBlockerBalance reward p observer`,

`D(p)=quittingForcedOwnerOutsiderDefect reward p observer`.

The row's division-free live-mass estimate gives

`g <= 2 M L_n`,

where `M=quittingRewardBound reward` and `L_n` is the row live mass.  Since
`g>0`, also `M>0` and therefore `L_n>=g/(2M)`.  The checked collector
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow.liveMass_mul_forcedRefusal_le_eta` gives

`L_n max(0,-B(p_n)) <= eta_n`,

so

`max(0,-B(p_n)) <= (2M/g) eta_n -> 0`.

The finite root cube is compact.  After a further subsequence, `p_n->p*`.
The observer remains surely quitting, and continuity of the finite multilinear
expressions gives

`B(p*)>=0`.

Applying the checked every-row atomic barrier at `p*` yields

`gamma <= max(D(p*), max(0,-B(p*))) = D(p*)`.

Thus `p*` is not outsider-Nash: it is a literal forced-Q limit row with
nonnegative owner balance and a fixed positive outsider defect.

Now let `q` be any mixed Nash row of the finite normal-form game in which the
observer is forced to Quit; equivalently,

`IsQuittingForcedOwnerNashRow reward observer q`.

Such a row exists by the ordinary finite-game mixed Nash theorem.  The checked
theorem
`quittingAtomicBlockerBalance_le_neg_of_terminalExploitabilityGap` gives the
opposite strict sign

`B(q) <= -gamma`.

Consequently the actual paid arm supplies a compact forced-owner face with two
separated regions:

- at the paid limit `p*`, `B>=0` and `D>=gamma`;
- on the entire zero-outsider-defect/Nash subset, `B<=-gamma`.

The separation is quantitatively nonlocal.  Identify an outsider product root
with its vector of Quit probabilities `y`.  For a pure outsider coalition
`S`, the observer's atomic balance integrand is

`b(S)=r(S union {observer})_observer
      - (if S is nonempty then r(S)_observer else punishmentValue_observer)`.

The canonical bound and
`abs_quittingPunishmentValue_le_quittingRewardBound` give `|b(S)|<=2M`.
Coordinatewise maximal coupling of two Bernoulli product laws therefore gives

`|B(y)-B(z)| <= 4M sum_(w!=observer)|y_w-z_w|`.

Indeed the coupled coalitions differ with probability at most the displayed
sum, and on that event two integrand values differ by at most `4M`.  Hence for
every forced-Q outsider-Nash completion `q`,

`sum_(w!=observer)|p*_w-q_w| >= gamma/(4M)`.

In particular, when there are `N=|I|-1>0` outsiders, some coordinate changes
by at least `gamma/(4MN)`.  This is an actual-source lower bound on the
nonperturbative anchor/carrier jump.  It supplies a scale, not an orientation
or a chronological return.

This is stronger than the wrapper-level audit in section 14 and corrects the
claim in section 15 that the observer reversal itself is absent.  What remains
absent is a degree-closing condition on the opposite face where the observer
Continues.  For two players, a strict reverse best-response sign for the
outsider on that face completes the alternating rectangle and forces an
interior joint root.  In higher dimension one needs the corresponding
boundary-degree, acyclic-carrier, or Poincare--Miranda datum for all outsiders.
Neither the paid row nor the atomic barrier controls that owner-Continue face.

Even after such a joint root is obtained, the actual-data producer must still
place its tail and successor above all punishment floors and close the payoff
state by a fixed point or finite exact return.  Section 15 shows this second
step can occur nonperturbatively, but only in the test table.

## 17. The one-outsider face closes automatically

Suppose there are exactly two players, the observer `o` and one outsider `w`,
and use the punishment-value vector

`P=(quittingPunishmentValue reward o,
    quittingPunishmentValue reward w)`

as the continuation tail.  Let `x` be `o`'s Quit probability and `y` the
probability of the outsider action selected as the better pure response on the
forced-`o`-Quit face; relabel that action as Quit.

For a diagonal paid limit `p*` from section 16, write its outsider probability
as `y_0`.  Since `D(p*)>=gamma>0`, the outsider has a strict pure best response
when `x=1`, and `p*` puts positive mass on the other action.  Thus after the
relabeling,

`Delta_w(1)>0`, `y_0<1`.

The unique forced-owner outsider-Nash row is therefore `y=1`.  Section 16
gives

`Delta_o(y_0)=B(p*)>=0`,

`Delta_o(1)<=-gamma<0`.

Here `Delta_o` is Quit minus Continue in the one-root game with tail `P`; by
definition it is exactly the atomic blocker balance.  Since it is affine in
the single outsider mixing probability and `y_0<1`, these inequalities force

`Delta_o(0)>0`.

Now inspect the remaining scalar `Delta_w(0)`.

- If `Delta_w(0)>=0`, the pure root `(x,y)=(0,1)` is Nash: the observer
  Continues because `Delta_o(1)<0`, while the outsider Quits because
  `Delta_w(0)>=0`.
- If `Delta_w(0)<0`, the four strict signs alternate.  The formula of section
  15 yields a fully mixed Nash root with both probabilities in `(0,1)`.

In either case the root has positive absorption charge.  Let `U` be its exact
successor payoff from tail `P`.  The tail lies on the punishment floor and in
the canonical reward box; convexity of the one-step successor keeps `U` in the
box.  The existing floor-propagation statement embodied by
`QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleEdge.ofExactEdge` shows `U` also dominates
the punishment floor.  Hence the pair `(P,U)` is a positive exact
floor-admissible Nash--Bellman edge.

This is an actual-source root producer for the one-outsider specialization of
the earlier paid arm, conditional only on taking the vanishing-error cluster
described in section 16.  It does not solve the original producer arrow:

1. no exact path returning `U` to `P` is produced;
2. with two players existence is already covered by the stronger checked
   two-player theorem; and
3. for several outsiders, positivity of a largest coordinate defect at `p*`
   does not orient all faces of their best-response cube, and the scalar affine
   dichotomy above no longer applies.

The smallest higher-dimensional missing theorem is now precise: extend the
forced-Q separation to a nonzero boundary degree for the full outsider
best-response field, allowing a pure anchor transfer on a boundary face as a
successful positive root rather than treating it as failure.  Only after that
root exists does the payoff-return problem become the remaining consumer gap.

## 18. Two outsiders: exact static separation without a charged root

The Section 16 data alone do not extend to a two-dimensional outsider cube,
even when the tail is exactly the punishment vector.

Take players `1,2,3` cyclically and tail `s=(1,1,1)`.  For each player `i` and
opponent coalition `S subset {1,2,3}\{i}`, put

`g_i(S)=1_(i+1 in S)-2*1_(i+2 in S)`.

Define the raw terminal table coordinatewise as follows.  If `i` does not
Quit and the nonempty coalition `S` does, set `r(S)_i=3`.  If `i` Quits alone,
set `r({i})_i=1`.  If `i` Quits together with a nonempty opponent coalition
`S`, set

`r(S union {i})_i=3+g_i(S)`.

These prescriptions are consistent because they are made independently in
each payoff coordinate.  Every quitting coalition containing `i` pays it at
least one.  Therefore Quit at date zero guarantees player `i` at least one
against every opponent profile.  Conversely all-Continue opponents cap its
best reply at the solo reward one.  Hence

`quittingPunishmentValue reward i=1`

for all three players, exactly matching the tail `s`.

Write `(x,y,z)` for the three Quit probabilities.  The Quit-minus-Continue
endpoint gaps at tail `s` are the expectations of the edge functions above:

`G_1=y-2z`,

`G_2=z-2x`,

`G_3=x-2y`.

Force player `1` to Quit.  At the paid-like row `(x,y,z)=(1,1,0)`,

`G_1=1`, `G_2=-2`, `G_3=-1`.

Thus the owner strictly prefers Quit and outsider `2` has a strict deviation
to Continue.  The unique outsider Nash completion on the forced-owner face is
`(1,0,1)`: player `2` Continues and player `3` Quits.  There the owner gap is
`G_1=-2`.  This is precisely the nonnegative paid balance, positive outsider
defect, and strict negative balance at forced-owner completion extracted in
Section 16.

Nevertheless the full root game has no positively charged product Nash root.
At any binary product Nash, `p_i>0` implies `G_i>=0`, while `p_i=0` implies
`G_i<=0`.  If all three probabilities were positive, the first implications
would give

`y>=2z`, `z>=2x`, `x>=2y`,

and hence `x>=8x`, impossible for `x>0`.  If, say, `x>0` but `y=0`, then
`G_1>=0` forces `z=0`, while the zero-probability condition for player `3`
would require `G_3=x<=0`, another contradiction.  Cyclic symmetry handles
every partial support.  Hence `(0,0,0)` is the unique Nash root.

This example is not claimed to satisfy `HasTerminalExploitabilityGap`.  In
fact the checked three-player existence theorem rules out its being a genuine
counterexample to uniform equilibrium.  Its conclusion is narrower: the
static root data retained in Section 16, even augmented by exact punishment
values and strict margins, do not produce a positive root for two outsiders.
Any actual-data proof must use additional information from the global terminal
gap to orient the full boundary, or supply a nonzero degree/acyclic carrier
which the static forced face does not encode.

### 19. Universal floor-Nash dichotomy and the genuine support seam

Let `gamma>0` and assume `HasTerminalExploitabilityGap reward gamma`.  Write

`P_i=quittingPunishmentValue reward i`.

Consider the finite one-stage root game with continuation tail `P`, and choose
an ordinary mixed product Nash row `q`.  This gives an exact Nash--Bellman edge
from `P` to its successor payoff.  The checked floor-forward theorem
`quittingPunishmentValue_le_rootSuccessorPayoff_of_tail_ge` shows that the
successor is also above `P`.

There are exactly two cases.

1. If the absorption charge of `q` is positive, this is already a positive
   exact floor-admissible edge.
2. If its charge is zero, every player Continues surely.  Endpoint Nash then
   gives

   `r({i})_i <= P_i`

   for every player `i`.

Apply the terminal gap to the behavioral all-Continue profile.  Its terminal
payoff is zero.  Against opponents who never Quit, any unilateral behavioral
deviation produces either no absorption or the singleton outcome `{i}`;
hence its expected payoff is at most `max(r({i})_i,0)`.  The fixed gain
`gamma` therefore selects some player `o` with

`gamma <= r({o})_o`.

On the other hand, the all-Continue stationary punishment row gives

`P_o <= max(r({o})_o,0)=r({o})_o`.

Together with endpoint Nash, this proves the exact tightness identity

`P_o=r({o})_o>=gamma`.

Now apply `soloCertification_or_universalJoining`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Punishment/OwnerSoloCertification.lean`) to
`o`.  Its certification arm would provide a positive singleton hazard at
which every outsider is inactive.  Since the owner's own singleton payoff is
nonnegative, the checked theorem
`isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_soloReward_of_inactive` would then give a uniform-
equilibrium payoff, contradicting the positive terminal gap.  Consequently
the other arm holds:

for every `0<p<=1`, some `j!=o` satisfies

`r({o})_j < (1-p) r({j})_j + p r({o,j})_j`.

This proves the following universal reduction in ordinary mathematics:

> either the punishment-floor root game supplies a positive exact floor-
> admissible edge, or there is a punishment-tight positive-solo owner carrying
> a strict joining obstruction at every positive rate.

The latter is precisely the named support-enlargement seam already exposed by
`QuittingPositiveDebtDynamicTailWitness.limitOwner_endpointNash_requires_second_quitter`
and
`limitOwner_exists_universalJoiner_or_switchingPair`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Chronology/EventualAllContinuePlateau.lean`).
It is stronger and more conjecture-facing than the single forced face retained
in Section 16.  Any continuation of the present route must convert this
universal joiner/switching-pair data into the one-step signed refusal transport
stated at the top; merely adding another static face sign does not change the
universal obstruction.

The Section 18 table is an exact boundary test.  At tail `P=(1,1,1)` it has
only the all-Continue Nash root.  But for owner `1`, take tail

`v=(1,3,3)`

and the singleton root in which player `1` Quits with rate `0<x<=2/3` while
the others Continue.  The root is a fixed point at target `v`: absorption at
owner `1` pays exactly the singleton vector `(1,3,3)`.  The owner is
indifferent.  Player `2`'s Quit endpoint is at most its prescribed value, and
player `3`'s endpoint difference is

`3x-2<=0`.

Thus this is an exact positive stationary self-loop.  Equivalently, it is the
general owner-solo certificate from
`isUniformEquilibriumPayoff_soloReward_of_inactive`.  Raising the tail above
the punishment floor repairs the frozen-tail failure completely.  Therefore
Section 18 does not kill the universal dynamic thesis; an actual terminal-gap
candidate must lie in the universal-joining branch where every such solo tail
compensation is blocked.

### 20. No positive floor edge forces a tight collision cycle

This step no longer uses the special table.  Continue with the arbitrary
finite game and punishment floor `P` from Section 19, and suppose the root
game at tail `P` has no positively charged endpoint-Nash row.  Write

`s_i=r({i})_i`

and call player `i` *tight* when `P_i=s_i`.  The all-Continue root is endpoint
Nash, so `s_i<=P_i` for every `i`; Section 19 shows that the tight set is
nonempty and contains at least one player with `s_i>=gamma`.

Fix any tight owner `o`.  At the solo root where `o` Quits with probability
`p` and every outsider Continues, evaluated at tail `P`, the owner is exactly
indifferent.  For an outsider `j!=o`, the Quit-minus-Continue endpoint gap is

`J_(o,j)(p)
 = (1-p)(s_j-P_j)
   + p(r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j)`.

Indeed, if `j` Quits, the owner Continues with probability `1-p`, producing
`{j}`, and joins with probability `p`, producing `{o,j}`.  If `j` Continues,
the corresponding outcomes pay `P_j` and `r({o})_j`.

Suppose that no tight outsider has positive collision increment; that is,

`P_j=s_j  implies  r({o,j})_j<=r({o})_j`

for every `j!=o`.  Then some sufficiently small `p>0` makes every `J_(o,j)(p)`
nonpositive.  For a tight `j` this holds for every `p`.  For a slack `j`, put
`delta_j=P_j-s_j>0`; then

`J_(o,j)(p)=-delta_j
  +p(delta_j+r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j)`.

If the coefficient of `p` is nonpositive there is nothing to prove.  If it is
positive, take `p` below
`delta_j/(delta_j+r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j)`.  There are only finitely many
outsiders, so one common positive `p<1` works.  The resulting solo root is an
exact endpoint-Nash row of positive charge at tail `P`, contradicting the
standing assumption.

Consequently every tight owner has a distinct tight successor `j` satisfying

`0<r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j`.

This defines a strict serial directed relation on the finite nonempty tight
set.  Following successors and taking the first repeated vertex produces a
periodic cycle of length at least two.  Since the chosen cycle has finitely
many strict edges, their collision increments have a positive minimum
`kappa>0`.

For a punishment-tight successor, the displayed formula reduces to

`J_(o,j)(p)=p(r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j)`.

Thus the solo-owner row at tail `P`, with every outsider continuing and owner
clock at least `eta`, must pay endpoint error at least `eta*kappa` along the
selected edge.  This bound is not asserted after arbitrary other outsider
marginals are made positive; such marginals can destroy the factorization.
This is the same algebraic shape as
`quittingRootEndpointDifference_solo_eq_hazard_mul_toggle_of_tight`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/OneActiveTransferDefectGraph.lean`),
but it is **not** an instance of that declaration: the checked theorem assumes
cross-singleton tightness `r({j})_j=r({o})_j`, whereas the present argument
uses punishment tightness `P_j=r({j})_j`.  The factorization here is ordinary
mathematics at the fixed tail `P`.

This is a real universal support reduction, but it is not yet the desired
producer.  The repeated object is a player label, not a payoff state.  The
tail coordinate changes needed to repair one insertion defect can fund or
erase the next defect, and no exact signed telescope across those changes has
yet been proved.  Section 18 realizes precisely the bare tight-collision
cycle and repairs it by moving to a singleton payoff tail; the terminal-gap
universal-joining property is what rules out that particular repair, but a
general chronological account remains open.

### 21. Exact chronological orientation of one tight collision edge
