Let `o!=j`, assume `P_j=s_j`, and let one singleton phase use owner `o` at
hazard `0<p<1`.  Write `t` for its successor tail and

`u=p r({o})+(1-p)t`

for its Bellman current value.  The inactive player `j`'s pure-Quit endpoint
is

`(1-p)P_j+p r({o,j})_j`.

Therefore its exact endpoint-Nash inequality is equivalent to

`(1-p)(t_j-P_j)
 >= p(r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j)`.                                      `(21.1)`

This is the precise funding law for the collision increment.  A positive
edge from Section 20 does not give current credit; it demands *future tail
excess* in the collider's labelled coordinate.

Two adjacency consequences are immediate.

First, suppose `j` is the owner of the immediately following singleton phase
and mixes at a positive rate.  Its active equality and its punishment
tightness force the following phase's current value to satisfy

`t_j=P_j`.

Then (21.1) is impossible whenever the selected collision increment is
positive.  Thus the directed tight collision cycle cannot simply be read as a
forward chronological owner word.

Second, suppose instead that `j` owns the immediately preceding singleton
phase.  Its active equality pins the current value of the `o` phase:

`u_j=P_j`.

The Bellman equation uniquely requires

`t_j-P_j = p(P_j-r({o})_j)/(1-p)`.

Substituting in (21.1) shows that this reverse adjacency is feasible in
coordinate `j` exactly when

`r({o,j})_j<=P_j`.

Call such a selected collision edge *weak*.  If instead
`r({o,j})_j>P_j`, call it *strong*.  At an arbitrary exact phase, the pure-
Quit lower bound gives the current-excess forcing inequality

`u_j-P_j >= p(r({o,j})_j-P_j)`

for every strong edge.  A later positive-hazard owner phase of `j` pins its
own current excess to zero, so any chronology using that edge must explicitly
transport and then discharge this labelled excess before `j` becomes active.

These identities rule out the naive signed account in which collision margin
itself is added along the repeated label cycle.  Weak reverse edges may be
paid entirely by the tail displacement
`p(P_j-r({o})_j)/(1-p)`, while strong edges create current excess whose later
erasure depends on all intervening singleton rows.  The exact unresolved
account must therefore combine:

- the labelled excess vector `v-P`, not just its sum;
- the chronology and owner order;
- strong collision forcing; and
- the full-gap preemption inequalities, which are the only current universal
  data capable of pricing intervening row transfers.

No contradiction or return is claimed in this section.  It is a universal
orientation lemma and a no-go for silently treating the Section 20 label
cycle as a chronological Bellman path.

### 22. Every tight collision edge is preemption or strong forcing

For a selected edge `o -> j` of the Section 20 cycle, define

`A_(o,j)=P_j-r({o})_j`,

`C_(o,j)=r({o,j})_j-P_j`,

and

`Delta_(o,j)=r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j`.

Punishment tightness of `j` gives `P_j=s_j`, and the exact decomposition is

`Delta_(o,j)=A_(o,j)+C_(o,j)`.

Every selected edge has `Delta_(o,j)>=kappa`.  Hence

`max(A_(o,j),C_(o,j))>=kappa/2`.

If the first term is large, then `j` strictly solo-preempts `o` at margin
`kappa/2`:

`r({o})_j+kappa/2<=r({j})_j`.

If the second term is large, the edge is quantitatively strong and Section
21 forces, at every solo phase of `o` with hazard `p`,

`u_j-P_j>=p*kappa/2`.

Thus the periodic tight collision cycle admits an exact two-coloring in which
every edge is either a quantitative preemption edge or a quantitative labelled
current-excess forcing edge (and it may be both).  This decomposition is
scale-free once the finite cycle and its minimum `kappa` are fixed.

This connects the new floor reduction to the existing global-gap geometry.
`QuittingTerminalExploitabilityWitness.nonempty_soloPreemptionCycle`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Collision/PreemptionCycle.lean`)
already produces a full-`gamma` preemption cycle, and
`immediateSingletonCollision_and_soloPreemptionCycle` retains an immediate
collision certificate simultaneously.  Those named declarations do not
align every collision edge with the same preemption cycle or convert a colored
cycle into an exact Bellman return.  The genuinely new obligation is narrower:
between two strong edges, prove that the intervening preemption run cannot
erase the forced labelled excess without paying an equal or larger amount
into a cyclic signed account.

No such telescope is proved here.  The decomposition prevents a hidden sign
change in the proposed account and fixes the only two terms that a universal
forcing proof may use on the tight cycle.

### 23. Exact consumer alignment after the source-boundary refresh

The current production boundary is explicit in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/UniformExistenceBoundary.lean`.
`PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissibleReturnConsumer` must take every actual
full-replacement paid-row exit and return a
`QuittingPositiveAdmissibleReturn`: one positive exact punishment-floor-
admissible Nash--Bellman edge together with an exact path from its current
state back to its tail.  A paid gain, a forced root face, or an independently
selected stack of finite prefixes supplies none of those three equalities.

The alternative `VanishingDebtAtomChronologicalConsumer` is equally literal.
It requires one `QuittingChronologicalDebtShadowingCertificate` for every
accuracy, with one executable infinite root sequence, every-suffix prescribed
and direct-debt forcing bounds, vanishing joint and every-player-deleted
survival from every start, and small initial candidate debt.  The static
collision labels in Sections 20--22 provide no candidate semantic debt,
generated secant, moving-source reprojection, or survival statement.  They
therefore do not instantiate that consumer either.

There is a third checked endpoint route: support-rational root paths with
divergent absorption at every accuracy.  Propositions 30--31 of
`notes/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION.md` show that vanishing
support-paid normalized seams on source-matched exact windows would feed
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_supportRationalDivergentPaths`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Paths/SupportWitnessPathCompiler.lean`).  My
Round 7 review verifies that conditional consumer, including its active and
passive signs.  No vanishing-seam family is constructed here.

Finally, full norm closure is already known to be unavailable on the optimized
counterexample tail: `exists_pos_eventually_endpointDistance_ge_absorptionMass`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Chronology/AbsorptionClockBallisticity.lean`)
gives a uniform positive absorption-clock speed on every sufficiently late
positive window.  Thus an argument which silently restores payoff-state
recurrence is not merely absent from the paid interface; it contradicts the
checked counterexample-tail reduction.  Any genuinely different window route
must use the support-aware one-sided exemption, not ambient norm recurrence.

This source audit changes the acceptance test for the thesis.  A future local
lemma counts only if it is inserted into an actual paid-row-to-return
construction, or if it produces all-accuracy support-rational divergent paths
and is explicitly presented as the different endpoint route.

### 24. Preemption pays normalized seam; strong forcing pays seam or charge

The two colors of Section 22 have an exact interpretation in the signed
periodic-window defect.  Continue with a selected edge `o -> j`, let a
chronological solo phase of `o` have hazard `0<p<1`, tail `t`, and current

`u=p r({o})+(1-p)t`.

Assume the root is exact endpoint Nash, `P_j=r({j})_j`, and write

```text
A=P_j-r({o})_j,
C=r({o,j})_j-P_j,
Delta=A+C>0.
```

For the one-phase window, absorbed mass is `p` and its normalized endpoint
seam is

`z=(u-t)/p=r({o})-t`.

Only `o` is active.  Hence the support-aware defect charges player `j` by
`max(0,-z_j)=max(0,t_j-r({o})_j)`.  Exact Nash for the passive player gives

`t_j-P_j >= p Delta/(1-p)`.                         `(24.1)`

If this is a preemption-colored edge, `A>=kappa/2`, then

```text
t_j-r({o})_j
  =(t_j-P_j)+A
  >= p Delta/(1-p)+A
  > kappa/2.                                        (24.2)
```

Thus every exact solo realization of a preemption edge has one-phase
support-paid normalized seam strictly larger than `kappa/2`, independently
of how small its hazard is.  This is why dividing by absorbed mass cannot make
the preemption obstruction perturbative.

This conclusion survives the sharper action-gap compensation of Proposition
33 in `notes/CODEX_NOETHER__QUIT_TIME_COMPACTIFICATION.md`.  Periodically
repeating the one solo root makes its actual continuation equal to
`r({o})`.  Player `j`'s exact stationary Quit-minus-Continue gain is then

```text
(1-p)P_j+p r({o,j})_j-r({o})_j
  =A+pC
  =(1-p)A+p Delta.                                  (24.2a)
```

If `A>=kappa/2` and `Delta>=kappa`, the last expression is a convex
combination of two quantities at least `kappa/2`, and hence is itself at
least `kappa/2`.  Therefore `kappa/2` is a lower bound on the *actual* support
error of the repeated row, not only on the uncompensated seam proxy.  No
pre-existing Continue slack can absorb it.

For a strong edge, Section 21 gives instead

`u_j-P_j>=p C>=p kappa/2`.                          `(24.3)`

Suppose a finite solo-owner chronology starts with this phase and let `ell`
be the first later phase at which `j` is the solo owner with an interior
hazard.  Its active equality and `P_j=r({j})_j` pin both its current and its
successor-tail coordinates to `P_j`.  Take the window from the current `u`
through and including that `j` phase.  If its absorbed mass is `a`, then `j`
is active in the window and its normalized seam obeys

`E >= |u_j-P_j|/a >= p*kappa/(2a)`.                 `(24.4)`

Consequently, for every `K>=1`, either

```text
E >= kappa/(2K),
```

or the chronology between forcing and discharge has literal absorbed mass

```text
a > K p.                                            (24.5)
```

This is the exact seam-or-charge dichotomy for a strong edge.  It separates
the two remaining universal mechanisms: a bounded charge ratio makes the
support-paid defect quantitative, while a small defect forces a
nonperturbative increase of the absorption clock before the collider can be
activated.

The distinction is necessary.  Take scalar data `A=-1`, `C=2`, `Delta=1`
and fund (24.1) at equality.  Then for small `p`,

`t_j-r({o})_j=(-1+2p)/(1-p)<0`,

so the passive one-phase defect is zero even though
`u_j-P_j=pC=2p>0`.  Strong current excess cannot be replaced by the
preemption estimate.  It becomes visible to the support-aware norm only when
the window is extended to a later activation of `j`, precisely as in (24.4).

Equations (24.2)--(24.5) are ordinary mathematics.  They still do not provide
the reached chronology required by either checked direct consumer.  The hard
next adapter is to obtain, from the actual paid first-disagreement row, a
serial owner word in which every forced collider is eventually activated and
the charge-amplification alternatives can be telescoped around the finite
label cycle without changing the source profile.

### 25. Source-level transport audit and pivot

The checked static transport interface rules out the remaining implicit
shortcut.  In `QuittingSoloPreemptionCycle.forcedCellGraph`, the forced edge
of a preemption phase runs from payoff cell `(o,j)` to `(j,j)`.  The theorem
`QuittingSoloPreemptionCycle.target_ne_source_forcedCellGraph`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/PreemptionTransport.lean`) says
that no forced edge can follow another; equivalently,
`QuittingSoloPreemptionCycle.edges_eq_nil_of_closedWalk` says no nonempty
closed walk exists in the forced graph.  Adding the missing observer switch
does concatenate the phases, but
`QuittingSoloPreemptionCycle.period_mul_gap_le_sum_observerSwitchCost` prices
those switches by at least the whole accumulated gap.  The diagnostic
`QuittingStaticObserverSwitchData.augmentedCycleWeight_nonpos`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Collision/PreemptionTransport.lean`)
then makes every table-justified augmented cycle nonpositive.

Thus Sections 21--24 cannot be completed by reweighting the same terminal
payoff cells.  A successful transport really needs profile-derived phase
values, observer-indexed continuations, or semantic debts.  The paid-row
carrier supplies a literal receiving profile, so its all-Continue semantic
spine does provide such values, but its exact account is

`debt_t = rootDefect_t + survival_t debt_(t+1)`

by `QuittingTerminalSemanticPrefixChain.debt_account`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/TerminalSemanticDirectedTransport.lean`).
These spine roots are exact Nash--Bellman edges only when every coordinate
root defect is zero.  The paid temporal dispatch does the opposite at its
selected row: in the later orientation the observer has a positive endpoint
defect, and in the earlier orientation a dispatched outsider has one bounded
below by the terminal-gap scale.  Consequently the paid profile's reached
chronology cannot be inserted into the exact punishment-floor admissible
relation without precisely the nonperturbative Nashification already isolated
in Sections 10--18.

This is the route's kill checkpoint.  The static labels cannot concatenate;
the one actual chronology carried by the row has a quantitatively nonzero
Nash defect; and the source/full-replacement adapter exposes no second
chronology after a Nashifying correction.  This does not prove that the full
paid producer is false.  It proves that the present refusal/collision account
has exhausted all data named in its proposed one-step lemma.  Continuing it
would recycle either a free observer switch refuted by the checked potential
or an inexact Bellman edge.  The main conjecture-facing work therefore pivots
to the independently checked eventual-all-Continue blocker residue of the
canonical exact dynamic tail.

### 25A. The paid interface does not always supply an opponent block hazard

The external packing note's equations `(1)`--`(2)` need one qualification
before they can be used on the actual source.  If both compared pure Quit
times `s<t` are finite, then outside the event that an opponent absorbs or
ties between those dates, both observer plans eventually Quit alone and have
the same terminal reward.  In that finite--finite case the bound

```text
gain <= 2M * P(opponent event between s and t)
```

is valid.

But `QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow` allows its later witness to be `Never`
(`later : Option Nat`, with `none` admitted).  Then the two plans need not
agree off the opponent event: the finite plan may end at the observer's
singleton reward, while the Never plan ends at the zero cemetery convention.
The later-receiving example in Section 3 is already an exact falsifier.  The
opponent Never Quits, observer Quit at `0` pays `-1`, observer Never pays `0`,
and the paid gain and row live mass are both one; nevertheless the probability
of any opponent absorption in the proposed block is zero.

Thus a general actual paid row supplies positive first-disagreement live mass,
not necessarily a positive opponent-absorption block.  Any block-hazard route
must first prove that a finite--finite witness occurs (or separately dispatch
the singleton/cemetery `Never` arm).  Sections 26--29 concern the finite-block
arm after a genuine aggregate absorption lower bound has been supplied; they
do not silently apply equations `(1)`--`(2)` to `Never`.

### 26. Fixed aggregate absorption has an all-behavior compiler

The proposed target `(B)` in
`notes/CHATGPT_EXTERNAL__PAID_BLOCK_HAZARD_PACKING_REDUCTION.md` does not need
a new strategic denominator theorem.  The scalar affine identity in that note
is not, by itself, an all-behavior argument, but the existing reversed-block
lasso construction contains the missing support, seam, rationality, and
positive-absorption fields.

For a finite exact floor-admissible path `pi`, let its decoded stage charges be
`q_0,...,q_(H-1)` and define

```text
A(pi) = 1 - product_(t<H) (1-q_t).
```

**Proposition 26 (aggregate-absorption payoff-near-return compiler).**  Fix
`c>0`.  Suppose that for every `eta>0` there are
floor-admissible states `source_eta,target_eta` and a finite exact admissible
path `pi_eta : source_eta -> target_eta` such that

```text
max_i |payoff(source_eta)_i-payoff(target_eta)_i| <= eta,
A(pi_eta) >= c.
```

Then the quitting game has a uniform-equilibrium payoff against unrestricted
behavioral deviations.

**Proof.**  Every stage charge lies in `[0,1]`, so `A(pi)<=1`; sampling one
path shows `0<c<=1`.  Fix a requested lasso error `epsilon>0` and invoke the
hypothesis at

```text
eta = epsilon*c.
```

Reverse the decoded exact forward block.  Put

```text
seamError    = epsilon*c,
supportError = epsilon-seamError = epsilon*(1-c).
```

Both errors are nonnegative and sum to `epsilon`.  Every nonclosing policy
equation is an exact reindexing of a Bellman edge.  Exact endpoint Nash at
each forward row gives zero support error and hence also the weakened
`supportError` inequality.  At the closing phase the policy residual is at
most the endpoint seam, while

```text
seamError = epsilon*c <= epsilon*A(pi).
```

The reversed cycle's weighted absorption is exactly `A(pi)`.  All forward
values dominate the punishment floor, and hence also the floor minus
`epsilon`.  Finally `A(pi)>0` implies that at least one of the finitely many
stage charges is positive, which supplies the required absorbing phase.
It also rules out an empty player type, since the unique empty product root
has absorption zero.
Thus the reversed block is a finite single-seam projective lasso at error
`epsilon`.  Producing one at every positive error invokes the existing
single-seam lasso consumer, whose conclusion is the unrestricted-behavior
uniform-payoff statement.  QED.

The exact ingredients inspected are
`quittingCyclicWeightedAbsorption_reversedForwardCycle` and
`quittingFiniteSingleSeamProjectiveLasso_of_reversedForwardBlock`
(`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Projective/ForwardBlockSingleSeam.lean`) and
`quittingGame_exists_uniformEquilibriumPayoff_of_singleSeamProjectiveLassos`
(through `UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Projective/LassoAll.lean`).  The proof
is also the direct aggregate version of
`exists_singleSeamProjectiveLasso_of_floorPrefix_payoffNearReturn`
(`PunishmentFloorNearReturn.lean`), whose present statement obtains its
aggregate lower bound from one large stage.

This proposition is a genuinely all-behavior compiler for `(B)`, but not a
new lasso architecture: it is an omitted direct specialization of existing
block machinery.  It does **not** instantiate
`QuittingPositiveAdmissiblePayoffNearReturnFamily`, whose current field asks
for one large edge.  It instead gives a direct route to the weaker source
output `PaidFirstDisagreementUniformPayoffConsumer` if `(B)` can be produced
with the full paid quantifiers.

### 27. Exact prescribed-payoff-matched erasure of a unit behavioral block hazard

The remaining Nashification problem is real even when the paid profile's
prescribed payoff coordinate is already a floor-admissible source.  Here is a
finite exact regression.

Take players `1,2,3`.  For each player `i`, define a nonnegative function
`b_i` on coalitions of its opponents, including the empty coalition, by

```text
b_1(A) = 0,
b_2(A) = 1 if A={3}, and 0 otherwise,
b_3(A) = 1 if A={2}, and 0 otherwise.
```

For every nonempty quitting coalition `T`, define the `i` coordinate of the
reward by

```text
r(T)_i = b_i(T)                 if i is not in T,
r(T)_i = b_i(T\{i}) - 1         if i is in T.
```

All rewards lie in `[-1,1]`.  In particular

```text
r({2,3})=(0,0,0),       r({1})_1=-1.
```

Let the receiving behavioral profile have players `2` and `3` Quit surely at
date `1`, while player `1` waits beyond that date.  Its terminal semantic
pair has prescribed payoff exactly `r({2,3})=0`; its best-response cap vector
is `(0,1,1)`, so the full semantic pair is not diagonal.  Against the same
opponents, observer `1` gets `-1` from pure Quit at date `0` and `0` from pure
Quit at date `2` (the
opponents have already absorbed).  Hence the two pure times give a literal
later-receiving paid first-disagreement row of gain one.  The opponent block
absorption between them is one.  The later witness is even an exact best
response: waiting gives zero, while quitting alone or joining the collision
gives minus one.

The receiving profile even has the literal one-coordinate replacement form
used by the paid source: start from a profile where player `3` Quits at date
`1` and player `2` Never Quits, then replace only player `2` by Quit at date
`1`.  What is absent is not the update syntax but the frontier's limiting
debt geometry.

The behavioral punishment vector is also exactly zero.  Pure Never guarantees
player `i` either zero or the nonnegative value `b_i(A)` against every first
opponent coalition `A`.  Conversely, when all opponents Never, the best of
Never (zero) and Quit alone (minus one) is zero.  Thus the receiving
prescribed payoff is not merely close to an admissible anchor; it is the
literal floor vector.

Now consider the one-stage root game with continuation tail `v=0`.  Conditional
on any realized opponent Quit set `A`, including `A=empty`, player `i`'s
forced-Continue payoff is `b_i(A)`, whereas its forced-Quit payoff is
`b_i(A)-1`.  Quit is therefore strictly dominated by Continue, by exactly
one, for every player and every product law of the opponents.  The unique
exact endpoint-Nash product root is all-Continue.  Its Bellman successor is
again zero and its absorption charge is zero.

It follows inductively from the orientation `src=tail,tgt=current` of
`quittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation` that **every** finite exact
admissible path whose source payoff is zero has only zero-charge edges and
keeps payoff zero.  Thus unit behavioral block hazard, exact paid-witness
optimality, exact prescribed-payoff source matching, and exact floor matching
do not produce any positive aggregate exact-block absorption.

This table is not a counterexample to the full paid consumer.  It does not
claim the positive-minimum tangent frontier, separated full-replacement
cluster, or terminal exploitability gap.  It falsifies the local implication
from one paid row plus its strongest obvious prescribed-payoff matching to `(B)`.
Consequently any Nashification theorem must use an additional global field of
the actual frontier, not only the row and its reached prescribed payoff.

### 28. Criterion `(R)` and exact-block replication fail as literal source data

The finite-packing implication `(R) -> payoff near-return` is correct, but
`(R)` cannot describe a renewable object living inside the counterexample
branch.  Let `C` be the common exact punishment-floor finite-prefix charge
bound supplied by a terminal exploitability witness.  For every fixed
`c>0` and every exact floor-admissible path,

```text
(number of edges with q_e>=c)*c <= total raw charge <= C.       (28.1)
```

This is the all-admissible-prefix version of
`highChargeCount_mul_threshold_le_prefixChargeBound` / the corresponding
`highAbsorptionStageCount` statement in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Capacity/InfiniteOrbitConsequences.lean`.
Choosing an integer `K>C/c` contradicts `(R)`.  Thus proving `(R)` from the
full paid source would already be a contradiction proof of uniform-payoff
existence, not a family that can consistently be retained and iterated under
the terminal witness.

The same issue applies to aggregate blocks.  For each finite block,

```text
1-product_t(1-q_t) <= sum_t q_t.                      (28.2)
```

Therefore a block with aggregate absorption at least `c` spends at least `c`
units of the raw exact-prefix budget.  If `K` such blocks literally
concatenate at their reached endpoints, their union spends at least `Kc`, so
again `K<=C/c`.  This does not obstruct Proposition 26, which asks for only
one tolerance-dependent block at a time.  It does rule out an unqualified
replication proof.

The actual paid source does not meet the concatenation premise.  Its eventual
rows live on the independently indexed profiles

```text
frontier.fullReplacementProfile mover (endpoint.subseq rank).
```

`FullReplacementCluster` retains convergence of their terminal semantic
pairs, coordinatewise debt-change inequalities, and exact mover-coordinate
debt change.  It contains no exact Nash--Bellman edge from rank `k` to rank
`k+1`, no equality between one produced block's target and the next rank's
source, and no invariant restart class.  Hence neither the rows nor separately
Nashified rankwise blocks may be stacked to obtain `(R)`.

### 29. Clean boundary after the denominator and replication audits

The three questions in the external packing note now have separate answers.

0. **Behavioral block input:** only conditionally.  A finite--finite paid row
   gives the stated opponent-event bound; a finite--Never row need not give
   any opponent absorption at all.
1. **All-behavior block denominator:** yes, conditionally.  Proposition 26
   supplies it from exact per-row Nash--Bellman equalities, punishment floors,
   endpoint payoff closeness, and fixed aggregate absorption.  The scalar
   affine fixed-point formula alone was insufficient, but no further cap
   hypothesis is needed once these exact block fields are present.
2. **Replication `(R)`:** not from the current data.  It is incompatible with
   the terminal-witness prefix budget unless used as the final contradiction,
   and actual paid ranks are not reached consecutive sources.
3. **Nashified paid block:** still open for the full source.  Proposition 27
   proves that a paid row, even with unit behavioral hazard, exact witness
   optimality, prescribed-payoff source matching, and floor matching, can lose
   all absorption under exact Nashification.

The exact remaining producer gap is therefore:

> From the separated full-replacement cluster and its eventual paid rows,
> use a genuinely global frontier datum to construct, for every `eta>0`, one
> exact floor-admissible path with endpoint payoff seam at most `eta` and
> aggregate absorption bounded below by one fixed `c>0`.

Equivalently, one must prove that the strict-dominance erasure mechanism of
Section 27 is incompatible with the *full* positive-minimum/full-replacement
debt geometry, or make the resulting dominance debt pay for a nonlocal exact
block elsewhere.  Neither convergence of semantic pairs nor the paid block
hazard provides that implication.  This is the clean stopping boundary; no
large-edge packing or local root correction should be attempted again without
an actual reached-source adapter.

The mover's vanishing full-replacement debt is the first actual-source field
that could oppose the regression, but an incidence seam remains.  The paid
observer is distinct from the mover, and the finite--finite opponent event only
says that *some* opponent of the observer absorbs.  It does not say that the
mover carries any fixed part of that absorption.  Hence small mover debt does
not by itself prevent all of the block hazard from being carried by unchanged,
strictly Quit-dominated nonmovers.  A concrete source-level strengthening
would be either

```text
P(block absorption in which mover Quits) >= kappa_0 > 0,
```

with enough reached optimality to Nashify that mass, or a debt-transfer
identity charging absorption by nonmovers to an exact floor block elsewhere.
No inspected `FullReplacementCluster` or paid-row field supplies this
incidence statement.

### 30. Exact selected-ray regression: normalized curvature need not involve the mover

The incidence hope already fails for the exact one-ray mathematics consumed
by the curvature decoder, even after retaining a flat tangent, negligible
source excess, zero full-replacement mover debt, inactive-coordinate
nonentry, and a fixed off-minimum endpoint excursion.  The sole full frontier
field not tested below is global minimum provenance (and the compatible rays
for the other active movers).

Take four players `o,m,h,k`.  Fix `0<lambda<=1/2`, and put

```text
a        = 1/8,
p        = 1/2 + a*lambda,
q0       = 1/2,
r        = 3/2 - (3/4)/p.
```

Then `0<r<1`.  The prescribed strategies are: `o` and `h` Quit at date `1`;
`m` Quits at date `0` with probability `p` and otherwise Never; and `k`
Quits at date `1` with probability `r` and otherwise Never.  The selected
full replacement changes only `m` to Never.

Only the following payoff cells matter.  For observer `o`, adding or removing
`k` from a displayed coalition does not change the value:

```text
r_o({o,m})=1,   r_o({o})=0,   r_o({m})=0,
r_o({o,h})=0,   r_o({h})=1.
```

For mover `m`,

```text
r_m({m})=q0,
r_m({o,h})=1,
r_m({o,h,k})=0,
```

and every coalition in which `m` joins at date `1` is assigned a value below
`1-r`.  Coordinates `h,k` are identically zero.  Unused cells may be filled
arbitrarily within the same strict inequalities.

Against the source opponents, observer `o` obtains `p` by Quit at date `0`,
zero by the prescribed Quit at date `1`, and `1-p` by waiting beyond date
`1`.  Hence its cap and debt are

```text
B_o(source)=p,          d_o(source)=p.
```

Mover `m` has cap `1-r`: Never obtains
`(1-r)*1+r*0=1-r`, while Quit at date `0` obtains `q0` and every other finite
choice is worse.  Its prescribed debt is

```text
d_m(source)=p*((1-r)-q0)=3/4-p=1/4-lambda/8.
```

Thus the total source debt is exactly `3/4`, independent of `lambda`; the
other two debts vanish.  As `lambda -> 0`, the source semantic pairs converge
to a base with debt vector `(1/2,1/4,0,0)`, so the source excess over scale is
identically zero.

At the full replacement, `m` Never Quits and is an exact best response, so
its debt is zero.  Observer `o` still receives zero from prescribed Quit at
date `1`, zero from Quit at date `0`, and one from waiting beyond date `1`.
Its debt is therefore one.  The endpoint total debt is one, a fixed excess
`1/4` over the limiting source total.

Now mix the source strategy of `m` with its full replacement with target
weight `lambda`.  The resulting date-zero Quit probability is

```text
p'=(1-lambda)*p
   =1/2-3*lambda/8-lambda^2/8 < 1/2.
```

Consequently the mixed observer cap is `1-p'`, and its normalized debt
direction is

```text
((1-p')-p)/lambda = 1/4+lambda/8 -> 1/4.
```

The mover's mixed debt is `(1-lambda)d_m(source)`, so its normalized direction
is

```text
-d_m(source) -> -1/4.
```

The other directions are zero.  The limiting selected column is therefore
flat, `(1/4,-1/4,0,0)`, and has no positive entry into a limiting zero-debt
coordinate.  The observer's literal normalized curvature is

```text
(d_o(full)-d_o(source)) - (1/4+lambda/8)
  = (1-lambda)/4.                                      (30.1)
```

In particular it is bounded below by `1/8`.  For example, the exact decoder
budget holds uniformly with

```text
gain=1/64, endpointError=1/64, sourceError=lambda/64;
```

the left side of its curvature inequality is at most `3*lambda/64`, whereas
the right side is at least `lambda/8`.

Nevertheless the receiving full-replacement strategy of `m` is literally
Never.  At the decoder's displayed source error `lambda/64`, every admissible
source-near-optimal witness for `o` is Quit at date `0` (the alternative is
worse by `lambda/4`), and at endpoint error `1/64` every admissible receiving
witness waits beyond date `1`.  Their
unit payoff difference is paid entirely by the unchanged players: if `k`
Quits at date `1` the terminal coalition contains `k,h`, and otherwise `h`
Quits alone.  The probability that `m` Quits anywhere in the paid interval is
exactly zero.

**Proposition 30 (selected-ray incidence no-go).**  Positive normalized
curvature, the exact paid-witness inequalities, flatness of the selected
tangent column, negligible source excess, zero debt of the full-replacement
mover, strict endpoint total-debt separation, and no tangent entry into an
inactive coordinate do not imply any positive lower bound on mover-involving
absorption in the paid first-disagreement interval.

The missing-cross-ray qualification can also be removed.  Retain the same
table and additionally set, in mover `m`'s coordinate,

```text
r_m({o,m})=-3/4,        r_m({o})=3/4.
```

For every rank let the replacement of active observer `o` be pure Quit at
date `0`.  Along a stopping-law mixture of weight `s` from its prescribed
Quit at date `1` to Quit at date `0`, observer `o` has debt `(1-s)p`; hence
its normalized self direction at `s=lambda` is `-p -> -1/2`.  Against the
same mixture, mover `m`'s pure-Never minus pure-Quit-at-zero gap is

```text
s*(3/4-(-3/4)) + (1-s)*((1-r)-1/2)
  = (3/2)s + (1-s)(1/2-r) > 0.
```

Never remains its cap, so its prescribed debt changes by

```text
p*s*(1+r),
```

and its normalized direction tends to `1/2`.  The `o`-column is therefore
`(-1/2,1/2,0,0)`, also flat, while the previously computed `m`-column is
`(1/4,-1/4,0,0)`.  Both literal full replacements have zero own debt.
The limiting positive-debt support is exactly `{o,m}`; both inactive
directions are zero.

Taking, for example, `lambda_n=1/(n+2)` now supplies every field of a
`QuittingPositiveMinimumDebtTangentFamily` by the displayed literal profiles,
scales, replacements, and tangent limits **except** `base_minimum`.  The base
is in the terminal semantic carrier, has positive total debt `3/4`, the
source semantic pairs converge to it, source total debt is identically
`3/4`, and both replacement-own-debt tolerance inequalities hold with left
side zero.

**Corollary 30A (sole surviving family field).**  The entire paid selected-ray
regression, including compatible rays for every active player, satisfies all
numerical/topological fields of the positive-minimum tangent-family interface
except the assertion that its positive-debt base globally minimizes total
semantic debt over the full carrier.  Therefore any incidence repair using
only the declared tangent-family data must use `base_minimum` essentially;
cross-mover compatibility by itself does not recover mover absorption.

This is not a negative answer to the maintained paid-near-return question:
the table has not been shown to make the limiting base a positive *global*
minimum of semantic debt.  It is a sharp adapter result.  Any theorem deriving
mover incidence from the actual source must use global minimum provenance,
not the normalized-curvature decoder, the full compatible tangent rays, or
the full-replacement mover's small debt alone.  Alternatively the
producer must accept the nonmover absorption and charge the debt created by
the observer's best-response replacement to a different exact floor block.

Declarations inspected for this audit are
`exists_paidFirstDisagreementRow_of_stoppingLawNormalizedCurvature` and
`FullReplacementCluster.normalizedCurvature_tendsto` in
`StoppingLaw/Endpoint/NormalizedCurvaturePaidRow.lean`, the exact one-ray
fields in `QuittingPositiveMinimumDebtTangentFamily` and
`FullReplacementCluster`, and the first-disagreement row fields in
`TerminalSemanticPaidFirstDisagreement.lean`.

Independent falsification review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_30.md`
validates the exact arithmetic and the zero-incidence conclusion.  Its
addendum independently validates Corollary 30A and confirms that
`base_minimum` is the sole missing tangent-family field.

### 31. What the sole surviving field actually buys: rankwise opponent-debt transfer

Write `D(X)` for total terminal semantic debt.  At rank `n`, let

```text
P_n = sourcePair(n),
F_n = fullReplacementPair(m,n),
e_n = D(P_n)-D(base),
G_n = fullReplacementPrescribedGain(m,n).
```

The exact mover identity is

```text
G_n = d_m(P_n)-d_m(F_n).                              (31.1)
```

Global minimality, applied to the literal full-replacement profile, gives
`D(F_n)>=D(base)`, while `D(P_n)=D(base)+e_n`.  Splitting total debt change
into the mover and its opponents and using (31.1) yields

```text
sum_(j!=m) [d_j(F_n)-d_j(P_n)]
  = D(F_n)-D(P_n)+G_n
  >= G_n-e_n.                                         (31.2)
```

This is the exact quantitative consequence unavailable in Corollary 30A.
It uses `base_minimum` once and only once.

Let `d=d_m(base)>0`.  Source convergence gives `d_m(P_n)->d` and `e_n->0`.
The selected replacement bound gives

```text
G_n >= d_m(P_n)/2.
```

Therefore, eventually,

```text
sum_(j!=m) [d_j(F_n)-d_j(P_n)] >= d/4.                (31.3)
```

For example, take ranks for which `d_m(P_n)>=3d/4` and `e_n<=d/8`; then the
right side of (31.2) is at least `3d/8-d/8=d/4`.  Since the paid observer
already certifies that there is at least one opponent, finite pigeonhole gives
a fixed `j!=m` and an infinite subsequence on which

```text
d_j(F_n)-d_j(P_n) >= d/[4*(|I|-1)].                  (31.4)
```

**Proposition 31 (global-minimum opponent-debt transfer).**  Every literal
full reset satisfies the exact rankwise lower bound (31.2).  Along all
sufficiently late ranks this creates the fixed aggregate amount (31.3), and
one fixed opponent receives the fixed share (31.4) along a subsequence.

This still does not construct the requested exact edge.  The debt in (31.2)
is terminal exploitability at an independently supplied behavioral profile,
not a Nash defect of a punishment-floor Bellman root.  The paid first-
disagreement decoder can locate a positive cap-convexity coordinate, but it
does not identify that coordinate with the fixed pigeonhole recipient in
(31.4), an absorbing owner, or the owner of a reached exact Nash row.
Moreover, after best-responding the recipient at the off-minimum endpoint,
global minimality permits the entire decrease to be paid out of the endpoint's
existing total-debt excess; (31.2) cannot simply be iterated.  Thus the next
bridge must convert this one-time source-matched debt transfer into either a
floor-admissible charged block or a new near-minimum source before attempting
recurrence.

`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_31.md`
validates (31.2)--(31.4), including constants and subsequence quantifiers, and

### 32. Exact diagonal-cap regression: a semantic cap can erase a charged floor root

The obvious attempt to bridge Proposition 31 is to discard the prescribed
coordinate of the full semantic pair and use its cap vector as a diagonal,
floor-safe Bellman tail.  This operation can erase all charge even when the
original prescribed vector was already floor-safe and supported a charge-one
exact self-loop.

Use the four players and the Section 30 parameters, and complete the reward
table as follows.  For every nonempty coalition `S`, set

```text
r_o(S)=1  iff  (o,m are both in S)
                 or (o,m are both absent and S meets {h,k});
r_o(S)=0  otherwise.
```

For mover `m`, set

```text
r_m({m})=1/2,
r_m(S)=-3/4   if m,o are both in S,
r_m(S)=-1     if m is in S, o is absent, and S!={m},
r_m({o})=3/4,
r_m({o,h})=1,
r_m(S)=0      for every other S not containing m.
```

Finally, for `i=h,k`, put

```text
r_i(S)=0   if i is absent or o is in S,
r_i(S)=-1  if i is in S and o is absent.
```

All calculations of Sections 30--30A remain unchanged.  In particular, along
the limiting source profile (`lambda=0`), `o,h` Quit at date `1`, `m` mixes
equally between Quit at date `0` and Never, and `k` Never.  Its terminal
semantic pair has

```text
U=(0,3/4,0,0),             B=(1/2,1,0,0).             (32.1)
```

For `h,k`, prescribed play, Never, and joining `o` all pay zero, while an
unaccompanied Quit pays `-1`; hence their cap and debt remain zero throughout
both tangent rays.  Thus this completion preserves Corollary 30A.

The punishment vector of the completed table is zero.  Each player can
guarantee zero by Never: every payoff in a coalition not containing that
player is nonnegative.  Conversely, opponents can make the best attainable
payoff zero (`o,h,k` against all-Never, and `m` against an immediate solo
Quit by `h`).  Hence `U>=P=0`.

At tail `U`, let `q^o` have observer `o` Quit surely and every other player
Continue.  Its current payoff is the singleton row

```text
r({o})=(0,3/4,0,0)=U.
```

Observer `o` is indifferent between Quit and the continuation `U_o=0`;
`m` strictly prefers Continue (`3/4` versus `-3/4`); and `h,k` are indifferent
between Continue and joining `o`, both of which pay zero.  Therefore `q^o` is
an exact endpoint-Nash root, its Bellman successor is `U`, and it is a
charge-one punishment-floor-admissible self-loop.

At the diagonal cap tail `B`, by contrast, the unique exact product-Nash root
is all-Continue.  This follows without a mixed-root calculation:

1. `m` strictly prefers Continue for every opponent Quit set.  Against no
   Quit it compares `1` with `1/2`; against a set containing `o`, its Continue
   payoff is one of `3/4,1,0` while its Quit payoff is `-3/4`; and against a
   nonempty set omitting `o`, it compares `0` with `-1`.
2. With `m` continuing, `o` strictly prefers Continue.  If neither `h` nor
   `k` Quits it compares tail `1/2` with solo `0`; otherwise it compares the
   outsider payoff `1` with joining payoff `0`.
3. With `m,o` continuing, each of `h,k` compares Continue payoff zero with
   Quit payoff `-1`, regardless of the other clock.

Thus dominance elimination forces every marginal to Continue.  The resulting
root has charge zero and successor `B`.

**Proposition 32 (diagonal-cap charge erasure).**  Even for an actual terminal
semantic pair whose prescribed vector dominates the punishment floor, the
replacement `U -> B` can change a charge-one exact floor-admissible self-loop
into a tail whose unique exact Nash root has zero charge.  Therefore cap
diagonalization is not a valid terminal-debt-to-exact-chronology adapter.

This does not obstruct the maintained producer for the displayed game: its
tail `U` already has the desired charged return.  It falsifies only the
proposed universal bridge through `B`.  An actual use of Proposition 31 must
retain prescribed-payoff provenance through the Nashification, or construct
a separate floor-safe route to `U`; knowing only the cap vector and transferred
debt is insufficient.

`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_32.md`
validates the full reward table, the semantic pair and punishment floor, both
tangent rays, the charged self-loop at `U`, and strict dominance elimination
at `B`.  It confirms that the proposition is exactly a cap-diagonalization
adapter no-go, not a negative instance of the maintained near-return target.

### 33. Charge is not lower-semicontinuous under arbitrarily small payoff lifts

The same fixed reward table gives a sharper source-matching obstruction than
the full cap replacement.  For every `epsilon>0`, put

```text
U^epsilon=(epsilon,3/4,0,0).
```

These floor-admissible tails converge to the charged-loop tail `U`, but every
one has only the all-Continue exact product-Nash root.  The dominance proof is
the same as in Section 32 and does not use the size of `epsilon`:

1. `m` strictly prefers Continue against every opponent Quit set.  Against no
   Quit it compares tail `3/4` with solo payoff `1/2`; all other comparisons
   are the strict reward-cell comparisons already listed in Section 32.
2. After eliminating `m`'s Quit action, `o` strictly prefers Continue.  If
   neither `h` nor `k` Quits, Continue gives `epsilon>0` and Quit gives zero;
   if at least one Quits, Continue gives one and joining gives zero.
3. After eliminating `m,o`, each of `h,k` strictly prefers Continue: it gets
   zero whether or not the other inactive player Quits, while joining gives
   `-1`.

Thus iterative strict dominance leaves all-Continue, which is indeed a Nash
root and has zero charge.  At the limit `epsilon=0`, however, the charge-one
root `q^o` of Section 32 reappears because `o` becomes indifferent.

**Proposition 33 (failure of positive-charge lower semicontinuity).**  For one
fixed finite quitting reward table and one floor-admissible payoff `U`, there
is a charge-one exact floor-admissible self-loop at `U`, while a sequence of
floor-admissible tails `U^epsilon -> U` has no positive-charge exact root at
all.  Hence convergence of source payoff vectors—even from within the
punishment-floor box—cannot transport a fixed positive Nash--Bellman charge.

This remains an adapter no-go, not a paid-branch counterexample.  Its exact
use is to sharpen the bridge after Proposition 31: preserving the endpoint
payoff only up to the final tolerance is insufficient for Nashification unless
one also proves a robust face sign or constructs charge elsewhere in the
path.  The payoff-near-return consumer tolerates a seam between path
endpoints, but every internal row must still be an exact root at its literal
tail.

recomputes the floor-safe tails, strict dominance for every `epsilon>0`, and

### 34. Exact source boundary after constrained reset-face minimization

There is a stronger, already named semantic consumer of Proposition 31 than
rankwise pigeonhole.  The full-replacement cluster has exact mover debt zero:
the selected replacement debt is at most the squared scale, and the scale
tends to zero along the compactifying subsequence.  Apply the reset-face
minimization theorem to the global minimum `base`, this cluster `F`, and mover
`m`.  It supplies a carrier point `R` satisfying

```text
d_m(R)=0,
D(base) <= D(R) <= D(F),
sum_(j!=m) [d_j(R)-d_j(base)]
  = D(R)-D(base)+d_m(base) >= d_m(base),             (34.1)
```

and every exact one-row Nash root against the cap vector `B(R)` is the
all-Continue root.  In particular `B(R)` is a literal zero-charge fixed point
of cap prefixing.  This is the exact constrained analogue of the rankwise
identity (31.2), with no asymptotic loss and no need to select a recipient.

There are now only two debt-semantic cases.

1. If `D(R)=D(base)`, then `R` is another global minimum and its positive-debt
   support omits `m`.  This becomes a strict minimum-fiber support-cardinality
   drop only when combined with the separate flat/no-entry cluster hypotheses;
   it is not a consequence of reset minimization alone.
2. If `D(base)<D(R)`, then `R` is an off-minimum reset excursion carrying at
   least the transfer (34.1), but its cap correspondence is maximally rigid:
   it contains only all-Continue and spends no absorption charge.

This does **not** prove a new theorem beyond the named reset-face result.  It
identifies the exact source adapter left after Propositions 31--33.  The paid
first-disagreement row and its fixed behavioral hazard are attached to the
literal full-replacement profiles and their endpoint law.  Constrained
minimization from `F` to `R` retains only the semantic reset and debt account;
it does not retain that endpoint law, first-disagreement witnesses, or a
reached chronological path.  A fixed-law/global-minimizer comparison with an
aligned-or-positive premium is known for the separate rectangle packet, but
no inspected theorem supplies that alternative for an arbitrary paid full-
replacement cluster.

Therefore the live bridge is now exact:

> transport one paid, law-supported positive block from `F` to the constrained
> reset minimizer `R` without losing its fixed aggregate absorption, or first
> prove a paid-source law-retention premium and then make that premium fund a
> floor-admissible excursion returning in payoff.

The first alternative needs a source-matched law-preserving minimizer; the
second needs an exact Nash--Bellman conversion of the scalar premium.  Neither
is supplied by semantic compactness.  Proposition 32 shows that replacing
the missing law by the cap vector cannot solve it, while Proposition 33 shows
that even arbitrarily accurate payoff reprojection cannot transport charge
without a robust root-face sign.

Named sources inspected for this boundary are
`capNashPrefix_resetExcursion_exact_account` and
`exists_resetFace_minimizer_with_unique_allContinue_capNash` in
`TerminalSemanticResetExcursionReturn.lean`.  The fixed-law versus global-
minimizer comparison in `RectangleResetFaceMinimizer.lean` was inspected only
as a rectangle-specific analogue; it is not asserted as an adapter from the
paid source.  No Lean implementation or new checked declaration is claimed
here.

Independent review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_34.md`
validates `d_m(F)=0`, (34.1), the debt bounds, and the unique all-Continue cap
root, and requires exactly the two scope qualifications now stated above.

### 35. Semantic pair plus terminal law still does not transport the paid row

The provenance loss in Section 34 is stronger than loss of the terminal
coalition distribution.  Even retaining both the complete terminal semantic
pair and that distribution does not retain an ordered first-disagreement
witness.

Take two players `o,m` and set

```text
r_o({o})=1,   r_o({m})=r_o({o,m})=0,
r_m(S)=0      for every nonempty S.
```

Let `sigma^1` prescribe `o=Never` and `m=Quit at date 1`; let `sigma^2`
prescribe `o=Never` and `m=Quit at date 2`.  Both profiles have exactly the
same terminal coalition law, the point mass on `{m}`, and exactly the same
semantic pair

```text
U=(0,0),             B=(1,0).                       (35.1)
```

Indeed `o` earns zero as prescribed and can obtain one by quitting strictly
before `m`, while every payoff of `m` is zero.

Now keep the ordered observer witnesses `s=0,t=1`.  Against `sigma^1`'s
opponents, quitting at `0` gives the solo reward one and quitting at `1`
collides with `m` for reward zero.  Their payoff difference is one.  Against
`sigma^2`'s opponents, both dates `0` and `1` precede `m` and both give the
solo reward one, so the same ordered-witness difference is zero.

**Proposition 35 (terminal-law insufficiency for paid-witness transport).**
Equality of terminal semantic pairs and equality of complete terminal
coalition laws do not preserve the gain of a fixed ordered pair of pure Quit
times.  The stopping-time placement hidden behind the terminal law is
essential paid-row data.

This proposition does not say that `sigma^2` has no paid row: dates `0` and
`2` again give a unit difference.  It proves the narrower and needed adapter
statement.  A reset-face minimizer retaining only semantic/law data cannot
inherit the original paid witnesses or their live-mass interval.  To use the
paid block after Section 34, one must retain the actual opponents' stopping
law through minimization, or independently re-extract a new executable paid
row at the selected minimizer.  Neither follows from equality of `(U,B)` and
the terminal outcome distribution.

validates (35.1), the common terminal law, and the gains one versus zero for

### 36. A generic executable paid-port minimizer

The rectangle-specific law-premium theorem cannot be imported into the paid
branch, but a narrower generic premium is available directly from the paid
row.  Fix an actual receiving profile `sigma`, observer `o`, and two observer
pure stopping strategies `a,b` such that, against the fixed opponents
`sigma_{-o}`,

```text
payoff_o(b;sigma_{-o})-payoff_o(a;sigma_{-o}) >= g > 0.  (36.1)
```

For `t in [0,1]`, let `sigma_t` keep every opponent strategy fixed and let
`o` use the ex-ante stopping-law mixture `(1-t)a+t b`.  This mixture is an
ordinary executable behavioral stopping law.  Convexity here is in the
complete stopping-law representation; the hazard sequence implementing the
mixture need not be pointwise affine.  Write

```text
U_i(t)=prescribed terminal payoff at sigma_t,
B_i(t)=unrestricted unilateral terminal cap at sigma_t,
d_i(t)=B_i(t)-U_i(t),
D(t)=sum_i d_i(t).
```

For the observer, unilateral replacement erases its prescribed strategy, so
`B_o(t)` is constant.  Prescribed payoff is affine, and (36.1) gives

```text
d_o(t)-d_o(s) <= -(t-s)g        whenever s<=t.       (36.2)
```

With equality when the witness difference in (36.1) is exactly `g`.

For every `j!=o`, the payoff of each fixed deviation of `j` is affine in the
observer's ex-ante mixture.  The cap `B_j(t)`, being the supremum of those
affine functions, is convex.  It is also Lipschitz: changing the mixture
weight by `|t-s|` changes any fixed deviation payoff by at most
`2M|t-s|`, uniformly over the deviation, where `M` bounds absolute rewards.
The prescribed payoff is affine and has the same bound.  Hence each `d_j`,
and therefore `D`, is finite, continuous, and convex on `[0,1]`.

It follows that `D` attains a minimum at some `tStar`.  Let `DStar` be the
global minimum of total semantic debt over the full carrier and put

```text
pi = D(tStar)-DStar >= 0.                            (36.3)
```

Every profile `sigma_t` has exactly the same opponent stopping laws for `o`.
Consequently the two witness payoffs, their chronology, opponent live mass,
reached gain, and paid edge identity are independent of `t`: the original
paid row remains executable at the selected profile `sigma_tStar`.

**Proposition 36 (executable paid-port premium dichotomy).**  Every actual
paid row lies on a compact convex one-dimensional family of actual profiles
on which the row is literally retained and total terminal debt is convex.
At its total-debt minimizer, either

```text
pi=0,
```

so an actual paid profile lies on the global minimum fiber, or `pi>0`, giving
a genuine positive debt premium for retaining this fixed executable opponent
environment.  Moreover, for every `s>=tStar`, minimality and (36.2) give the
opponent compensation bound

```text
sum_(j!=o) [d_j(s)-d_j(tStar)] >= (s-tStar)g.        (36.4)
```

If (36.1) is strict beyond `g`, the right side can be replaced by the actual
witness difference times `s-tStar`.

The proof of (36.4) is the exact split

```text
0 <= D(s)-D(tStar)
   = d_o(s)-d_o(tStar)
       + sum_(j!=o)[d_j(s)-d_j(tStar)].
```

This is ordinary mathematics, not a checked declaration.  It is narrower
than a fixed-terminal-law minimizer but stronger in the datum that matters:
the complete opponents' stopping laws and the paid row are executable at
every point of the port.

Two limitations are essential.  First, varying `o` can change the reset
mover's semantic debt, even though the mover's prescribed stopping strategy
is held fixed; no double-reset conclusion follows.  Second, if `tStar=1`,
(36.4) is vacuous, and the scalar premium `pi>0` is not itself a Nash--Bellman
edge or a floor path.  The next exact question is whether the actual
normalized-curvature construction forces either `tStar<1` by a fixed amount,
or a second paid/reset port leaving the endpoint `tStar=1`.  Without one of
those statements Proposition 36 does not produce the maintained near-return.

Independent falsification review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_36.md`
validates executability, the observer debt identity, outsider cap convexity
and uniform Lipschitz control, minimizer/premium dichotomy, literal row
retention, and (36.4).  It supplies the stopping-law-versus-hazard wording
clarification incorporated above and confirms the two stated limitations.

### 37. Exact prefix transport of a paid row, and the true cap seam

An actual chronological prefix does not itself destroy the paid port.  Let
`sigma` carry a paid row for observer `o` with witnesses `s,t` and paid gain
`g>0`.  Prepend one independent product root `q`, and after joint Continue let
the actual continuation be `sigma`.  Modify both observer witnesses to
Continue at the new date zero and then use their old stopping time one date
later (with `Never` remaining `Never`).

The two deviations are identical at the new first stage.  Their payoff
difference is therefore paid only on the event that every opponent of `o`
Continues at that stage.  Writing

```text
O_o(q)=product_(j!=o)(1-q_j),
```

the exact identity is

```text
new witness difference = O_o(q) * old witness difference. (37.1)
```

Equivalently, the new live mass is `O_o(q)` times the old live mass, the
conditional reached gain is unchanged, and the paid edge identity scales by
the same factor.  If `O_o(q)>0`, the prefixed profile therefore carries the
same ordered paid row with positive gain `O_o(q)g`.

Iterating actual roots `q_0,...,q_(N-1)` lets one retain the guaranteed paid
threshold

```text
g_N = g * product_(k<N) O_o(q_k).                   (37.2)
```

If no stage has an opponent who Quits surely and the cumulative total
absorption charge is finite, the infinite product in (37.2) is positive.
Indeed the opponent-absorption mass `1-O_o(q_k)` is at most `Q(q_k)`, so its
sum is finite; absence of an opponent-sure factor makes every `O_o(q_k)`
strictly positive, and the standard finite-sum/infinite-product criterion
gives `product_k O_o(q_k)>0`.  This formulation also covers a stage where the
observer itself Quits surely: total survival then vanishes, but the paid row
still survives whenever all opponents have positive Continue probability.
If instead an opponent is sure to Quit at some stage, that stage is already a
charge-one edge, although no payoff return follows from it alone.

**Proposition 37 (paid-row prefix transport).**  Paid first-disagreement data
is stable under literal reached prefixing, with the exact opponent-survival
factor (37.1).  Along an infinite exact actual-prefix orbit of finite
cumulative charge it either retains a positive limiting guaranteed gain, or
an opponent-sure charge-one stage occurs.

The proposition is conditional on the prefix being the **actual semantic
prefix of `sigma`**.  This exposes the precise unresolved seam.  Prefixing
`sigma` evaluates the root at continuation payoff `U(sigma)`.  The cap-Nash
reset return of Section 34 instead evaluates an exact root at `B(sigma)`,
which is floor-safe but generally differs from `U(sigma)`.  Its Bellman
successor is therefore not the prescribed payoff of the actual prefixed
profile, so (37.1) cannot simply be attached to that floor edge.  Proposition
32 shows that moving the root from `U` to `B` can erase all charge.

Consequently a sufficient next source datum is now very concrete: an actual
paid-port profile with prescribed payoff already above punishment, together
with a positive exact Nash root at that literal prescribed payoff.  Such a
root gives a genuine floor edge, its actual semantic prefix retains the paid
row by (37.1), and further exact roots can be concatenated without a cap seam.
Neither the paid-row interface nor the reset-face premium supplies these two
facts; all-Continue can still be the only exact root at the prescribed payoff
as in Section 27.

Independent falsification review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_37.md`
validates the exact survival factor including ties, `Never`, observer-sure and
opponent-sure boundaries, and the infinite-product argument.  It emphasizes
the scope already used here: a sure-Quit stage is an exact charged edge only
when the prefixed roots are themselves exact Nash roots; probabilistic row
transport alone does not Nashify them.

### 38. Exact cap-to-prescribed root transport: the debt-survival toll

The remaining `U/B` seam has an exact coordinatewise form.  Let `(U,B)` be a
terminal semantic pair, put

```text
d_i = B_i-U_i >= 0,
p_i = Pr_q(i Quits),
O_i(q) = product_(j!=i)(1-p_j),
C(q) = product_j(1-p_j),
Delta_i(v,q) = QuitPayoff_i(q)-ContinuePayoff_i(v,q).
```

The forced-Quit payoff does not depend on player `i`'s tail coordinate,
whereas the forced-Continue payoff sees that coordinate exactly on the event
that all opponents Continue.  Consequently

```text
Delta_i(U,q) = Delta_i(B,q) + O_i(q)d_i.          (38.1)
```

This is the specialization of the named exact identity
`quittingRootEndpointDifference_tail_add` in
`Quitting/Cycles/OwnShiftCycleExactification.lean`.  The corresponding
successor identity is

```text
Succ(B,q)_i-Succ(U,q)_i = C(q)d_i,                (38.2)
```

which is `quittingRootSuccessorPayoff_sub_eq_continueMass_mul` from
`Quitting/Cycles/PeriodicCompiler.lean`.  Thus the endpoint seam is governed
by deleted-player survival `O_i`, while the Bellman payoff seam is governed
by joint survival `C`; these denominators must not be conflated with one
another or with total block hazard.

Assume now that `q` is an exact endpoint-Nash root at the cap tail `B`.
Binary-action complementarity and (38.1) give the following exact
characterization of whether the **same** root is exact at `U`:

```text
p_i=1:       automatic;
0<p_i<1:     O_i(q)d_i=0;
p_i=0:       -Delta_i(B,q) >= O_i(q)d_i.          (38.3)
```

Indeed, at `B` a mixed coordinate has `Delta_i(B,q)=0`, a pure continuer has
`Delta_i(B,q)<=0`, and a pure quitter has `Delta_i(B,q)>=0`.  Lowering the
tail raises the gap by the nonnegative quantity in (38.1).  Hence a pure
quitter remains optimal; a mixed coordinate remains indifferent exactly
when its toll vanishes; and a pure continuer remains optimal exactly when
its old slack pays the toll.  The support implications used here are also
available as the named theorems
`quitProbability_eq_one_of_positive_endpointDifference_of_isZeroNash`,
`quitProbability_eq_zero_of_negative_endpointDifference_of_isZeroNash`,
`sureQuit_or_nonpositive_endpointDifference_of_isZeroNash`, and
`sureContinue_or_nonnegative_endpointDifference_of_isZeroNash` in
`Quitting/Boundary/Repair/SupportEnlargementAlternative.lean`.

**Proposition 38 (exact cap-to-prescribed support criterion).**  For an exact
cap--Nash root `q`, (38.3) is necessary and sufficient for `q` to remain an
exact endpoint-Nash root at the prescribed tail `U`.

Two consequences sharply narrow the paid source problem.

1. If some player `k` Quits surely, then `q` automatically transports.  For
   every `i!=k`, `O_i(q)=0`; for `k`, pure Quit remains optimal.  Moreover
   `C(q)=0`, so (38.2) says that the cap-tail and prescribed-tail successor
   payoffs are identical.  If `U` dominates the punishment floor, the current
   successor automatically does too: this is the ordinary minmax argument
   packaged by
   `quittingPunishmentValue_le_rootSuccessorPayoff_of_tail_ge` and
   `QuittingPunishmentFloorAdmissibleEdge.ofExactEdge` in
   `Quitting/Bellman/Finite/PunishmentFloorAdmissibleChargedRelation.lean`.
   Thus `q` is a literal charge-one floor-admissible edge at `U`.  This is the
   exact positive seam closure; a floor-safe prescribed tail and sure-Quit
   cap row still have to be produced simultaneously from the paid source.

2. If no player Quits surely, then every `O_i(q)>0`.  Every active coordinate
   has `0<p_i<1`, so (38.3) forces `d_i=0`.  Hence a positive transported root
   without sure absorption can be active only on the zero-debt face; every
   positive-debt player must Continue purely with cap slack at least
   `O_i(q)d_i`.  At the constrained reset face this makes the reset mover's
   zero debt structurally relevant, but Section 34 supplies neither an active
   root on that face nor the required inactive-coordinate slacks.

The mixed-coordinate obstruction is sharp even before any paid provenance is
added.  In a two-player table, give player `i` payoff `1` whenever `i` Quits,
give the other player payoff `0`, and take `B=(1,0)`, `U=(0,0)`.  With the
other player continuing, any strictly mixed probability of `i` is an exact
root at `B`; at `U`, Quit pays `1` and Continue pays `0`, so that same mixture
is not Nash.  Here `O_i d_i=1`, exactly the toll in (38.1).  Replacing the
mixture by pure Quit repairs the seam, illustrating the sure-absorption arm
rather than contradicting it.

This proposition does not construct the requested excursion.  It replaces
the vague demand to “Nashify at `U`” by two source-level alternatives:

```text
(a) produce a cap-Nash root with a sure quitter and the needed floor bounds;
or
(b) produce a positive cap-Nash root supported on the zero-debt face, with
    every inactive positive-debt coordinate carrying the exact slack (38.3).
```

The paid-port minimizer, terminal-debt transfer, and reset-face minimizer do
not presently imply either alternative.  Conversely, any no-go must rule
out both, rather than merely exhibit a discontinuity of positive root charge
as in Section 33.

For the specific reset-face point `R` of Section 34, the named source theorem
already says that the cap tail `B(R)` has only the all-Continue Nash root.
Therefore neither positive arm above is available **at that cap tail**; the
criterion does not covertly repair the reset minimizer.  It shows instead
that any successful bridge must obtain a different prescribed-tail root,
leave the reset point, or add source information strong enough to contradict
that unique-cap-root conclusion.  This is why the remaining issue is an
exact excursion/re-entry problem rather than probabilistic port retention.

validates both tail identities, all three pure/mixed support cases, the
sure-Quit transport and common-successor conclusion, the non-sure
active-support zero-debt consequence, and the sharp two-player regression.
It confirms the exact scope: the floor-safe prescribed tail and production
of either root arm remain separate source obligations.

#### 38A. The prescribed-tail singleton arm is already dispatched

There is one further source-level narrowing, but it is not a new theorem.
The checked declaration
`exists_isZeroQuittingRootEndpointNash_simplex_with_positive_absorption_of_singleton_gap`
in `Quitting/Boundary/Repair/FixedTailPositiveAbsorptionRoot.lean` says that
for any tail `U`,

```text
U_i < r({i})_i for some i
```

produces an exact endpoint-Nash root at `U` with positive absorption.  If
`U>=P`, the floor-edge constructor cited above makes its successor floor-safe
as well.  Thus at the reset point `R` the literal prescribed-tail root problem
has only the following unresolved arms:

```text
(i)  U(R) is not punishment-floor admissible; or
(ii) r({i})_i <= U(R)_i for every i.                 (38.4)
```

In arm (ii), all-Continue is itself exact at `U(R)` by
`isZeroQuittingRootNash_allContinue_iff_singleton_le`, although additional
positive roots are not excluded.  Any positive semantic debt at `R` is then
genuinely non-instant: its cap is attained or approximated by a later-time
behavioral deviation, not by quitting alone immediately.  In particular,
the aggregate opponent transfer (34.1) does not force a singleton-gap edge.
Section 27 is an exact warning that (ii) can coexist with a unit paid
first-disagreement gain and still leave all-Continue as the only root at the
floor tail.

The fixed-threshold part is also already quantitative.  If rewards have
absolute value at most `M` and, uniformly along a family of floor tails,

```text
U_i <= r({i})_i-delta,       delta>0,
```

then
`gap_div_le_quittingRootAbsorptionMass_of_isZeroEndpointNash` in
`Quitting/Boundary/Repair/FixedTailUniformAbsorption.lean` gives **every**
exact root at that tail charge at least

```text
c=delta/(delta+2M)>0.                                 (38.5)
```

Thus a uniform singleton gap does not merely produce some unquantified
positive edge: it supplies the fixed charge threshold required by the paid
near-return consumer.  What it still does not supply is a path from that
edge's current payoff back near its tail payoff.

Even the dispatched singleton arm supplies only one charged outgoing edge;
it does not return its Bellman current payoff to `U(R)`.  Therefore it is a
necessary branch reduction, not the requested payoff near-return.  The
remaining producer must either repair the floor mismatch in (i), or turn the
non-instant behavioral cap debt in (ii) into a recurrent exact excursion.

### 39. Exact upward-tail and prescribed-to-floor transport: the dual toll

The dual support characterization holds for every coordinatewise upward tail
move.  Let `V>=U`, put `e_i=V_i-U_i>=0`, and suppose `q` is an exact
endpoint-Nash root at `U`.  For the below-floor arm in (38.4), the intended
specialization is

```text
V_i=max(U_i,P_i).
```

The same tail identity reads

```text
Delta_i(V,q)=Delta_i(U,q)-O_i(q)e_i,                 (39.1)
Succ(V,q)_i-Succ(U,q)_i=C(q)e_i.                    (39.2)
```

Thus the same root is exact at the floor clip `V` exactly under

```text
p_i=0:       automatic;
0<p_i<1:     O_i(q)e_i=0;
p_i=1:       Delta_i(U,q) >= O_i(q)e_i.              (39.3)
```

The proof is the same three support cases as Proposition 38 with the sign
reversed.  Raising a tail helps a pure continuer; it breaks a mixed
indifference unless the displayed toll vanishes; and a pure quitter must pay
the lift out of its old strict Quit advantage.

**Proposition 39 (exact upward-tail support criterion).**  Conditions (39.3)
are necessary and sufficient for an exact `U`-root to survive any
coordinatewise lift `U -> V`.  For the floor clip `V=U vee P`, if no player
Quits surely, every active player must already satisfy `U_i>=P_i`; deficient
coordinates may occur only among pure continuers.  If `k` Quits surely, all
coordinates `i!=k` transport automatically, and the only remaining floor-
clip inequality is

```text
Delta_k(U,q) >= O_k(q)(P_k-U_k)_+ .                 (39.4)
```

In particular, a sure quitter with `U_k>=P_k` transports automatically.
Then `C(q)=0`, so its successor payoff is unchanged by clipping, and the
transported root is a literal charge-one floor-admissible edge.

Section 33 is the sharp arbitrary-lift boundary example for the pure-Quit
inequality underlying (39.4).  Its charge-one root has sure quitter `o`, but
`Delta_o(U,q)=0` and raising only `U_o` by any `epsilon>0` gives
`O_o e_o=epsilon`; the same root immediately ceases to be Nash.  In that
completed table strict dominance additionally removes every other positive
root after the lift.  That lift is **not** a floor clip—the original `U`
already equals the floor in the moved coordinate.  It nevertheless proves
that no generic small-lift or sure-absorption argument can omit the sure
quitter's own-coordinate slack.  In the actual below-floor specialization,
the corresponding quantity is exactly the buffer (39.4).

Together, Propositions 38--39 reduce the semantic Nashification seam to
support incidence and two explicit scalar tolls.  A source-matched positive
answer may now target any one of:

```text
- a sure cap quitter with floor-safe prescribed coordinate;
- a non-sure cap root active only on zero semantic debt, with (38.3) slack;
- an exact prescribed-tail root active only on already floor-safe players;
- a sure prescribed-tail quitter satisfying the strict buffer (39.4).
```

No inspected paid-source field forces one of these alternatives.  A sharp
negative source theorem would have to exclude all four while retaining the
full separated frontier, not merely repeat the local charge discontinuity.
