validates (39.1)--(39.3), the arbitrary-lift and literal-floor-clip scopes,
the sure-Quit buffer (39.4), automatic predecessor-floor bound, and the exact
qualification of Section 33 as an arbitrary-lift rather than floor-clip
regression.

### 40. Sure-Quit edges self-close except for one collision premium

The sure-Quit arms of Sections 38--39 admit an exact payoff re-entry test.
Let `U` be a floor-admissible tail, let `q` be an exact endpoint-Nash root at
`U`, and assume some player Quits surely.  Put

```text
X=Succ(U,q),
O_i=product_(j!=i)(1-p_j),
Q_i=QuitPayoff_i(q,0),
K_i=ContinuePayoff_i(q,0).
```

The symbol `K_i` is the forced-Continue absorbing contribution at zero tail;
it is not an absorption charge.  Forced Quit is tail-independent, while

```text
ContinuePayoff_i(q,v)=K_i+O_i v_i.                  (40.1)
```

Joint Continue mass is zero, so the successor is independent of the tail:

```text
Succ(X,q)=Succ(U,q)=X.                               (40.2)
```

It remains only to decide whether `q` is endpoint Nash at `X`.

If `i` is not a sure quitter, its opponent set contains a sure quitter, so
`O_i=0`; its endpoint gap is tail-independent and its Nash inequality at `U`
transports automatically.  If at least two players Quit surely, the same is
true for every sure quitter because each sees another sure quitter among its
opponents.  Therefore any exact root with at least two sure quitters is
automatically exact at `X`.

Suppose instead that `k` is the **unique** sure quitter.  Every other
coordinate still transports automatically.  Since `q_k` is pure Quit,

```text
X_k=Q_k.
```

At the successor tail its exact Quit-minus-Continue gap is consequently

```text
Delta_k(X,q)
 = Q_k-(K_k+O_k X_k)
 = (1-O_k)Q_k-K_k.                                  (40.3)
```

Thus `q` is exact at `X` if and only if

```text
K_k <= (1-O_k)Q_k.                                  (40.4)
```

**Proposition 40 (sure-root self-closure).**  Under the displayed hypotheses,
if there are at least two sure quitters, or if the unique sure quitter
satisfies (40.4), then `(X,q)` is a charge-one exact self-loop.  Moreover `X`
is floor-admissible: it is the exact Nash predecessor of the floor tail `U`,
so the punishment-floor predecessor bound applies.  Hence this is already a
positive admissible return and answers the payoff-near-return question with
zero endpoint error.

For a literal solo root all outsiders Continue, so `O_k=1` and `K_k=0`;
(40.4) holds with equality.  This recovers the familiar fact that an exact
floor-safe solo edge lands on its singleton-payoff self-loop.  The argument
also shows why simultaneous pure coalitions of size at least two self-close
without any reward sign assumption once their root is exact at one floor
tail.

If (40.4) fails, the failure is itself exact:

```text
ContinuePayoff_k(q,X)-QuitPayoff_k(q,X)
 = K_k-(1-O_k)Q_k > 0.                              (40.5)
```

So the unique sure quitter strictly refuses to repeat the root at its own
successor because the opponent-collision payoff earned while it Continues
exceeds the scaled payoff from quitting.  This is a genuine collision premium,
not a tail/provenance mismatch.  Any remaining sure-root paid producer must
either fund this scalar premium by a later exact edge or change the outsider
root so that (40.4) is restored.

The obstruction occurs at a literal floor edge.  Take two players `k,j` and
give `j` payoff zero at every terminal.  For player `k`, set

```text
r_k({k})=0,       r_k({j})=3/4,       r_k({k,j})=1.   (40.6)
```

The punishment vector is `(0,0)`: all rewards are nonnegative, while if `j`
Never Quits then `k` can obtain at most zero, and `j` always obtains zero.
At tail `U=(0,0)`, let `k` Quit surely and let `j` Quit with probability
`1/2`.  Player `j` is indifferent.  For `k`,

```text
Q_k=1/2,
K_k=3/8,
O_k=1/2,
ContinuePayoff_k(q,U)=3/8 < 1/2=Q_k.
```

Thus `q` is an exact charge-one root at the floor tail, with successor
`X=(1/2,0)`.  But

```text
K_k-(1-O_k)Q_k=3/8-1/4=1/8,                         (40.7)
```

so at `X` the sure quitter gains exactly `1/8` by Continue and the root does
not self-close.  This does not refute re-entry for the table: raising `j`'s
Quit probability to `3/4` gives the higher self-loop tail `(3/4,0)`.  It
shows instead that (40.4) is a genuine scalar obstruction and suggests the
remaining nonlocal move—outsider-hazard adjustment—without proving that the
paid source can perform it while retaining exact Nash for every player.

This result narrows but does not close the uniform singleton-gap arm: the
named fixed-tail theorem may return a wholly mixed positive root rather than
a sure-Quit root, and a unique-sure collision root may violate (40.4).
Those are the two remaining re-entry cases after the exact support transport
criteria.

### 41. Exact normalized-delivery criterion: a wholly mixed root cannot repair itself by repetition

The most direct proposed repair for the wholly mixed arm is to repeat the same
positive product root until its Bellman payoff converges to the root's
stationary absorbing delivery.  There is an exact support criterion, and it
shows that this repair gives nothing new in the wholly mixed case.

Let `q` be an exact endpoint-Nash root at a floor tail `U`, and suppose its
absorption charge is positive.  Write

```text
C=product_i(1-p_i)<1,
A=Succ(0,q),
Z=A/(1-C),
O_i=product_(j!=i)(1-p_j),
Delta_i(v)=QuitPayoff_i(q,0)-ContinuePayoff_i(q,v).
```

Thus `Z` is the full collision-aware absorbing delivery of `q`.  The affine
Bellman identities are

```text
Succ(v,q)=A+C*v,
Delta_i(Z)=Delta_i(U)-O_i*(Z_i-U_i).                (41.1)
```

In particular `Succ(Z,q)=Z`.  Exact endpoint Nash at `Z` is equivalent,
coordinate by coordinate, to

```text
p_i=0:        Delta_i(U) <= O_i*(Z_i-U_i),
0<p_i<1:      O_i*(Z_i-U_i)=0,
p_i=1:        Delta_i(U) >= O_i*(Z_i-U_i).          (41.2)
```

These conditions include the slack already present at `U`; no sign of
`Z_i-U_i` is assumed.

**Proposition 41 (fixed-root normalized re-entry criterion).**  Conditions
`(41.2)` are necessary and sufficient for the same root `q` to give a
positive exact self-loop at `Z`.  If they hold, `Z` is punishment-floor
admissible as well.

**Proof.**  Equation `(41.1)` and the three endpoint support conditions give
the equivalence directly.  For the floor statement, if `q` is exact at both
`U` and `Z`, its endpoint gaps are affine, so it is exact at every point of
the segment `[U,Z]`.  Define

```text
U_n=Z+C^n*(U-Z).
```

Then `U_0=U` and `U_(n+1)=Succ(U_n,q)`.  Starting from the floor tail `U`,
the exact punishment-floor predecessor bound places every `U_n` above the
floor.  Since `U_n -> Z` and the floor box is closed, `Z` is above the floor.
Finally `Succ(Z,q)=Z` and the edge charge is `1-C>0`. QED.

If `q` is wholly mixed, then every `O_i>0` and every coordinate uses the
middle line of `(41.2)`.  Hence `Z=U`.  But then

```text
Succ(U,q)=C*U+(1-C)*Z=U.                            (41.3)
```

So a wholly mixed root can be normalized into a fixed-root return **only if
the original edge was already a payoff self-loop**.  Repeating a moving
wholly mixed root cannot be the missing paid re-entry mechanism; at least one
product root must change.

This obstruction is sharp at a floor tail.  In a symmetric two-player table,
give each player `i` rewards

```text
r_i({i})=0,       r_i({j})=3,       r_i({i,j})=3.   (41.4)
```

All rewards are nonnegative, while an opponent who Never Quits holds the
player's best payoff to zero; hence the punishment floor is `(0,0)`.  At
`U=(0,0)`, the root `p_1=p_2=1/2` is exact and wholly mixed: forced Quit and
forced Continue both pay `3/2`.  Its charge is `3/4`, its first successor is
`(3/2,3/2)`, and its normalized delivery is `Z=(2,2)`.  At `Z`, forced
Continue pays `5/2` while forced Quit still pays `3/2`, so the root is not
exact there.  Indeed it already ceases to be exact at its first successor,
where Continue pays `9/4`.  The table may have other equilibrium mechanisms;
it falsifies only fixed-root normalization as a re-entry principle.

The affine delivery identity used here is the ordinary mathematical content
of
`quittingRootSuccessorPayoff_eq_survival_mul_add_absorption_mul_delivery` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Cycles/CollisionAwareFiniteReturn.lean`.
That file packages a *supplied* collision-aware finite return, and
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Stationary/EndpointCompiler.lean` verifies a
supplied stationary fixed point.  Neither inspected declaration produces the
support transport `(41.2)` from an arbitrary paid-source root.  Proposition
41 is therefore a reduction/no-go, not a new stationary producer.

Independent falsification review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_41.md`
validates the affine delivery formula, all three support cases, the segment
floor induction, the wholly mixed consequence, and the rational regression.
Its scope is exactly fixed-root normalization; changing-root re-entry remains
open.

### 42. Outsider Nash reselection need not repair the unique-sure premium

Section 40's two-player regression could be repaired by changing the sole
outsider's hazard while keeping the sure quitter fixed.  That is not a
universal mechanism: with two outsiders the induced absorbed game can have a
unique mixed Nash profile at which the collision premium is strictly
positive.

Take players `k,a,b`.  All unspecified rewards below are zero.  Set the
otherwise unused singleton payoff

```text
r_a({a})=1/2.                                        (42.0)
```

Give player `k` the rewards

```text
r_k({k})=0,
r_k(S)=4/3     if k in S and S != {k},
r_k(S)=6/5     if k notin S.                         (42.1)
```

On coalitions containing `k`, give `a` the matching payoff and `b` the
mismatching payoff:

```text
S                 {k}   {k,a}   {k,b}   {k,a,b}
r_a(S)              1      0       0        1
r_b(S)              0      1       1        0.       (42.2)
```

Every terminal reward is nonnegative.  Each punishment value is nevertheless
zero.  Opponents who all Never hold `k` to its zero singleton/terminal payoff.
To punish `a`, let `k` Never and `b` Quit surely at date zero: whether `a`
Continues or Quits, the terminal coalition omits `k` and its payoff is zero.
The symmetric profile with `a` sure and `k` Never holds `b` to zero.  Hence
the punishment floor is exactly `(0,0,0)`.  Notice that `(42.0)` gives the
literal floor-tail singleton gap `U_a<r_a({a})`; all-Never is no longer an
equilibrium.

At the floor tail `U=0`, consider

```text
p_k=1,       p_a=p_b=1/2.                            (42.3)
```

Conditional on `k` quitting surely, the outsiders play matching pennies.
For `a`, Quit and Continue pay respectively `p_b` and `1-p_b`; for `b`, they
pay `1-p_a` and `p_a`.  Their unique Nash profile is therefore exactly
`p_a=p_b=1/2`, and each receives `1/2`.

For `k`, forced Quit pays

```text
Q_k=(3/4)*(4/3)=1,
```

whereas its zero-tail forced-Continue contribution is

```text
K_k=(3/4)*(6/5)=9/10.
```

Thus `(42.3)` is an exact charge-one root at the punishment floor.  But
`O_k=1/4`, so its Section 40 premium is

```text
K_k-(1-O_k)Q_k=9/10-3/4=3/20>0.                    (42.4)
```

The successor is `X=(1,1/2,1/2)`.  At that tail, `k` obtains
`9/10+(1/4)*1=23/20` by Continue instead of `1` by Quit, exactly the gain
`3/20`.

**Proposition 42 (rigid outsider-completion regression).**  The table
`(42.0)--(42.2)` has a floor-safe exact unique-sure root of charge one, a
strict singleton gap at its floor tail, and
strict collision premium `3/20`.  Among product profiles that keep `k` sure
and make every outsider a best response, `(42.3)` is the unique profile.
Consequently no reselection of an outsider Nash completion while retaining
the sure quitter can repair the root into its successor self-loop.

This does not say that the table lacks a different equilibrium or an exact
return.  It rules out only the proposed universal correction that freezes the
sure paid owner and reselects an arbitrary Nash completion on its absorbed
outsider face.  A source-independent repair of `(40.5)` must sometimes
desaturate the sure quitter, introduce a later root, or leave that face.

Independent falsification review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_42.md`
validates the zero punishment floor despite `(42.0)`, uniqueness of the
matching-pennies outsider completion, every exact fraction in `(42.3)--(42.4)`,
and the stated frozen-owner scope.

### 43. Exact conditional-tail desaturation with a fixed half-charge

The positive premium itself supplies an exact tail at which the sure owner can
be desaturated.  The only new obstruction is whether every already-mixed
outsider's conditional indifference tail lies above punishment.

Continue with the unique sure quitter `k` of Section 40.  Let `U` be the
floor tail, let `q` be exact at `U`, and suppose

```text
mu=K_k-(1-O_k)Q_k>0.                                (43.1)
```

Because `k` is the unique sure quitter, `O_k>0`.  Define the switch tail

```text
H_k=(Q_k-K_k)/O_k=Q_k-mu/O_k,
H_i=U_i  (i!=k).                                    (43.2)
```

Exactness at `U` and `(43.1)` give

```text
U_k<=H_k<Q_k=X_k.                                   (43.3)
```

Thus `H` is above punishment.  At `H`, player `k` is exactly indifferent.
Every outsider's gap is unchanged because it faces the sure quitter, so `q`
is still an exact charge-one root at `H`, with the same successor `X`.

Now condition instead on `k` **Continuing**.  For each outsider `i`, keep all
other outsider probabilities equal to `q` and put

```text
R_i=product_(j!=i,k)(1-p_j)>0.
```

Let `Q_i^C` be `i`'s forced-Quit payoff in this conditional row, and let
`K_i^C` be its forced-Continue absorbing contribution at zero tail.  Its
conditional endpoint gap at tail coordinate `v_i` is

```text
Q_i^C-K_i^C-R_i*v_i.
```

Define the unique conditional indifference threshold

```text
T_i=(Q_i^C-K_i^C)/R_i.                              (43.4)
```

Assume only that

```text
T_i>=P_i  for every outsider with 0<p_i<1,          (43.5)
```

where `P` is the punishment floor.  Construct a new floor tail `V` by

```text
V_k=H_k,
V_i=T_i              if 0<p_i<1,
V_i=max(P_i,T_i)     if p_i=0.                      (43.6)
```

For `s in (0,1)`, let `q^s` replace only `k`'s sure Quit probability by `s`.

**Proposition 43 (conditional-tail desaturation).**  Under `(43.1)` and
`(43.5)`, `q^s` is an exact endpoint-Nash root at `V` for every `s in (0,1)`.
Its charge is

```text
1-(1-s)O_k >= s.                                    (43.7)
```

In particular `s=1/2` gives an exact floor-admissible edge of charge at least
`1/2`, with `k` genuinely mixed.

**Proof.**  Player `k`'s forced-action values depend only on the unchanged
outsider profile.  Equation `(43.2)` makes them equal, so every mixture `s`
is optimal.  For an outsider, condition on `k`'s action.  When `k` Quits, its
endpoint gap is the original gap: it is zero at a mixed coordinate and
nonpositive at a pure-Continue coordinate.  When `k` Continues, `(43.4)--
(43.6)` makes the gap zero at a mixed coordinate and nonpositive at a pure-
Continue coordinate.  The new gap is the convex combination with weights
`s,1-s`, hence has exactly the required support sign.  This proves endpoint
Nash.  Formula `(43.7)` is the complement of joint Continue probability
`(1-s)O_k`.  The tail `V` is above punishment by `(43.3),(43.5),(43.6)`, and
the exact predecessor bound puts the successor above punishment. QED.

Condition `(43.5)` is also necessary for this **fixed-outsider**
desaturation.  A mixed outsider must remain indifferent, while its
conditional-Continue gap has strictly negative slope `-R_i`; its only
possible tail coordinate is `T_i`.  If `T_i<P_i`, no floor tail can retain
that outsider probability once `k` Continues with positive probability.

The reviewed Section 42 regression lies on the positive side of this new
boundary.  There `H_k=2/5`.  Conditional on `k` Continuing, the thresholds
are

```text
T_a=1/2,       T_b=0.
```

Thus `V=(2/5,1/2,0)`.  Replacing `p_k=1` by any `s in (0,1)` while keeping
`p_a=p_b=1/2` is exact at `V`; at `s=1/2` its charge is `7/8`.  Its successor
is

```text
(1, (1+s)/4, s/2),                                  (43.8)
```

so this exact repair still does not return in payoff.  The example therefore
separates the two remaining tasks cleanly: desaturation/root change can be
exact and uniformly charged, while payoff re-entry remains absent.

Proposition 43 does not yet give an actual paid-source adapter.  It changes
the non-owner tail coordinates from the reached tail to the conditional
thresholds `(43.4)`.  The source must prove `(43.5)` and then connect those
threshold payoffs by exact reached edges, or use a different root-changing
mechanism when a mixed threshold lies below punishment.

`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_43.md`
validates the switch-tail signs, conditional decomposition, mixed and pure
support cases, floor and charge bounds, and every fraction in the Section 42
application.  It confirms that the remaining gap is source production and
payoff re-entry, not exact desaturation under `(43.5)`.

### 44. Mixed-threshold floor failure is compatible with a singleton gap

Condition `(43.5)` is a real source obligation, not a consequence of a strict
singleton gap.  Modify only the rewards **not containing `k`** in the Section
42 table.  Set

```text
r_a({a})=r_a({a,b})=-1,      r_a({b})=0,
r_b({b})=1/2,                r_b({a})=r_b({a,b})=0. (44.1)
```

Keep every coalition containing `k`, and all of player `k`'s rewards, exactly
as in `(42.1)--(42.2)`.  The punishment floor is still zero.  Player `a` can
guarantee zero by Never, while opponents `k` Never and `b` sure hold its best
payoff to zero (Quit gives `-1`, Continue gives zero).  Player `b` has only
nonnegative rewards, and `k` Never with `a` sure holds both its actions to
zero.  Player `k` is punished as before by both outsiders Never.

At `U=0`, the root `(42.3)` is unchanged and exact, because `k` Quits surely
and none of the modified rows is reached.  Its unique outsider completion,
charge one, and premium `3/20` are also unchanged.  There is a strict
singleton gap for `b`, namely

```text
U_b=0<1/2=r_b({b}).                                  (44.2)
```

But conditional on `k` Continuing and the other outsider mixing `1/2`,
player `a` has

```text
Q_a^C=-1,       K_a^C=0,       R_a=1/2,
T_a=-2<P_a=0.                                        (44.3)
```

Therefore, after replacing `p_k=1` by any `s<1` while keeping
`p_a=p_b=1/2`, `a` can remain mixed only at tail coordinate `V_a=-2`.
No punishment-floor tail permits that fixed-outsider desaturation.

**Proposition 44 (sharp threshold-failure regression).**  A floor-safe exact
unique-sure root with positive collision premium and a strict singleton gap
need not satisfy the mixed-threshold floor condition `(43.5)`.  The failure
can occur at a mixed outsider while every datum on the original sure-owner
face, including its unique Nash completion, remains unchanged.

This is not a no-go for root change in the table: the failing outsider may
leave support, the sure owner may move jointly with the outsider profile, or a
later edge may pay the threshold displacement.  It proves that the paid
source must control the conditional `k`-Continue rows or supply precisely one
of those support-changing alternatives; the singleton-gap theorem alone
cannot dispatch this branch.

### 45. Outsider-Nash switch-tail compiler, and exact repair of Section 44

There is a general exact compiler for a genuine outsider root change.  It
does not force its two scalar side conditions, but it identifies them without
any continuity or Nash-selection assumption.

Fix an intended owner hazard `s in (0,1)` and boxed floor-safe outsider tail
coordinates `P_i<=W_i<=M` for `i!=k`, where `M` is the canonical reward
bound.  Treat `k`'s action as an independent coin
with Quit probability `s`, and consider the finite endpoint game played by
the outsiders at those tail coordinates.  Let `z` be any mixed Nash profile
of that finite game; existence is the ordinary finite-game Nash theorem.  Put

```text
O(z)=product_(i!=k)(1-z_i),
Q_k(z)=k's forced-Quit payoff against z,
K_k(z)=k's forced-Continue absorbing contribution against z.  (45.1)
```

Suppose

```text
O(z)>0,
P_k <= H_k(z)=[Q_k(z)-K_k(z)]/O(z) <= M,            (45.2)
```

where `M` is the canonical reward bound.

Set `V_k=H_k(z)`, `V_i=W_i` for outsiders, and combine owner hazard `s` with
outsider profile `z`.

**Proposition 45 (outsider-Nash switch-tail compiler).**  Under `(45.2)`, the
combined product root is exact endpoint Nash at the floor tail `V`.  Its
absorption charge is at least `s`, so `s=1/2` would already give the fixed
threshold required upstream.

**Proof.**  Every outsider is a best response by the definition of `z` in the
finite endpoint game with the owner coin and tail `W_i`.  The owner gap is

```text
Q_k(z)-K_k(z)-O(z)V_k=0,
```

so its interior mixture is also optimal.  Joint Continue probability is
`(1-s)O(z)<=1-s`, proving the charge bound.  The tail is above punishment by
construction, and the exact predecessor bound puts the successor above
punishment. QED.

If `O(z)=0`, tail adjustment cannot affect the owner.  The same construction
works exactly when `Q_k(z)=K_k(z)`; otherwise an interior owner mixture is
impossible at that outsider profile.  Thus the general residual is precisely
a positive-survival outsider Nash completion whose owner switch value is
above punishment, or the displayed zero-survival equality.

The Section 44 threshold failure has an exact support-changing repair of this
form.  Keep outsider tails zero and take

```text
s=3/4,
z_a=7/13,
z_b=2/3.                                             (45.3)
```

For general `s>1/2`, the outsider endpoint gaps at zero tail are

```text
Delta_a=2*s*z_b-1,
Delta_b=(1+s)/2-(1+3*s)*z_a/2.                      (45.4)
```

Thus `(45.3)` is the unique fully mixed outsider Nash profile at `s=3/4`.
It has

```text
O=2/13,
Q_k=(4/3)*(11/13)=44/39,
K_k=(6/5)*(11/13)=66/65,
H_k=11/15.                                           (45.5)
```

The tail `V=(11/15,0,0)` lies in the canonical box and is above the zero
punishment floor, and Proposition
45 gives an exact root of charge

```text
1-(1/4)*(2/13)=25/26.                               (45.6)
```

Its successor is

```text
X=(44/39,1/4,21/52).                                (45.7)
```

So the mixed-threshold failure of Section 44 does **not** obstruct exact,
uniformly charged support change.  But `(45.7)` is still a fixed positive
distance from `V`; the construction supplies no payoff return.  The actual
paid problem is now cleanly split into two scalar/source tasks:

```text
produce an outsider Nash completion satisfying (45.2),
then connect the resulting exact changed-root edge into a payoff near-return.
```

Neither follows from the paid row or singleton gap alone.

### 46. A regular sure-face equilibrium desaturates, but does not return

Section 45's scalar completion is automatic near the original sure face under
one standard, completely finite-dimensional regularity hypothesis.  This
localizes the actual-source obstruction more sharply than merely asking for
an arbitrary Nash selection.

Let `k` be the unique sure quitter in an exact product root `q` at a tail
`U>=P`.  Thus every outsider probability is strictly below one and

```text
O(q)=product_(i!=k)(1-q_i)>0.
```

Write `A={i!=k:0<q_i<1}` for the mixed outsiders and
`Z={i!=k:q_i=0}` for the pure continuers.  Fix arbitrary boxed outsider tail
coordinates `P_i<=W_i<=M`.  For `s` near one and an outsider product profile
`z`, let

```text
Delta_i(s,z;W_i)
```

be outsider `i`'s Quit-minus-Continue endpoint gap when `k` Quits with
probability `s` and `i`'s continuation tail is `W_i`.  These gaps are
multilinear, hence continuously differentiable, in `(s,z)`.  At `s=1` they
are independent of `W`, and `q_-k` is an outsider Nash profile.  Assume:

```text
Delta_i(1,q_-k;W_i)<0                         for every i in Z,       (46.1)
det D_(z_A)(Delta_i(1,z;W_i))_(i in A)|_(z=q_-k) != 0,               (46.2)
P_k < H_k(q_-k)=[Q_k(q_-k)-K_k(q_-k)]/O(q_-k) < M.                 (46.3)
```

For `A` empty, `(46.2)` has its usual vacuous meaning.  Conditions `(46.1)`
and `(46.2)` say precisely that the outsider equilibrium is support-regular:
every inactive outsider strictly prefers Continue, and the active
indifference equations have nonsingular Jacobian.

**Proposition 46 (regular sure-face continuation).**  Under `(46.1)--(46.3)`,
there is `epsilon>0` and a continuous outsider profile `z(s)` for
`1-epsilon<s<=1` such that:

1. `z(1)=q_-k`, every coordinate in `A` remains strictly mixed, and every
   coordinate in `Z` remains zero;
2. `z(s)` is a Nash equilibrium of the outsider endpoint game with owner
   hazard `s` and tail `W`; and
3. for every `s<1` in that interval, Proposition 45 produces an exact
   punishment-floor edge with tail

```text
V(s)_k=H_k(z(s)),     V(s)_i=W_i  (i!=k),                         (46.4)
```

   and absorption charge at least `s`.  In particular, after shrinking
   `epsilon` if necessary, all these edges have charge at least `1/2`.

**Proof.**  Apply the implicit-function theorem to the `|A|` equations
`Delta_i(s,z;W_i)=0`, `i in A`, with all `Z` coordinates fixed at zero.
Nonsingularity in `(46.2)` gives a unique continuous local solution.  The
strict inequalities `0<q_i<1` on `A` persist, as do the strict inactive signs
`(46.1)`, so this solution is an outsider Nash profile.  Since every
outsider probability is below one at the limit, `O(z(s))>0` nearby.  The
owner switch value is continuous there, and `(46.3)` keeps
`P_k<=H_k(z(s))<=M` after shrinking the interval.  Proposition 45 now applies,
and its charge estimate is `charge>=s`.  QED.

The owner switch at the original sure root always satisfies the lower bound

```text
H_k(q_-k)>=U_k>=P_k,                                             (46.5)
```

because exactness of the sure action says
`Q_k>=K_k+O(q_-k)U_k`.  Therefore Proposition 46 gives the following exact
failure trichotomy.  If no floor-safe Section 45 completion exists for owner
hazards arbitrarily close to one along a fixed outsider tail `W`, then at the
chosen sure-face equilibrium at least one of the following holds:

```text
H_k(q_-k) notin (P_k,M);               (floor/box boundary or inadmissible)
some inactive outsider is tied;       (Delta_i=0 at q_i=0)
the active-gap Jacobian is singular.                                  (46.6)
```

In the positive-premium setting `(43.1)`, `H_k<Q_k<=M`; hence the upper
boundary and above-box cases in `(46.6)` are impossible, and its first arm is
exactly `H_k=P_k`.

Thus the missing source datum is not continuity of a globally selected Nash
profile.  A local support-regular equilibrium with strict switch slack is
enough.  Conversely, the inspected actual source structures do not state any
of the three exclusions in `(46.6)`: `FullReplacementCluster` retains its
semantic cluster, subsequence, debt-direction bounds, and exact mover debt;
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow` retains the ordered pure-time witnesses,
live mass, and reached gain; and `QuittingStoppingLawCurvaturePaidWitness`
adds the two approximate witness inequalities.  None supplies an outsider
gap Jacobian, strict owner switch slack, or a reached successor relation for
the new tail.  This is an adapter deficit, not a proof that the full source
data logically forbid such a completion.

There is also a sharp chronological warning.  Let `X(s)` be the successor
payoff of the edge in Proposition 46.  Both `X(s)` and `V(s)` are continuous.
Consequently, if

```text
d=||X(1)-V(1)||_infinity>0,                                      (46.7)
```

then every sufficiently near desaturation satisfies

```text
||X(s)-V(s)||_infinity>=d/2.                                    (46.8)
```

So local root continuation alone cannot yield the required one-edge payoff
near-return; at least one further exact edge or a nonlocal parameter move is
necessary.

The Section 44 regression tests both conclusions exactly.  Its outsider gaps
near `s=1` are

```text
Delta_a=2s z_b-1,
Delta_b=(1+s)/2-(1+3s)z_a/2.
```

At the sure-face equilibrium `(z_a,z_b)=(1/2,1/2)`, the active Jacobian is

```text
[[0,2],[-2,0]],       determinant=4,
```

and the switch value is `H_k=2/5>P_k=0`.  The local branch is the explicit
one already used in Section 45,

```text
z_b(s)=1/(2s),       z_a(s)=(1+s)/(1+3s).
```

But as `s` tends to one its tail and successor tend to

```text
V(1)=(2/5,0,0),      X(1)=(1,1/2,1/2),
```

so `d=3/5`; all sufficiently late edges on this regular, uniformly charged
branch still have payoff mismatch at least `3/10`.  This does not rule out a
multi-edge return or a support change away from the local branch.  It proves
that the two outstanding obligations remain genuinely separate: local
floor-safe exactification can be robust while chronological payoff re-entry
is absent.

### 47. Uniform tail continuation closes all outsider payoffs

The regularity hypothesis in Proposition 46 does more than continue one
fixed outsider tail.  At `s=1` the owner Quits surely, so every outsider gap
and its active Jacobian are independent of **all** outsider continuation
coordinates.  Compactness therefore makes the continuation uniform over the
whole boxed outsider-tail rectangle.

Assume `(46.1)--(46.3)`, and put

```text
K_out=product_(i!=k)[P_i,M].                                    (47.1)
```

**Proposition 47 (outsider-closed normal form).**  There is `epsilon>0` and a
jointly continuous map

```text
(s,W) |-> z_s(W),       1-epsilon<s<=1,  W in K_out,
```

with the following properties.

1. `z_s(W)` has the same support as `q_-k` and is an outsider Nash
   equilibrium for owner hazard `s` and outsider tail `W`.
2. Its switch value satisfies `P_k<H_k(z_s(W))<M`, so Proposition 45 gives an
   exact floor-admissible edge of charge at least `s`.
3. For at least one `W*=W*(s)` in `K_out`, that edge has tail payoff `V` and
   successor payoff `X` satisfying

```text
X_i=V_i=W*_i                for every i!=k,                       (47.2)
X_k-V_k=[K_k(z_s(W*))-(1-O(z_s(W*)))Q_k(z_s(W*))]/O(z_s(W*)).   (47.3)
```

Thus the exact payoff-return problem on the regular unique-sure arm reduces
to the single scalar collision premium in `(47.3)`.  If that scalar vanishes,
the edge itself is a positive-charge payoff self-loop and answers the
maintained near-return question on this arm.

**Proof.**  Regard `W` as an additional parameter in the active equations of
Proposition 46.  At every point `(s,W,z)=(1,W,q_-k)` the equations have the
same value and the same invertible active Jacobian.  Parameterized implicit-
function neighborhoods cover the compact rectangle `K_out`; a finite
subcover and local uniqueness give one common `epsilon` and patch the local
solutions into the displayed continuous map.  Strict inactive signs, active
interiority, positive outsider survival, and the strict switch inequalities
all persist uniformly.

For fixed `s`, let `F_s(W)` be the vector of outsider successor payoffs of the
Proposition 45 edge defined by `z_s(W)`.  It is continuous.  Exact predecessor
floor transport gives `F_s(W)_i>=P_i`, while the canonical payoff box gives
`F_s(W)_i<=M`; hence `F_s` maps the compact convex rectangle `K_out` into
itself.  Brouwer gives `W*` with `F_s(W*)=W*`, proving `(47.2)`.  Since the
owner mixes, its successor payoff is its forced-Quit payoff `Q_k`.  Its tail
is the indifference switch `H_k=(Q_k-K_k)/O`, and subtraction gives `(47.3)`.
QED.

The branch under attack has the original strict premium

```text
mu_0=K_k(q_-k)-(1-O(q_-k))Q_k(q_-k)>0.                           (47.4)
```

The uniform construction converges to the constant sure-face profile
`q_-k` as `s` tends to one, uniformly in `W`.  Therefore, after shrinking
`epsilon`, the numerator in `(47.3)` is at least `mu_0/2`.  Since `O<=1`,
every outsider-closed edge furnished above obeys

```text
X_k-V_k>=mu_0/2>0.                                               (47.5)
```

So Brouwer closure does not finish the positive-premium case: it removes all
outsider payoff seams and leaves one uniformly positive owner seam.  Any
chronological payoff near-return must subsequently decrease that owner's
payoff, transfer the residual to a different owner, or leave this local
regular branch.  This is stronger than the raw mismatch `(46.8)`: the
surviving mismatch is now exactly one coordinate and exactly the normalized
premium.

Proposition 47 is a conditional supplied-root theorem, not an actual paid-
source adapter.  In particular, no inspected paid/full-replacement field
forces support regularity or connects the one-coordinate successor to a later
exact edge.  The source-level target is now precise: either exclude the
degenerate alternatives `(46.6)`, then pay the signed owner seam `(47.5)` by
a reached owner transfer, or construct a genuinely nonlocal completion that
does both at once.

### 48. Exact charge cost of repaying the owner seam

The one-coordinate residual in Proposition 47 cannot disappear for free in a
later exact path.  Let `M` be the reward bound.  For any exact Bellman edge
with tail payoff `v`, current payoff `x`, product root `p`, and literal
absorption charge `A(p)`, the successor identity gives, for every player `i`,

```text
x_i-v_i = sum_(nonempty S) Pr_p(first coalition S) [r_i(S)-v_i]. (48.1)
```

Both terminal rewards and boxed tail coordinates lie in `[-M,M]`.  Hence

```text
x_i-v_i >= -2M A(p).                                             (48.2)
```

This statement uses the literal charge of the exact edge.  It is not a claim
about a behavioral paid block's raw hazard or about the existence of one
large edge inside a block.

**Proposition 48 (owner-seam repayment ledger).**  Suppose an exact charged
edge goes from tail payoff `V` to current payoff `X` and satisfies

```text
X_i=V_i  for i!=k,       X_k-V_k=a>0.                            (48.3)
```

Let an exact floor-admissible path continue from `X` to a payoff `Y`.  If

```text
||Y-V||_infinity<=eta<a,                                        (48.4)
```

then the later path has total `k`-coordinate displacement at most
`-(a-eta)`, contains at least one edge with negative `k` displacement, and
its total literal absorption charge is at least

```text
(a-eta)/(2M).                                                    (48.5)
```

Here `a>0` already forces `M>0`, since both endpoint payoffs are boxed by
`M`.

In particular, when `eta<=a/2`, repayment costs total later charge at least
`a/(4M)`.

**Proof.**  Chronological concatenation telescopes the payoff coordinates:

```text
sum_(later edges e) [current(e)_k-tail(e)_k]
  =Y_k-X_k
  =(Y_k-V_k)-a
  <=eta-a.
```

The negative total forces at least one negative summand.  Summing `(48.2)`
over the later edges gives

```text
eta-a >= -2M * sum_(later e) A(e),
```

which is `(48.5)`.  QED.

Combining Propositions 47 and 48 in the positive-premium arm gives a fixed
quantitative target.  There is a regular exact first edge of charge at least
`1/2` whose only payoff motion is

```text
a=X_k-V_k>=mu_0/2.
```

Any payoff return of accuracy `eta<=mu_0/4` must later decrease player `k` by
at least `mu_0/4` and spend at least

```text
mu_0/(8M)                                                     (48.6)
```

in aggregate exact-edge charge after the first edge.  This does not produce
the repayment path, and without a path-length bound it does not force one
later edge of charge bounded below.  The maintained near-return threshold is
already supplied by the first edge; `(48.6)` instead identifies the exact
nonlocal source obligation.  A successful paid adapter must produce a
reached edge or block with negative displacement in the current premium
owner coordinate.  Static outsider reselection and local desaturation cannot
do so because Proposition 47 has closed all other coordinates while keeping
that one strictly increasing.

### 49. The terminal gap excludes the entire near-sure completion route

The actual positive-minimum branch carries a positive terminal
exploitability gap.  Under that hypothesis, the sure-face roots used in
Sections 40--48 are not merely difficult to source: no exact floor-admissible
edge can have a sure quitter at all.

Let `P` be the behavioral punishment vector and assume

```text
HasTerminalExploitabilityGap reward gamma,       gamma>0.          (49.1)
```

Suppose a product root `q` is exact endpoint Nash at a boxed tail `U>=P`, and
some player `k` Quits surely.  Since `k` is sure, every outsider's endpoint
game has zero continuation and exactness makes `q` a
`IsQuittingForcedOwnerNashRow` for owner `k`.  Put

```text
O=quittingForcedOwnerAllOutsidersContinueMass(q,k),
Q=quittingForcedOwnerObeyValue(q,k),
K=quittingStationaryFixedOpponentsContinueReward(q,k).
```

Owner optimality at tail `U` gives

```text
Q >= K+O U_k >= K+O P_k,
```

so the atomic blocker balance is nonnegative:

```text
B(q)=Q-K-O P_k >=0.                                      (49.2)
```

But the named terminal-gap theorem
`quittingAtomicBlockerBalance_le_neg_of_terminalExploitabilityGap` gives

```text
B(q)<=-gamma<0,                                          (49.3)
```

a contradiction.

**Proposition 49 (uniform no-sure floor boundary).**  Under `(49.1)`:

1. no exact punishment-floor endpoint root has a sure quitter; and
2. there is a game-dependent `rho<1` such that every exact endpoint-Nash root
   at every boxed tail `U>=P` satisfies

```text
q_i <= rho     for every player i.                         (49.4)
```

**Proof.**  The first statement is `(49.2)--(49.3)`.  For the second, if no
uniform `rho` existed, choose exact floor roots `q_n` and boxed floor tails
`U_n` with `max_i q_n(i)->1`.  The finite root cube and boxed floor-tail set
are compact.  After a subsequence, `q_n->q`, `U_n->U`, and one fixed player
has probability tending to one.  Endpoint-Nash inequalities are closed, so
`q` is exact at `U>=P`; the fixed player is sure, contradicting part 1.  QED.

There is an equivalent direct reading of the Section 45 switch.  For a
forced-`k` outsider Nash completion `z` with `O(z)>0`, its switch value
satisfies

```text
O(z)[H_k(z)-P_k]
  =Q_k(z)-K_k(z)-O(z)P_k
  =B(z)<=-gamma.                                         (49.5)
```

Thus `H_k(z)<P_k`, so the switch is strictly below the punishment floor.  If
instead `O(z)=0`, `H` is undefined and interior owner mixing would require `Q=K`,
whereas `(49.5)` says `Q-K<=-gamma`; that boundary also fails.  Thus the
forced-owner outsider Nash completion supplied by ordinary finite-game Nash
is always on the **wrong** side of the Section 45 floor condition in the
actual terminal-gap branch.

Proposition 49 changes the paid boundary materially:

- Sections 40--42 remain valid local/positive examples, but their sure-root
  arms cannot occur inside the unresolved positive-gap frontier.
- Sections 46--48 give a valid local normal form for generic games, but the
  actual branch cannot approach it with `s->1`; every exact floor root has
  owner hazards bounded by `(49.4)`.
- The source producer must make a nonperturbative support/root move with owner
  hazard at most `rho`, or work directly in the wholly non-sure root arm.  It
  cannot obtain payoff re-entry by an infinitesimal desaturation of the paid
  observer's forced-Q outsider Nash face.

This does not yet construct the desired payoff near-return.  It removes the
regular/floor-tight alternatives posed after Section 48 from the actual
near-sure source: their correct resolution is strict exclusion.  The
remaining positive task is a bounded-away-from-sure exact root followed by a
reached negative payoff transfer.  The paid row supplies the forced-face sign
separation of Section 16, but no current declaration turns that separation
into such a nonlocal floor root or a chronological return.

The qualitative no-sure conclusion also overlaps the checked exclusion of

`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Classification/TerminalExploitabilityBranchExclusions.lean`
and the sure-quitter adapter in `InstantPunishmentEquivalence.lean`.  The new
content here is only the direct switch-tail identity `(49.5)` and its uniform
compact exact-floor-root reformulation for the paid adapter.  The proposition
is ordinary mathematics assembled from those checked endpoints and
compactness; no new Lean implementation or export novelty is claimed.

### 50. The singleton-gap arm has a uniformly interior charged root

Section 49 rules out near-sure roots, but in the floor-safe uniform-singleton-
gap arm the actual first charged edge is already available nonperturbatively.
This is an assembly of two named fixed-tail facts with Proposition 49.

Let `U` be a boxed tail with `U>=P`, let `M` be the reward bound, and suppose
for some player `g` and some fixed `delta>0` that

```text
U_g <= r({g})_g-delta.                                      (50.1)
```

The checked fixed-tail theorem
`exists_isZeroQuittingRootEndpointNash_simplex_with_positive_absorption_of_singleton_gap`
produces an exact endpoint-Nash product root at `U`.  The quantitative theorem
`gap_div_le_quittingRootAbsorptionMass_of_isZeroEndpointNash` gives every such
root absorption charge at least

```text
c=delta/(delta+2M)>0.                                      (50.2)
```

Assume also the terminal gap `(49.1)`, and let `rho<1` be Proposition 49's
uniform marginal ceiling.  Write `n=|I|` and

```text
kappa=(1-rho)^n>0.                                         (50.3)
```

**Proposition 50 (uniformly interior charged packet).**  Every exact root `q`
at `U` satisfies

```text
absorption(q)>=c,
q_i<=rho                         for every i,
jointContinue(q)>=kappa,
deletedContinue_i(q)>=(1-rho)^(n-1)  for every i.           (50.4)
```

Moreover, some player `k` satisfies

```text
c/n <= q_k <= rho.                                         (50.5)
```

Thus the singleton-gap arm supplies a fixed-charge exact floor edge with one
uniformly interior active marginal, persistent joint survival, and every
player-deleted survival bounded away from zero.  No support regularity,
near-sure limiting argument, or Section 45 switch selection is required.

**Proof.**  The first two inequalities are `(50.2)` and Proposition 49.
Multiplying `1-q_i>=1-rho` gives the joint and deleted survival bounds.  The
union bound for independent Quit events gives

```text
absorption(q)=1-product_i(1-q_i)<=sum_i q_i.
```

Together with `absorption(q)>=c`, finite averaging yields a `k` with
`q_k>=c/n`; Proposition 49 gives its upper bound.  QED.

For the selected active `k`, exact endpoint indifference and the deleted-
survival lower bound give the well-conditioned Bellman identity

```text
U_k=[Q_k(q)-K_k(q)]/O_k(q),
O_k(q)>=(1-rho)^(n-1).                                    (50.6)
```

This removes both denominator pathologies that motivated the near-sure
analysis: exact-edge charge and player-deleted survival are simultaneously
uniform.  It does **not** close the payoff.  The successor `X` is above the
floor, but the actual paid/reset data still provide no exact path from `X`
with negative displacement sufficient to return near `U`.  Proposition 48's
repayment ledger applies to any coordinate-pure normalization one can derive,
while the general root may move several payoff coordinates at once.

Source scope is exact.  Proposition 50 dispatches only the floor-safe uniform
singleton-gap arm already isolated in Section 38A.  If the prescribed tail is
below punishment, or every singleton payoff is at most the tail, the fixed-
tail inputs do not apply.  Those below-floor/wholly non-instant arms and the
chronological negative transfer remain open.

`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_50.md`
validates the all-root absorption bound, its composition with the same boxed
tail/root in Proposition 49, all product and averaging constants, and the
mixed-support identity `(50.6)`.  It emphasizes that these are local one-row
survival floors, not persistence along a supplied chronology.

### 51. Explicit terminal-gap marginal cap

Proposition 49's compact `rho` has a direct quantitative replacement.  Keep
the terminal gap `(49.1)`, let `M` bound all rewards, and let `q` be any exact
endpoint-Nash root at a boxed tail `U>=P`.  Then `M>0` under a positive gap.

Fix a player `k` and write its Continue probability as

```text
d_k=1-q_k.
```

Force only `k`'s marginal to pure Quit, obtaining `q^k`.  The checked
near-sure estimate `nearSureRootReplacement` gives

```text
q^k is a (4M d_k)-Nash root at tail U.                        (51.1)
```

Choose a stationary punishment continuation for `k` whose unilateral cap is
at most `P_k+epsilon<=U_k+epsilon`, and let `V` be its full continuation
best-response vector.  Against `q^k`, every outsider still faces the surely
quitting player `k`, even after changing its own root action, so both its
prescribed and deviating root payoffs are independent of `V_i`; its root
regret is exactly the one at `U`.  For player `k`, its prescribed sure-Quit
payoff is independent of `V_k`, while a pure-Continue deviation reaches the
continuation only with probability at most one.  Since
`V_k<=P_k+epsilon<=U_k+epsilon`, its regret rises by at most `epsilon`.
Affine dependence on `k`'s mixed action then gives the same bound for every
one-stage deviation.  Hence `q^k` is a `(4M d_k+epsilon)`-Nash root against
`V`.  The sure-first all-behavior compiler produces a terminal behavioral
profile with unrestricted exploitability at most that quantity.

**Proposition 51 (explicit no-near-sure scale).**  Under `(49.1)`, every exact
floor root satisfies, for every player `k`,

```text
1-q_k >= gamma/(4M),
q_k <= 1-gamma/(4M).                                      (51.2)
```

**Proof.**  If `4M d_k<gamma`, choose
`epsilon in (0,gamma-4M d_k)`.  The preceding punishment splice and first-
branch compiler give a terminal profile with exploitability strictly below
`gamma`, contradicting `(49.1)`.  Thus `4M d_k>=gamma`.  QED.

The argument also shows `gamma<=4M`, since `d_k<=1`.  Therefore

```text
rho_explicit=1-gamma/(4M) in [0,1)                         (51.3)
```

can replace the compact ceiling in Propositions 49--50.  In particular the
singleton-gap packet of Proposition 50 has explicit survival bounds

```text
jointContinue(q)>=(gamma/(4M))^n,
deletedContinue_i(q)>=(gamma/(4M))^(n-1).                 (51.4)
```

This is still not a return producer.  It proves that every exact floor edge
in the actual positive-gap branch retains a uniform all-Continue atom and all
deleted clocks.  Thus any successful negative payoff transfer must operate
with genuine positive survival; it cannot hide the repayment in a near-sure
or zero-denominator edge.

approximation `exists_punishRow_stationaryUnilateralCap_le`, and the sure-root
all-behavior compiler
`isεAsymptoticNash_quittingRootThenContinuation_of_isεQuittingRootNash` in
`Quitting/Root/FirstBranch.lean`.  This section is an ordinary-mathematics
specialization to exact boxed floor roots; no new Lean declaration is claimed.

`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_51.md`
validates the `4Md` replacement constant, outsider tail-independence, the
owner punishment-cap comparison, mixed one-stage deviations, the full
continuation-best-response vector, and the unrestricted first-branch
compiler.  It confirms that `(51.2)--(51.4)` are quantitative conditioning

### 52. Why global Nash-correspondence continuation does not supply re-entry

A tempting way to bypass the singular/support-change alternatives of Section
46 is to use a global continuation theorem for the finite one-shot Nash
correspondence.  This does not address the chronological equation.  Write
`E(U)` for the set of one-shot equilibrium payoffs at continuation tail `U`.
The exact Bellman direction requires an **inverse orbit**

```text
X_t in E(X_(t+1)),                                      (52.1)
```

with nonvanishing absorption often enough to recur.  A connected equilibrium
component over an exogenously prescribed parameter segment supplies pairs
`(U,q)` with `q` Nash at `U`; it does not make the equilibrium payoff equal to
the next parameter on that component.  In particular it does not imply
`(52.1)`.

This mismatch is not merely technical.  Solan, *The Dynamics of the Nash
Correspondence and n-Player Stochastic Games*, International Game Theory
Review 3 (2001), 291--300, defines precisely these inverse iterates.  His
Theorem 2.1 gives, for all sufficiently small positive perturbations of an
explicit three-player quitting game, only **trivial** inverse-iterate vectors:
every admissible exact inverse orbit retains positive infinite survival.  The
same game nevertheless has a uniform-equilibrium payoff by the separate
three-player existence theorem.  Thus pointwise Nash existence, compactness,
semialgebraicity, or connected continuation of finite-game equilibria cannot
by themselves force a charged exact orbit or payoff re-entry.

This literature regression does **not** refute the maintained paid producer.
It does not test the positive-minimum frontier, the fixed paid row, or the
singleton-gap source arm, and triviality of infinite exact inverse iterates is
weaker than exclusion of the tolerance-dependent finite near-returns in the
question.  It kills only the proposed selection shortcut.  A successful use
of global equilibrium continuation must add a paid-source quantity that
orients the equilibrium payoff itself and is preserved after each reached
successor; a parameter-spanning equilibrium component is insufficient.

Source checked for this section: E. Solan, 2001, abstract, Section 1's
definition of `E_0`, periods and inverse iterates, and Theorem 2.1 together
with its stated comparison to three-player uniform existence.  The paper's
payoff convention is terminal payoff `0` on Never, matching the quitting-game
convention relevant to the regression.  No claim from this paper is used as
a positive producer.

Independent source/scope review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_52.md`
checks Solan's definitions and Theorem 2.1 and validates exactly the narrow
shortcut no-go above.  In particular it confirms that the paper does not rule
out finite payoff near-returns or any continuation augmented by paid-source
data.

### 53. A negative joining account or a crossed active outsider

Return to the floor-safe singleton-gap arm of Proposition 50 and assume at
least two players.  Let `g` satisfy

```text
U_g <= r({g})_g-delta,       delta>0,                    (53.1)
```

and let `q` be any exact endpoint-Nash root at `U`.  Put

```text
d=gamma/(4M),
omega=d^(n-1),
a=delta*omega,
c=delta/(delta+2M).                                    (53.2)
```

Here `n=|I|`.  Propositions 50--51 give absorption at least `c` and
`q_i<=1-d` for every player.  In particular `g` has positive Continue support
and its opponent all-Continue mass satisfies

```text
O_g=product_(j!=g)(1-q_j) >= omega.                     (53.3)
```

Let `J_g(q)` be `quittingOutsiderJoiningContribution reward q g`: under the
opponent product law with `g` forced to Continue, it is the expectation of
the payoff change caused by inserting `g` into the realized nonempty opponent
coalition.  The exact decomposition is

```text
D_g=O_g*(r({g})_g-U_g)+J_g(q),                          (53.4)
```

where `D_g` is Quit minus Continue.  Since `g` has positive Continue support,
endpoint Nash gives `D_g<=0`.  Therefore

```text
J_g(q) <= -a.                                           (53.5)
```

This is a uniformly signed negative account inside **every** exact charged
root in this arm, not merely an unsigned absorption estimate.

There is a useful event form.  For a nonempty opponent coalition `S`, write

```text
Delta_g(S)=r(S union {g})_g-r(S)_g.
```

Every such increment lies in `[-2M,2M]`; define it to be zero at the empty
opponent coalition.  Let `Bad_g` be the opponent event

```text
Delta_g(S) <= -a/2.                                     (53.6)
```

Then

```text
P_opponents(Bad_g) >= a/(4M-a) >= a/(4M)>0.             (53.7)
```

Indeed `(53.5)` and the absolute joining bound first imply `a<=2M`.  If `p`
is the probability of `Bad_g`, the pointwise lower bounds `-2M` on `Bad_g`
and `-a/2` on its complement give

```text
-a >= J_g(q) >= -2M*p-(a/2)*(1-p),
```

which rearranges to the first inequality in `(53.7)`; the second uses
`a<=2M<4M`.

**Proposition 53 (collision-or-crossed-support dichotomy).**  Every root above
satisfies one of the following two alternatives.

1. `q_g>=c/2`.  The actual product root assigns probability at least

   ```text
   c*a/(8M)                                               (53.8)
   ```

   to a terminal collision containing `g` whose insertion decrement is at
   most `-a/2`.

2. `q_g<c/2`.  Some distinct outsider `k` satisfies

   ```text
   q_k>c/(2*(n-1)),      q_k<=1-d,                       (53.9)
   ```

   while the counterfactual `g`-joining event still has opponent probability
   at least `a/(4M)`.

**Proof.**  In the first case product independence multiplies `(53.7)` by
`q_g>=c/2`, giving `(53.8)`.  In the second case the union bound and
Proposition 50 give

```text
c <= absorption(q) <= sum_i q_i,
```

so the outsider marginals sum to more than `c/2`; finite averaging gives
`(53.9)`, and Proposition 51 gives its upper bound.  QED.

This advances the sign boundary but is not yet the requested negative payoff
edge.  Alternative 1 supplies an actual nonperturbative negative collision
cell inside the first exact root, but not a later root whose Bellman payoff
moves back toward `U`.  Alternative 2 exposes the exact incidence seam: a
uniformly active outsider is present, while the player carrying the negative
joining account may have zero actual Quit mass.  A source-matched support
change must activate `g` without losing floor Nash and then re-enter the
payoff.  Thus the remaining gap is no longer an unsigned collision estimate;
it is chronological activation of this signed joining account.

Named identities used here:
`quittingRootEndpointDifference_eq_outsiderNever` and
`quittingOutsiderJoiningContribution` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Paths/OutsiderNeverGluing.lean`, and the absolute
joining estimate in `FixedTailUniformAbsorption.lean`.  The charge and
marginal constants are exactly reviewed Propositions 50--51.  No paid-source
return or Lean declaration is claimed.

### 54. Negative joining does not orient Bellman displacement

The signed account `(53.5)` is not itself a negative payoff transfer.  The
following exact level-shift regression shows the missing datum.

Fix a reward table `r`, a player `g`, a tail `U`, and a product root `q`.  Let

```text
A_g=1-product_(j!=g)(1-q_j)
```

be opponent absorption when `g` is forced to Continue.  For any scalar `z`,
define a new reward table `r^z` by changing only coordinate `g`:

```text
r^z(T)_g = r(T)_g                    if T={g},
r^z(T)_g = r(T)_g+z                  if T!={g},
r^z(T)_i = r(T)_i                    if i!=g.             (54.1)
```

Thus every terminal outcome in which at least one opponent Quits receives
the same level shift in `g`'s coordinate, whether or not `g` joins.

**Proposition 54 (common-level invariance).**  At the same tail and root:

```text
Q_g(r^z,U,q)=Q_g(r,U,q)+A_g*z,
C_g(r^z,U,q)=C_g(r,U,q)+A_g*z,                            (54.2)
```

so `g`'s endpoint difference is unchanged.  Every other player's endpoint
difference is unchanged as well.  Consequently `q` is exact endpoint-Nash
for `(r^z,U)` iff it is exact endpoint-Nash for `(r,U)`.  Moreover,

```text
r^z({g})_g-U_g = r({g})_g-U_g,
J_g(r^z,q)=J_g(r,q),
X_g(r^z,U,q)=X_g(r,U,q)+A_g*z,                            (54.3)
```

while the root charge, all marginal probabilities, and all survival factors
are identical.

**Proof.**  Force `g` to either action.  On the event that every opponent
Continues, the two relevant payoffs are respectively the unchanged singleton
reward and the unchanged tail coordinate.  On the complementary event,
`(54.1)` adds `z` to both forced-action payoffs.  Taking expectations gives
`(54.2)`.  Other players' payoff coordinates were not altered, so all their
endpoint differences are literally unchanged.  The first identity in
`(54.3)` is definitional.  On each nonempty opponent coalition `S`, both
`r(S)_g` and `r(S union {g})_g` receive the same shift, so their joining
difference and its expectation `J_g` are unchanged.  The prescribed root
payoff receives `z` exactly on opponent absorption, independently of `g`'s
own sampled action, proving the successor identity.  QED.

In the setting of Proposition 53, `(53.5)` and the absolute joining bound
give `A_g>=a/(2M)>0`.  Hence the transformation can move the Bellman
successor coordinate in either direction while preserving every datum in
Proposition 53.  With strict punishment-floor slack and sufficiently small
`|z|`, floor admissibility also persists: changing one player's terminal
rewards by at most `|z|` changes that player's punishment value by at most
`|z|`, while the other punishment coordinates do not change.  Likewise a
strict terminal exploitability margin loses at most `2|z|`.  These robustness
remarks are local; they do not assert that the transformed table retains the
full paid frontier.

Proposition 54 is the sharp adapter boundary for the current attack.  A
uniform negative joining increment—even on an actual collision event of
fixed mass—contains only a **relative** join-versus-continue sign.  Payoff
re-entry needs an absolute source-matched level for the continuer outcome, or
a genuinely reached later exact edge which supplies the negative Bellman
motion.  Neither is encoded by the singleton gap, terminal-gap marginal cap,
or collision localization alone.

### 55. A literal semantic lift turns the gap into debt descent or source hazard

The artificial floor state in Proposition 50 need not be the payoff of an
actual continuation profile.  When it is, the exact prefix has an additional
all-behavior semantic account.  That account still does not orient payoff
motion, but it gives a sharp source-matched dichotomy.

Let `sigma` be an actual behavioral continuation profile, write

```text
U_i = prescribed terminal payoff of sigma,
B_i = unrestricted unilateral cap against sigma,
d_i = B_i-U_i,
```

and suppose `|r(S)_i|<=M`, `M>0`.  Fix `g` and `delta>0` with

```text
U_g <= r({g})_g-delta.                                (55.1)
```

Let `q` be an exact endpoint-Nash root at `U` whose `g` coordinate has
positive Continue probability.  Put

```text
O = product_(j!=g)(1-q_j),       A=1-O.              (55.2)
```

Let `sigma'` be the literal behavioral profile obtained by playing `q` at the
new first date and, after joint Continue, playing `sigma`.  Write `U',B',d'`
for its actual terminal semantic pair and debt.

**Proposition 55 (source debt-or-hazard dichotomy).**  The following exact
claims hold.

First, the root has a fixed **opponent** absorption bound, not merely a total
charge bound:

```text
A >= c := delta/(delta+2M).                           (55.3)
```

Second, the gap owner's literal semantic debt transports with equality,

```text
d'_g = O*d_g,                                        (55.4)
```

while every other debt coordinate weakly decreases.  Hence

```text
DebtSum(sigma') <= DebtSum(sigma)-A*d_g.             (55.5)
```

Finally let `A_src` be the probability that at least one opponent of `g`
Quits at `sigma`'s first live date.  Then

```text
delta <= d_g+2M*A_src.                               (55.6)
```

Consequently at least one of the following two source-matched alternatives
holds:

```text
d_g >= delta/2,
  DebtSum(sigma') <= DebtSum(sigma)
    - delta^2/(2(delta+2M));                          (55.7)

A_src >= delta/(4M).                                 (55.8)
```

**Proof.**  The exact outsider-Never decomposition at `g` is

```text
Delta_g(q)=O*(r({g})_g-U_g)+J_g(q).
```

Positive Continue support and exact endpoint Nash give `Delta_g(q)<=0`.
The joining bound gives `|J_g(q)|<=2M A`.  Using `(55.1)` and `O=1-A`,

```text
delta*(1-A) <= -J_g(q) <= 2M A,
```

which rearranges to `(55.3)`.

For `(55.4)`, let `Q_g,C_g` be the forced-Quit and forced-Continue values at
tail `U`.  Because Continue is supported, exact Nash makes
`C_g=max(Q_g,C_g)=U'_g`.  In the literal semantic prefix, the full behavioral
cap is

```text
B'_g=max(Q_g,C_g+O*d_g)=C_g+O*d_g,
```

where `d_g>=0` and `Q_g<=C_g`.  Subtracting `U'_g=C_g` proves `(55.4)`.
Exact-root prefixing weakly decreases every other nonnegative semantic debt
coordinate, so summing gives `(55.5)`.  This is the literal-profile form of
`quittingTerminalSemanticDebt_prefix_eq_blockAct` and
`quittingTerminalDeviationDebt_rootThenContinuation_le`.

For `(55.6)`, let `Q_src` be `g`'s payoff from pure Quit at the first date
against `sigma`'s actual opponents.  The unrestricted cap gives

```text
d_g>=Q_src-U_g.
```

On the event that every opponent Continues at that date, forced Quit pays the
solo reward.  On its complement the payoff differs from the solo reward by at
most `2M`.  Therefore

```text
r({g})_g-Q_src <= 2M*A_src.
```

Adding the last two inequalities and using `(55.1)` gives `(55.6)`.  If the
first arm `d_g>=delta/2` holds, combine it with `(55.3)--(55.5)` to get
`(55.7)`; otherwise `(55.6)` gives `(55.8)`.  QED.

Under the actual terminal-gap hypothesis and `U>=P`, reviewed Proposition 51
supplies the positive Continue premise for **every** exact root, quantitatively
`1-q_g>=gamma/(4M)`.  Thus Proposition 55 applies to every source-matched
realization of the Section 50 edge.  If `D_*` is the minimum total semantic
debt on the actual carrier, arm `(55.7)` also forces

```text
DebtSum(sigma)-D_* >= delta^2/(2(delta+2M)),          (55.9)
```

because `sigma'` is another actual profile.

This is a strict source qualification, not the payoff return.  Arm `(55.7)`
spends actual terminal-debt excess but does not say whether `U'_g-U_g` is
positive or negative: the missing sign can be absorbed by motion of the cap
`B'_g`.  Arm `(55.8)` is literal behavioral first-row opponent hazard in the
paid source, not absorption charge of an exact Bellman edge.  A producer must
therefore turn the source-hazard arm into an exact floor root or turn the
debt-descent arm into a reached payoff transfer.  Proposition 55 proves that
these are the only two ways a source-matched singleton gap avoids being a
purely artificial charged state.

Narrow source search used `terminal debt prefix`, `singleton descent`,
`opponent survival`, and `outsider joining`.  The closest named results are
the block-action identity in `Quitting/Root/TerminalSemanticPair.lean`, the
literal prefix inequality in `Quitting/Root/TerminalDebtPrefix.lean`, and the
coarser general singleton-descent estimate in
`Quitting/Boundary/Repair/TerminalDebtSingletonDescent.lean`.  The new content
is their exact Continue-supported combination with the source first-row
hazard alternative `(55.6)--(55.8)`.  No paid-return or Lean implementation is
claimed.

Independent falsification review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_55.md`
validates the opponent-absorption constant, exact literal cap recursion,
source Quit-now coupling (including simultaneous collisions), both dichotomy
constants, and the minimum-debt excess consequence.  It confirms the exact
scope: debt descent versus behavioral source hazard, with no exact-edge charge
or payoff return.

The attempted immediate bridge from the second arm is now closed at a clean
sign boundary.  In the earlier-receiving paid orientation, approximate
optimality controls the forced owner's refusal at the **actual** outsider row.
Passing to an outsider Nash completion changes that row nonperturbatively; by
Proposition 49, any exact forced-owner completion in the terminal-gap branch
has owner switch value below the punishment floor (with the `O=0` case handled
directly).  Therefore the source hazard and pre-correction refusal estimate do
not imply either Section 43's floor-safe desaturation inequality or Section
45's floor-safe support switch.  A valid next step must retain an absolute
owner continuation level through outsider Nashification, or produce a
different reached edge that repays the owner seam.  A relative paid
Quit-versus-wait comparison is insufficient.

### 56. Low-debt reversal of a full reset requires macroscopic opponent movement

There is an exact absolute-level obstruction on the debt-descent side which
does not use a one-stage root.  For behavioral profiles `sigma,tau` and player
`m`, write

```text
U_m(sigma) = prescribed terminal payoff,
B_m(sigma) = unrestricted unilateral cap,
d_m(sigma) = B_m(sigma)-U_m(sigma).
```

Define the uniform opponent-environment discrepancy

```text
E_m(sigma,tau)
  = sup over behavioral strategies alpha of m
      | payoff_m(alpha,sigma_-m)-payoff_m(alpha,tau_-m) |.       (56.1)
```

The elementary supremum inequality gives

```text
|B_m(sigma)-B_m(tau)| <= E_m(sigma,tau).                       (56.2)
```

**Proposition 56 (cross-opponent cost of low-debt negative re-entry).**
If `a>=0` and

```text
U_m(tau) <= U_m(sigma)-a,                                    (56.3)
```

then

```text
E_m(sigma,tau) >= a+d_m(sigma)-d_m(tau).                     (56.4)
```

In particular, if `d_m(tau)<=eta`, then

```text
E_m(sigma,tau) >= a-eta.                                    (56.5)
```

If rewards are bounded in absolute value by `M>0` and, for every strategy of
`m`, the two induced terminal-outcome laws admit a coupling whose mismatch
probability is at most the same number `epsilon`, then

```text
E_m(sigma,tau)<=2M*epsilon.                                  (56.6)
```

Consequently `a>eta` forces

```text
epsilon >= (a-eta)/(2M).                                    (56.7)
```

**Proof.**  By the definitions of terminal debt and `(56.3)`,

```text
B_m(sigma)-B_m(tau)
 = U_m(sigma)+d_m(sigma)-U_m(tau)-d_m(tau)
 >= a+d_m(sigma)-d_m(tau).
```

Combine this with `(56.2)` to obtain `(56.4)`, then use nonnegativity of
`d_m(sigma)` for `(56.5)`.  Under the coupling hypothesis, the two payoff
random variables agree off the mismatch event and differ by at most `2M` on
it, uniformly in `alpha`; taking the supremum gives `(56.6)` and hence
`(56.7)`.  QED.

Apply this to the actual paid full replacement.  At rank `n`, write `P_n` for
the source profile and `F_n` for its full replacement by mover `m`.  Their
opponents are literally identical, and the exact own-coordinate identity is

```text
G_n=U_m(F_n)-U_m(P_n)=d_m(P_n)-d_m(F_n),
d_m(F_n)<=lambda_n^2.                                      (56.8)
```

Thus the literal reverse reset `F_n -> P_n` saturates `(56.4)` with
`E_m(F_n,P_n)=0`: its payoff loss `G_n` is paid exactly by recreating the old
mover debt.  Since `G_n` tends to the positive base debt, this is not a
low-debt re-entry.  More generally, any source-matched profile `T_n` with

```text
U_m(T_n)<=U_m(F_n)-G_n+o(1),       d_m(T_n)=o(1)              (56.9)
```

must have

```text
liminf E_m(F_n,T_n) >= lim G_n > 0.                          (56.10)
```

So the only absolute negative transfer visibly present in the paid source is
strategically unusable: holding opponents fixed turns the whole transfer into
terminal debt.  Nashifying it while keeping the mover near its cap requires a
nonperturbative change of opponents, uniformly over every mover deviation.
This is stronger than loss of terminal-law provenance and is exactly the
two-reset/cross-player correction absent from the current source interface.

The constants and both alternatives are sharp already with two players.  Give
`m` solo reward one and reward zero on every other terminal coalition; give
the other player zero everywhere.  With the opponent Never, changing `m` from
immediate solo Quit to Never lowers its payoff by one, leaves the opponent
environment unchanged, and creates debt exactly one.  If instead the opponent
Quits immediately while `m` Never, the payoff still falls by one but the new
debt is zero; the environment discrepancy is exactly one, witnessed by `m`'s
immediate-Quit strategy.  Thus the loss is paid either as target debt or as
the full cross-opponent discrepancy in `(56.4)`.

The proposition is ordinary mathematics.  Narrow source comparison used
`fullReplacementPrescribedGain_eq_sourceDebt_sub_endpointDebt` and

fixed-opponent cap invariance underlying

or payoff return is inferred: `(56.7)` is a necessary opponent-movement cost,
not a producer of that movement.

### 57. A stable off-diagonal transfer chain must contain a negative cross-player step

The missing opponent movement would be forced by a genuinely serial version
of the full-replacement source.  The right account is off-diagonal debt
transfer, not repeated growth of bounded total debt.

Let `sigma^0,...,sigma^L` be actual behavioral profiles on `n>=2` players.
Suppose step `t` updates only player `m_t`'s strategy.  Write

```text
U_i^t = prescribed terminal payoff at sigma^t,
B_i^t = unrestricted unilateral cap at sigma^t,
d_i^t = B_i^t-U_i^t,
C_t   = sum_i B_i^t.
```

Because the opponents of `m_t` are unchanged,
`B_(m_t)^(t+1)=B_(m_t)^t`.  Assume rewards are bounded in absolute value by
`M` and for some `h>0` every step transfers at least `h` units of debt into
the other coordinates:

```text
sum_(i!=m_t) [d_i^(t+1)-d_i^t] >= h.                    (57.1)
```

**Proposition 57 (cap-capacity forcing of negative payoff transport).**  For
every `nu<h/(n-1)`, if every outsider at every step satisfies

```text
U_i^(t+1) >= U_i^t-nu       for i!=m_t,                 (57.2)
```

then

```text
L*(h-(n-1)*nu) <= 2*n*M.                                (57.3)
```

Consequently, if

```text
L > 4*n*M/h,                                             (57.4)
```

some step `t<L` and some outsider `i!=m_t` satisfy

```text
U_i^(t+1) < U_i^t-h/(2*(n-1)).                          (57.5)
```

Thus a sufficiently long stable reset chain contains a literal negative
cross-player payoff transfer.

**Proof.**  Expanding `(57.1)` and using `(57.2)` gives

```text
sum_(i!=m_t) [B_i^(t+1)-B_i^t]
  >= h-(n-1)*nu.                                         (57.6)
```

The updated coordinate has zero cap change, so the left side of `(57.6)` is
exactly `C_(t+1)-C_t`.  Summing over `t<L` yields `(57.3)` because every
unilateral cap lies in `[-M,M]`, hence `C_L-C_0<=2*n*M`.  If `(57.5)` failed,
`(57.2)` would hold with `nu=h/(2*(n-1))`, and `(57.3)` would contradict
`(57.4)`.  QED.

For the actual positive-total-slope full replacement, the one-step data have
the right sign.  Its mover debt falls by the mover payoff gain, while total
debt rises above the minimum; hence the sum of outsider debt changes is the
total-debt rise plus that gain and is uniformly positive along a late
subsequence.  But those endpoints were selected from independent source
profiles converging to the same base.  Nothing in
`PositiveTotalSlopeFullReplacement` restarts the same construction at the
reached endpoint or preserves `(57.1)` in a serial chain.  Proposition 57
therefore turns two-reset stability into an exact quantitative producer: more
than `4nM/h` reached stable resets would force a negative outsider payoff step
of size `h/(2(n-1))`.  One would still have to Nashify that step into the
punishment-floor exact relation and close the other payoff coordinates.  No
such serial adapter is asserted here.

This is ordinary mathematics.  The only source comparison is with the
`totalDebt_separated`, `mover_excess_eq`, and `prescribedGain_eventually`
fields of `PositiveTotalSlopeFullReplacement`; those fields describe one
base-to-endpoint family and do not imply the serial hypothesis `(57.1)`.

### 58. Independent positive-transfer resets need not concatenate once

The serial hypothesis of Proposition 57 is not a formal consequence of
having every reset column at one common source, even when each replacement is
an exact best response and each independent endpoint has all the one-step
signs used in the positive-total-slope comparison.

Take two players `a,b`, and let both use `Never` at the common source.  Give
the three terminal coalitions the payoff vectors

```text
r({a})   =(1,0),
r({b})   =(0,1),
r({a,b}) =(3,3).                                         (58.1)
```

Let `Q_a,Q_b` denote immediate Quit.  The source `NN`, the two independent
full resets, and the serial double reset have semantic data

```text
profile   U       B       d=B-U    D=sum d
NN        (0,0)   (1,1)   (1,1)      2
Q_a N     (1,0)   (1,3)   (0,3)      3
N Q_b     (0,1)   (3,1)   (3,0)      3
Q_a Q_b   (3,3)   (3,3)   (0,0)      0.                (58.2)
```

These values use unrestricted behavioral caps, not merely the two immediate
actions.  Against an opponent who Never Quits, a player's best payoff is its
solo reward one.  Against an opponent who Quits immediately, the only
possible improvement over Continuing is to Quit simultaneously, which pays
three.  Later pure times and Never give the displayed Continue payoff zero.

**Proposition 58 (failure of serial transfer from independent reset data).**
At the common source, each independent update has all of the following:

```text
updated-player prescribed gain                 =1,
updated-player endpoint debt                   =0,
total semantic-debt increase                   =1,
other-player semantic-debt increase            =2.       (58.3)
```

The immediate-Quit replacement is an exact best response at both the source
and its independent endpoint.  Nevertheless, after the reached first reset
`NN -> Q_a N`, applying the same selected replacement for `b` gives

```text
Q_a N -> Q_a Q_b,
d_a(Q_a Q_b)-d_a(Q_a N)=0,                              (58.4)
```

not the independent outsider transfer two.  In fact total debt falls by
three.  Thus no positive `h` in Proposition 57's serial hypothesis follows
from the two independent base-to-endpoint comparisons, their own-debt bounds,
their positive own gains, or their individual total-debt separations.

**Proof.**  The cap calculation preceding the proposition gives `(58.2)`.
Subtracting the rows proves `(58.3)--(58.4)`.  Immediate Quit attains the cap
one at `NN` and the cap three against the other player's immediate Quit, so
the replacement optimality statements are exact.  QED.

The scope limitation is decisive.  `Q_a Q_b` is itself an exact terminal Nash
profile, so this table has minimum total semantic debt zero and cannot be a
positive-minimum frontier.  The regression therefore does not refute a
serial theorem using the full terminal-gap/no-sure package.  It proves that
such a theorem must use that package substantively: the common-base tangent
family and even exact one-step best-response resets do not supply seriality.
Equivalently, the next paid argument must either show that transfer erasure
creates a solved exact terminal sink in general, or use the terminal gap to
force a different reached reset/root with retained off-diagonal transfer.

This is ordinary mathematics and a strict strengthening of Section 7's
one-sided debt-instability example.  Section 7 showed that one observer update
can destroy a mover's low debt.  Here both columns separately satisfy the
positive transfer/separation signs, while their literal reached composition
destroys the transfer required by Proposition 57.
