### 59. The terminal gap forces payoff loss or cap escalation across the full reset cube

The terminal gap can force a macroscopic cross-opponent move without assuming
that the individual off-diagonal transfers concatenate.  The resulting path
is literal in behavioral-profile space, though still not a path of exact
Nash--Bellman roots.

Let a positive-minimum tangent family have source profile `sigma_r`, scale
`lambda_r`, active support `A`, and selected replacement `R_(r,j)` for every
`j in A`.  Define the simultaneous full-replacement profile

```text
G_r(j)=R_(r,j)     if j in A,
G_r(j)=sigma_r(j)  if j notin A.                       (59.1)
```

For each player `j`, define its matching low-debt anchor

```text
H_(r,j)=update(sigma_r,j,R_(r,j))   if j in A,
H_(r,j)=sigma_r                     if j notin A.       (59.2)
```

The profiles `H_(r,j)` and `G_r` have literally the same strategy in
coordinate `j`.  Moreover there is a reached finite unilateral-reset word
from `H_(r,j)` to `G_r`: if `j in A`, update `j` first and then the remaining
active coordinates; if `j notin A`, start at `sigma_r` and update every
active coordinate.  Distinct-coordinate updates commute, so the endpoint is
exactly `(59.1)`.

Write `d_j(P)=B_j(P)-U_j(P)`.  Put

```text
eta_r=max(lambda_r^2,
          max_(j notin A) d_j(sigma_r)),                (59.3)
```

with the second maximum taken as zero when there are no inactive players.
The replacement endpoint field and source convergence give

```text
d_j(H_(r,j))<=eta_r  for every j,       eta_r->0.       (59.4)
```

For two profiles with the same `j` strategy, retain the opponent-environment
discrepancy `E_j` from `(56.1)`.

**Proposition 59 (full-reset payoff-loss/cap-escalation dichotomy).**  Assume
`gamma>0` and `HasTerminalExploitabilityGap reward gamma`.  At every rank
with `eta_r<gamma`, there is a player `j` such that, with

```text
a_r=gamma-eta_r>0,
```

one of the following holds:

```text
U_j(G_r) <= U_j(H_(r,j))-a_r/2;                         (59.5)

B_j(G_r) >= B_j(H_(r,j))+a_r/2.                         (59.6)
```

In either case

```text
E_j(H_(r,j),G_r) >= a_r/2.                              (59.7)
```

Consequently, eventually the same conclusions hold with the fixed lower
bound `gamma/4` in place of `a_r/2`.

**Proof.**  Apply the terminal exploitability gap to the actual profile
`G_r`.  It selects `j` and a deviation gaining at least `gamma`, hence

```text
d_j(G_r)>=gamma.                                        (59.8)
```

Together with `(59.4)`,

```text
[B_j(G_r)-B_j(H_(r,j))]
  -[U_j(G_r)-U_j(H_(r,j))]
 =d_j(G_r)-d_j(H_(r,j)) >= a_r.                         (59.9)
```

If `(59.6)` fails, `(59.9)` forces `(59.5)`; otherwise `(59.6)` holds.
Because the two profiles use the same own strategy, their prescribed-payoff
difference is the payoff difference for one admissible fixed strategy and is
therefore bounded in absolute value by `E_j`.  The supremum inequality
`|B_j(G_r)-B_j(H_(r,j))|<=E_j` also holds.  Either alternative thus gives
`(59.7)`.  Finally `eta_r->0`, so eventually `a_r>=gamma/2`.  QED.

The active part of `(59.4)` is the literal
`replacement_moverDebt_le_tolerance` field.  For inactive `j`, base debt is
zero by definition of `A`, and semantic source convergence gives
`d_j(sigma_r)->0`; finiteness makes the maximum vanish.  Thus no unrecorded
uniformity is used.

This is the first source-matched theorem here which **produces** the
macroscopic opponent movement required abstractly by Proposition 56.  Its
two outputs are nevertheless upstream of the maintained consumer.  In
`(59.5)` the destination `G_r` has large debt in coordinate `j`, not the
low-debt endpoint required for a Nashified negative return.  In `(59.6)` the
absolute cap rises, but the theorem does not select the profitable deviation
as an exact floor root.  Every displayed edge is a unilateral reset of an
actual behavioral profile, not an exact Nash--Bellman edge.  The next bridge
is therefore precise: either convert the reached payoff-loss arm into a
floor-root path while discharging `j`'s final debt, or turn the cap-escalation
arm into a new paid row/exact charged root and prove payoff re-entry.

Narrow source comparison: the simultaneous subset profiles and their literal
one-coordinate insertions are the ordinary content of
`quittingStoppingLawMixtureSubsetProfile_insert` in
`TerminalSemanticSimultaneousResetOrientationLocalization.lean`; the present
argument instead uses the full selected replacements and the terminal-gap

### 60. A bounded best-response walk forces a low-debt loss or a new paid recovery row

The cap-escalation alternative of Proposition 59 can be routed into an actual
reached payoff transfer without assuming that any tangent-family replacement
remains stable after another player moves.  This uses the terminal gap again,
but it still produces behavioral profiles rather than exact floor-Nash roots.

Let there be `n>=2` players, let every terminal reward coordinate have absolute
value at most `M`, and assume `gamma>0` and
`HasTerminalExploitabilityGap reward gamma`.  Fix

```text
0<epsilon<gamma/2,       g=gamma-epsilon,               (60.1)
```

and an integer `L` satisfying the tolerance-independent bound

```text
L*gamma>8*n*M.                                            (60.2)
```

Starting at an arbitrary behavioral profile `sigma^0`, construct a literal
unilateral path `sigma^0,...,sigma^L` as follows.  At `sigma^t`, the terminal
gap selects some player `m_t` with debt at least `gamma`.  Choose an
`epsilon`-best response for that player and update only its strategy.  Then

```text
U_(m_t)^(t+1)-U_(m_t)^t >= g,
d_(m_t)(sigma^(t+1)) <= epsilon.                         (60.3)
```

The weak first inequality can be made strict by choosing a strategy strictly
within `epsilon` of the supremum; its weak form is all that is used.

**Proposition 60 (bounded reached cross-player loss).**  Along every path
constructed above there are a step `t<L` and an outsider `j!=m_t` such that

```text
U_j^(t+1) < U_j^t-g/(2*(n-1)).                           (60.4)
```

In particular the loss is greater than `gamma/(4*(n-1))`, independently of
`epsilon` and of the starting profile.

**Proof.**  Put

```text
X_t=sum_(i!=m_t) (U_i^(t+1)-U_i^t).
```

Since `g>gamma/2`, condition `(60.2)` implies `L*g>4*n*M`.
Every payoff lies in `[-M,M]`, so telescoping the total prescribed payoff and
using `(60.3)` gives

```text
sum_(t<L) X_t
 =sum_i(U_i^L-U_i^0)
   -sum_(t<L)(U_(m_t)^(t+1)-U_(m_t)^t)
 <=2*n*M-L*g
 <-L*g/2.                                                (60.5)
```

Thus some `X_t<-g/2`.  At that step at least one of its `n-1` outsider
summands is strictly below `-g/(2(n-1))`, proving `(60.4)`.  Since
`g>gamma/2`, the final fixed bound follows.  QED.

There is a useful exact two-step refinement.  For the step selected in
Proposition 60, write

```text
P=sigma^t,       Q=sigma^(t+1),
delta=g/(2*(n-1)),                                      (60.6)
```

so `U_j(Q)<U_j(P)-delta`.  Fix any `0<theta<delta/4`, choose a
`theta`-best response of `j` at `Q`, and call the resulting reached profile
`R`.  Opponents of `j` are unchanged between `Q` and `R`, so

```text
d_j(R)<=theta,       B_j(R)=B_j(Q).                     (60.7)
```

Exactly one of the following (with harmless equality assigned to the first
arm) occurs:

```text
B_j(Q)<=U_j(P)-delta/2
  => U_j(R)<=U_j(P)-delta/2 and d_j(R)<=theta;           (60.8)

B_j(Q)>U_j(P)-delta/2
  => U_j(R)-U_j(Q)>delta/2-theta>delta/4.                (60.9)
```

Thus the terminal gap produces, after a uniformly bounded number of reached
unilateral moves, either a fixed negative payoff transfer ending at
arbitrarily small debt for the harmed player, or a new reached paid recovery
row of fixed gain whose endpoint has arbitrarily small mover debt.

The distinction from Proposition 57 is important.  No off-diagonal debt
transfer is assumed or iterated, so the Section 58 erasure regression is
irrelevant.  The cost is that neither `(60.8)` nor `(60.9)` is an exact
punishment-floor Nash--Bellman edge.  In the first arm the low-debt endpoint
is the desired absolute-level negative re-entry, but it has not been
Nashified simultaneously for the other players.  In the second arm the paid
gain has been recreated at an actually reached source, but it lacks the
first-disagreement block structure and a floor-admissible charged root.  The
remaining bridge is therefore no longer existence of a reached negative
transfer: it is exact floor-root conversion of one of these two reached
two-step alternatives while preserving its absolute payoff level.

This is ordinary mathematics.  The only source facts used are the definition
of `HasTerminalExploitabilityGap`, the unrestricted terminal cap as a
supremum over the nonempty behavioral-strategy set, and the uniform reward
bound.  No compactness or attainment of the cap is assumed; `epsilon`- and
`theta`-best responses suffice.

### 61. Positive survival makes cap Nashification uniformly nonsemantic

There is a quantitative obstruction to the most direct conversion of
Proposition 60's reached profiles into exact floor edges.  It uses the
terminal gap twice: once to force positive semantic debt at every actual
profile, and once through Proposition 51 to keep every exact floor root's
joint Continue mass uniformly positive.

Let `sigma` be any actual behavioral profile and write its terminal semantic
pair as `(U,B)`, with debt vector `d=B-U`.  Let `V` be any boxed tail above
the punishment floor, and let `q` be an exact endpoint-Nash root at `V`.
There are two different payoff vectors associated with the same product row:

```text
X^act  = Succ(U,q),       the payoff of the literal profile q then sigma;
X^edge = Succ(V,q),       the current payoff of the exact Bellman edge.       (61.1)
```

The exact successor-shift identity gives, coordinatewise,

```text
X^edge-X^act=C(q)*(V-U),                                  (61.2)
```

where `C(q)=product_i(1-q_i)` is joint Continue mass.

Assume rewards have absolute value at most `M`, `gamma>0`, and
`HasTerminalExploitabilityGap reward gamma`.  Put

```text
kappa=(gamma/(4M))^n>0.                                  (61.3)
```

As in Proposition 51, the positive gap implies `M>0`, `gamma<=4M`, and every
exact boxed floor root satisfies

```text
C(q)>=kappa.                                             (61.4)
```

**Proposition 61 (uniform semantic-to-edge seam).**  Under these hypotheses,
every actual profile `sigma`, boxed floor tail `V`, and exact root `q` at `V`
satisfy

```text
||X^edge-X^act||_infinity
  =C(q)*||V-U||_infinity
  >=kappa*||V-U||_infinity.                              (61.5)
```

In particular the cap vector `B` is itself boxed and above punishment.  If
`q` is any exact cap-Nash root at `B`, then

```text
||Succ(B,q)-Succ(U,q)||_infinity>=kappa*gamma.            (61.6)
```

Likewise, define

```text
floorDef(U)=max_i max(0,P_i-U_i).
```

Every boxed floor tail `V>=P` satisfies

```text
||X^edge-X^act||_infinity>=kappa*floorDef(U).             (61.7)
```

**Proof.**  Equation `(61.2)` is
`quittingRootSuccessorPayoff_sub_eq_continueMass_mul`, applied to tails `V`
and `U`.  Since `C(q)>=0`, taking the sup norm gives the equality in `(61.5)`;
Proposition 51 gives `(61.4)`.  For `(61.6)`, apply the terminal gap to
`sigma`.  Some unilateral deviation gains at least `gamma`, so some semantic
debt coordinate satisfies `d_j>=gamma`.  Hence
`||B-U||_infinity>=gamma`, and `(61.5)` with `V=B` finishes the proof.  QED.
For `(61.7)`, choose a coordinate attaining the finite maximum.  If its floor
deficit is positive, `V_i-U_i>=P_i-U_i`; if the deficit is zero the claim is
trivial.  Now apply `(61.5)`.

This changes the Nashification boundary materially.  The Section 50 charged
edge can be semantically realized when its tail `U` is the payoff of an
actual continuation `sigma`: prefixing `sigma` by its root gives the literal
profile with payoff `X^act`.  Proposition 60 may therefore start at that
actual prefixed profile; no latent-plan or terminal-law port is missing.
But a later attempt to exactify one of its reached profiles by moving from
the prescribed tail to the semantic cap pays the fixed seam `(61.6)`.  More
generally, an exact edge whose current payoff is within `eta` of the literal
prefix payoff must use a tail satisfying

```text
||V-U||_infinity<=eta/kappa.                              (61.8)
```

Thus the remaining producer must find roots at tails genuinely close to the
reached prescribed payoff, or use additional exact edges to repay a fixed
seam.  Cap seeding is not an absolute-level Nashification mechanism in the
terminal-gap branch.  This does not exclude an exact root directly at a
floor-safe `U`, and it does not exclude a multi-edge return.  Section 27
shows that a paid gain and low mover debt alone do not produce the former;
Section 33 shows that nearby tails need not retain positive charge.  The new
content is the uniform lower bound showing that the cap-tail shortcut is not
even asymptotically level-preserving for **any** actual profile under the
positive terminal gap.  Formula `(61.7)` also shows that a below-floor
behavioral endpoint cannot be exactified at the same absolute level unless
its entire floor deficit tends to zero.  The small debt of only the harmed
coordinate supplied by Proposition 60 is insufficient.

The source comparison is exact.  Equation `(61.2)` is the named successor
identity already used in Section 38, while `(61.4)` is the reviewed explicit
no-near-sure theorem of Section 51.  The fact `B>=P` is the definition of the
punishment value as the worst opponent environment for the unilateral cap.
No exact-root producer or Lean implementation is claimed.

validates the successor-shift orientation and norm equality, the uniform

### 62. Multi-debtor minima expel fixed charge from every near-prescribed tail

Proposition 61 leaves open exact roots at floor tails genuinely close to the
reached prescribed payoff.  On the actual tangent source this local route is
impossible whenever the positive minimum has at least two positive-debt
coordinates.

Let `base=(U_*,B_*)` be a carrier point minimizing total terminal semantic
debt, and suppose distinct players `a,b` satisfy

```text
d_a(base)>0,       d_b(base)>0.                          (62.1)
```

Let `sigma_r` be any actual behavioral profiles whose semantic pairs converge
to `base`; write their prescribed payoffs as `U_r`.  Let `V_r` be boxed floor
tails and `q_r` exact endpoint-Nash roots at `V_r`, with

```text
||V_r-U_r||_infinity -> 0.                               (62.2)
```

**Proposition 62 (vanishing charge near a multi-debtor minimum).**  Under
these hypotheses,

```text
absorption(q_r) -> 0.                                    (62.3)
```

**Proof.**  If `(62.3)` failed, pass to a subsequence on which absorption is
bounded below by some `c>0`.  The finite product-root simplex is compact, so
pass again until `q_r->q_*`.  Source convergence and `(62.2)` give
`V_r->U_*`.  The exact endpoint-Nash graph is closed, hence `q_*` is an exact
root at `U_*`; continuity of absorption gives
`absorption(q_*)>=c`.

Apply
`quittingTerminalSemantic_minimum_positiveDebt_opponents_pureContinue` to
`base`, `q_*`, and debtor `a`.  Every player other than `a`, including `b`,
Continues purely in `q_*`.  Apply it again to debtor `b`; now `a` also
Continues purely.  Thus every player Continues, so `absorption(q_*)=0`, a
contradiction.  QED.

This is stronger than the absence of one selected local root: every
fixed-charge family at tails asymptotic to the actual source payoff is ruled
out, regardless of changing supports or roots.  Together with Proposition
61 it gives an exact three-way boundary on the multi-debtor paid source:

1. lifting toward the cap or floor by a nonvanishing amount creates a
   nonvanishing payoff seam;
2. keeping the exact root tail asymptotic to the prescribed source forces its
   absorption charge to vanish; therefore
3. a successful fixed-charge path must put its high-charge edge at a
   genuinely nonlocal tail and later repay the excursion through additional
   exact edges.

The conclusion is conditional on `(62.1)`.  A unique positive-debt owner may
support a singleton-owner boundary root and is not dispatched here.  Nor does
the proposition rule out a path whose endpoints return in payoff while its
charged edge lies far from the source.  Those are precisely the surviving
unique-support and nonlocal multi-edge mechanisms.

The actual potential-co-decrease derivation does select a mover and a distinct
companion in the positive-debt support of `frontier.base`.  This does **not**
make Proposition 62 apply to the paid rows: those rows are based at full-
replacement profiles whose semantic pairs converge to the strictly off-
minimum `endpoint.cluster`, not to `frontier.base`.  The cluster can have a
different, possibly unique, positive-debt support.  Thus Proposition 62
excludes fixed local charge along the original tangent source when its
minimum is multi-debtor; transporting that exclusion to the off-minimum paid
source would require an additional support/debt comparison which the source
theorem does not state.

Narrow source comparison found the coordinate rigidity theorem in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Root/TerminalSemanticEqualityStratum.lean` and
the closed endpoint-Nash graph used throughout the root compactness files.
The asymptotic fixed-charge exclusion above is their direct source-facing
composition; no new Lean declaration or broader tight-face theorem is
claimed.

validates the compact root limit, closed-graph passage, and two applications
of minimum-debt rigidity forcing the limit root to all-Continue.  It confirms

multi-edge excursions remain open.

### 63. Curvature either survives as paid-endpoint debt or is already a negative tangent

The correction after Proposition 62 raises one precise source question.  The
paid observer is selected by a positive curvature coordinate at the
off-minimum full-replacement cluster, but positive curvature is not itself
positive endpoint debt.  The exact identity separates the two possibilities.

Let `C` be the selected full-replacement cluster, let `B0` be the minimum
base, and let `m` be the reset mover.  Under flatness and strict separation,
choose the observer `o!=m` supplied by
`FullReplacementCluster.exists_curvature_ge_opponentAverage`, and put

```text
c = curvature_C(o)>0,
d0 = debt(B0,o)>=0,
dC = debt(C,o)>=0,
t  = tangent(m,o).
```

By the definition of normalized curvature,

```text
dC = d0+t+c.                                             (63.1)
```

**Proposition 63 (paid-coordinate debt/tangent dichotomy).**  For this same
observer and curvature, at least one of the following holds:

```text
dC >= c/2;                                               (63.2)
t  <= -c/2.                                              (63.3)
```

In the second arm `o` necessarily belongs to the positive-debt support of
the minimum base.  Thus the paid coordinate is a quantitatively positive
endpoint debtor in the first arm, or a genuine active base debtor with a
negative reset tangent in the second; it cannot be debt-inactive at both
ports.

In the first arm there is a fixed data-bearing curvature-paid witness at all
sufficiently late ranks whose receiving pure-time strategy improves the
literal full-replacement profile's prescribed payoff for `o` by at least
`c/4`.  More explicitly, one may take

```text
gain=c/4,       sourceBudget=c/8,       endpointError=c/8,   (63.4)
```

so that the source witness is within `lambda_r*c/8` of the source cap, the
receiving witness is within `c/8` of the full-replacement cap, and

```text
payoff(receiving witness at F_r,o)-U_o(F_r) >= c/4         (63.5)
```

eventually.  In the second arm the literal normalized semantic-debt direction
of the same observer along the selected source/reset mixture is eventually at
most `-c/4`:

```text
[debt(mix(sigma_r,R_(r,m),lambda_r),o)-debt(sigma_r,o)]
  /lambda_r <= -c/4.                                    (63.6)
```

Writing

```text
E=D(C)-D(B0)>0,       N=|I\{m}|,
```

the curvature selection has `c>=E/N`.  Consequently the same alternatives
have the source-level constants

```text
dC>=E/(2N) and receiving improvement >=E/(4N),
or
t<=-E/(2N).                                             (63.7)
```

**Proof.**  If `(63.2)` fails, then `(63.1)` and `d0>=0` give

```text
t=dC-d0-c < -c/2,
```

which implies `(63.3)`.  This proves the algebraic dichotomy.

If the second arm held with `d0=0`, the tangent-family inactive-coordinate
inequality would give `t>=0`, contradicting `t<=-c/2<0`.  Hence `d0>0`, which
is exactly membership in the base positive-debt support.

For the first arm, the full-replacement semantic pairs converge to `C`, so
their `o`-debts converge to `dC`.  Hence eventually their debt is at least
`3c/8`.  The parameter sum in `(63.4)` is `c/2<c`.  The parameterized
normalized-curvature decoder therefore supplies a carrier whose receiving
witness payoff is at least the full-replacement cap minus `c/8`.  Subtracting
the prescribed payoff gives at least

```text
3c/8-c/8=c/4,
```

which is `(63.5)`.  In the second arm, convergence of the literal normalized
debt direction to `t<=-c/2` gives `(63.6)` eventually.  QED.

The quantitative reformulation `(63.7)` follows from the defining lower
bound in `exists_curvature_ge_opponentAverage`; its denominator is the actual
nonempty opponent set, not the ambient player count.

**Corollary 63A (tolerance-dependent reached paid recovery).**  In the
endpoint-debt arm, for every `0<eta<=c/8` and all sufficiently late ranks one
may replace `endpointError` in `(63.4)` by `eta`.  If `tau_r` is the resulting
receiving pure-time strategy and

```text
R_r=update(F_r,o,tau_r),
```

then this is one literal reached unilateral update satisfying

```text
U_o(R_r)-U_o(F_r)>=c/4,       debt_o(R_r)<=eta.          (63.8)
```

Indeed `c/4+c/8+eta<=c/2<c`, so the raw curvature budget remains valid.  The
receiving payoff is at least `B_o(F_r)-eta`; eventual endpoint debt at least
`3c/8` and `eta<=c/8` give the payoff gain.  Updating only `o` leaves its
opponent environment, hence its behavioral cap, unchanged.  The second
inequality in `(63.8)` follows immediately.  Thus the endpoint-debt arm
supplies the strongest reached behavioral port one could ask for before
simultaneous Nashification: fixed absolute payoff gain and arbitrarily small
debt for the updater.  It still supplies no exact root conditions for the
other players and no punishment-floor Bellman edge.

This proposition is deliberately not a Nashification theorem.  In `(63.5)`
only one pure-time deviation is near-optimal; simultaneous outsider and owner
root conditions, punishment floors, and exact edge charge remain absent.  In
`(63.6)` the debt decrease is of order `lambda_r` and occurs on independently
selected source mixtures, not on a reached serial chain; Proposition 58 shows
why it cannot simply be accumulated.  The result does, however, remove one
unsupported shortcut from the current boundary: positive paid curvature does
not force the observer into the endpoint positive-debt support.  Failure of
that support conclusion is exactly paid for by a negative tangent entry in
the same coordinate.

The declarations used are
`FullReplacementCluster.curvature`,
`FullReplacementCluster.normalizedCurvature_tendsto`,
`FullReplacementCluster.exists_curvature_ge_opponentAverage`, and the
data-preserving `exists_quittingStoppingLawCurvaturePaidWitness` in
`StoppingLaw/Endpoint/NormalizedCurvaturePaidRow.lean` and
`NormalizedCurvatureStrategicDispatch.lean`.  This is ordinary mathematics;
no new formal declaration or consumer is claimed.

Independent falsification review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_63.md`
validates the curvature identity and dichotomy, raw-carrier parameter budget,
endpoint recovery and negative-direction constants, base-support implication,
and `E/N` reformulation.  It confirms that no strategic-dispatch threshold is
needed for the raw carrier and that neither arm is yet an exact chronology.

### 64. A flat minimum-compatible reset ray can spend only vanishing negative debt

The negative-tangent arm of Proposition 63 still does not imply a fixed
reached transfer by subdividing the selected full replacement.  The following
fixed reward table makes the obstruction exact.

Take four players `o,m,p,h` and a parameter `0<lambda<=1/4`.  In the source
profile, `o` and `h` Quit surely at date `1`, `m` Never Quits, and `p` Quits at
date `0` with probability `lambda` and otherwise Never.  The full replacement
for mover `m` is sure Quit at date `0`.  Along the literal ray, let `m` Quit at
date `0` with probability `s in [0,1]` and otherwise Never.

All unspecified reward coordinates and cells are zero.  The relevant rewards
are as follows.

For observer `o`,

```text
r_o({m})=r_o({p})=r_o({m,p})=r_o({o,h})=0,
r_o({o})=r_o({o,m})=r_o({h})=1,
r_o({o,p})=r_o({o,m,p})=-1.                              (64.1)
```

For mover `m`,

```text
r_m({m})=r_m({m,p})=1,
r_m({p})=r_m({o,h})=0.                                   (64.2)
```

For `p`,

```text
r_p({p})=r_p({m,p})=1,
r_p({m})=-2,       r_p({o,h})=0.                         (64.3)
```

For `h`,

```text
r_h({m})=r_h({p})=r_h({m,p})=r_h({o,h})=r_h({o})=0,
r_h({h})=r_h({h,m})=0,
r_h({h,p})=2,      r_h({h,m,p})=3.                       (64.4)
```

The displayed source strategies are also used at every point of the ray,
except for `m`'s marginal `s`.

Pure-time enumeration gives the following prescribed payoff, cap, and debt
functions.  For `o`, its prescribed date-1 payoff is zero.  Quit at date zero
has value `1-2lambda`, while waiting beyond date one has value
`(1-s)(1-lambda)`.  Hence

```text
U_o(s)=0,
d_o(s)=max(1-2lambda,(1-s)(1-lambda)).                   (64.5)
```

For the other three players,

```text
U_m(s)=s,                    B_m(s)=1,
d_m(s)=1-s;

U_p(s)=lambda-2(1-lambda)s, B_p(s)=1,
d_p(s)=1-lambda+2(1-lambda)s;

U_h(s)=0,                    B_h(s)=2lambda+s lambda,
d_h(s)=2lambda+s lambda.                                   (64.6)
```

For example, `h`'s Quit-zero payoff is `2lambda+s lambda` because its reward
is `2` when only `p` joins and `3` when both `m,p` join.  Every other pure
time has the prescribed value zero.  The analogous endpoint comparisons in
`(64.5)--(64.6)` show that the displayed caps are unrestricted behavioral
caps, not merely values over a selected menu.

At the source `s=0`, the debt vector is

```text
(1-lambda, 1, 1-lambda, 2lambda),
```

so total debt is exactly `3`.  At the tangent-family mixture `s=lambda`, the
normalized debt direction is exactly

```text
(-(1-lambda), -1, 2(1-lambda), lambda),                 (64.7)
```

whose coordinate sum is zero for every `lambda`.  It converges to the flat
column `(-1,-1,2,0)`.  The limiting base has debt vector `(1,1,1,0)`, so the
reset mover is genuinely active, the only inactive coordinate has zero
tangent, and the full replacement has zero mover debt.

The full endpoint `s=1` has debt vector

```text
(1-2lambda,0,3(1-lambda),3lambda),
```

with total debt `4-2lambda`, converging to a strict excess of one above the
limiting level `3`.  The observer's limiting endpoint debt is still one, its
tangent is `-1`, and its normalized curvature is one.
Thus both arms of Proposition 63 hold in this regression; that dichotomy is
inclusive, and the negative-tangent obstruction remains relevant even when
the observer also retains positive endpoint debt.

**Proposition 64 (vanishing monotone-ray accumulation).**  Along the entire
literal reset ray,

```text
D(s)=3                                           if s<=lambda/(1-lambda),
D(s)=3-lambda+s(1-lambda)                        otherwise.       (64.8)
```

In particular `D(s)>=3` for every `s in [0,1]`: the ray never undercuts its
limiting total-debt level.  Nevertheless `d_o(s)` is nonincreasing, becomes
constant at `s=lambda/(1-lambda)`, and has total downward variation

```text
d_o(0)-min_(0<=s<=1)d_o(s)=lambda.                       (64.9)
```

Thus for every monotone subdivision

```text
0=s_0<=s_1<=...<=s_L<=1,
```

the sum of all observer debt decreases is at most `lambda`, even though the
selected normalized direction tends to `-1`.

**Proof.**  The two terms in `(64.5)` cross when

```text
(1-s)(1-lambda)=1-2lambda,
```

namely at `s=lambda/(1-lambda)`.  Before that point, summing `(64.5)` and
`(64.6)` cancels every `s`, `lambda`, and `s lambda` term and gives `D(s)=3`.
After it, substituting `d_o=1-2lambda` gives the second line of `(64.8)`,
which equals `3` at the crossing and then increases.  Formula `(64.9)` is

```text
(1-lambda)-(1-2lambda)=lambda.
```

Monotonicity of `d_o` makes every monotone partition telescope, proving the
last assertion.  QED.

This is a selected-ray regression, not a counterexample to the paid source
or the conjecture.  It retains the exact local features relevant to the
negative port—literal executable source and full replacement, a positive-
debt mover, vanishing replacement-own debt, an exactly flat selected column,
no inactive tangent entry, no undercut anywhere on the ray, and a strict
off-level endpoint—but it does not establish that the limiting base is a
global minimum over the whole terminal-semantic carrier or supply compatible
rays for the other two active labels.  It refutes only the proposed
amplification step: a fixed negative normalized tangent does not imply fixed
total negative debt motion along its own full-replacement ray.  Repetition
would have to leave and later re-enter that ray; simple backtracking restores
the same debt and supplies no net account.  The negative-port producer
therefore still needs genuine reached cross-ray reconnection or a payoff
quantity not exhausted by `(64.9)`.

### 65. Fixed reached recovery and zero updater debt still do not Nashify locally

The endpoint-debt arm of Corollary 63A also has a sharp local boundary.  Use
the reviewed three-player reward table from Section 27:

```text
b_1(A)=0,
b_2(A)=1 exactly when A={3},
b_3(A)=1 exactly when A={2},

r(T)_i=b_i(T)                 if i notin T,
r(T)_i=b_i(T\{i})-1           if i in T.                (65.1)
```

Let `F` be the profile in which observer `1` Quits at date `0` and players
`2,3` Quit surely at date `1`.  Against the same opponents, observer `1`
gets `-1` from Quit at `0` and `0` from waiting beyond date `1`.  These are a
literal later-receiving paid row of gain one and live mass one.  Now perform
the actual unilateral update replacing player `1` by pure Quit at date `2`;
call the reached profile `R`.

**Proposition 65 (reached paid-recovery Nashification no-go).**  The update
`F -> R` has all of the following exact properties:

```text
U_1(R)-U_1(F)=1,
debt_1(R)=0,
U(R)=(0,0,0)=P.                                         (65.2)
```

Nevertheless the unique exact endpoint-Nash product root at tail `U(R)=0`
is all-Continue.  Its charge is zero, its successor is again zero, and every
finite exact punishment-floor admissible path whose source payoff is `U(R)`
has zero charge at every edge.

**Proof.**  At `F`, player `1` terminates alone at date zero and receives
`r({1})_1=-1`; players `2,3` receive zero.  At `R`, players `2,3` terminate
together at date one before player `1`'s planned date, and

```text
r({2,3})=(0,0,0).
```

The opponents of player `1` are unchanged by the update.  Waiting past them
is a best response of value zero, so its cap remains zero and its reached debt
is zero.  As verified in Section 27, the punishment vector is exactly zero.

At one root stage with tail zero, condition on any opponent Quit set `A`,
including the empty set.  Player `i`'s forced-Continue payoff is `b_i(A)`,
whereas forced Quit gives `b_i(A)-1`.  Quit is strictly dominated by Continue
for every player under every product law.  Hence all-Continue is the unique
exact root.  Its Bellman successor is the same zero tail.  Induction along the
orientation `tail -> current` of the admissible relation proves the last
claim.  QED.

This is not a new counterexample to the full paid consumer: it is the
Section 27 local table with the observer's prescribed strategy moved from the
receiving witness to the paid source witness.  It does not instantiate a
positive-minimum tangent frontier or separated endpoint cluster.  Its role is
now exact and narrower.  Even the full positive output of Corollary 63A—one
literal reached update with fixed payoff gain, arbitrarily small (here zero)
updater debt, a paid row, and a floor-safe reached payoff—does not imply any
positive-charge exact edge at that payoff.  The endpoint-debt arm must use a
global source field which the local recovery forgets, or make a nonlocal
payoff excursion before exactification.

`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__COROLLARY_63A_SECTIONS_64_65.md`
validates Corollary 63A's cap invariance and tolerance quantifiers, every
unrestricted cap and the flat-ray/variation arithmetic in Section 64, and
the semantic pair, punishment floor, unique root, and path induction in
Section 65.  It confirms the stated selected-ray and local-interface scopes.

### 66. Terminal gap already gives exact pure-time behavioral payoff returns

Sections 60 and 63A produce reached pure-time recovery steps but leave open
whether their absolute payoff levels can recur.  At the level of actual
behavioral profiles the answer is stronger than compact near-recurrence:
starting from deterministic clocks makes the set of prescribed payoff vectors
finite, so one obtains an exact payoff return of uniformly bounded length.
The result is deliberately separated from exact Nash--Bellman chronology.

Let the finite nonempty player set have cardinality `n` and assume

```text
HasTerminalExploitabilityGap reward gamma,       gamma>0.        (66.1)
```

For an actual behavioral profile `sigma`, retain its prescribed payoff
vector `U(sigma)`, unrestricted cap `B(sigma)`, and debt
`d_i(sigma)=B_i(sigma)-U_i(sigma)`.

**Proposition 66 (exact pure-time behavioral payoff return).**  For every
`eta>0` there is a nonempty unilateral strategy-update path of deterministic
pure-time profiles

```text
sigma^r -> sigma^(r+1) -> ... -> sigma^s,        r<s,             (66.2)
```

of length at most `2^n` such that

```text
U(sigma^s)=U(sigma^r),                                      (66.3)
```

and at every step `t` exactly one player `m_t` changes its deterministic
Quit time (where Never is allowed), with

```text
U_(m_t)(sigma^(t+1))-U_(m_t)(sigma^t) >= 3*gamma/4,
d_(m_t)(sigma^(t+1)) <= min(eta,gamma/4).                     (66.4)
```

Thus a fixed positive recovery threshold `3gamma/4`, arbitrarily small
updater debt, and an exact endpoint payoff return coexist on one bounded
behavioral path.  The path and selected player/times may depend on `eta`.

**Proof.**  Put

```text
epsilon=min(eta,gamma/4)>0.                                 (66.5)
```

Start from the all-Never pure-time profile `sigma^0`.  Inductively, the
terminal gap at `sigma^t` selects a player `m_t` with debt at least `gamma`.
The checked pure-time extremality theorem says deterministic Quit times and
Never have the same payoff supremum as all behavioral deviations.  Hence
some pure time `tau_t` has payoff within `epsilon` of that cap.  Set

```text
sigma^(t+1)=update(sigma^t,m_t,tau_t).
```

The opponents of `m_t` are unchanged, so its cap is unchanged.  Therefore

```text
U_(m_t)(sigma^(t+1))-U_(m_t)(sigma^t)
 >=d_(m_t)(sigma^t)-epsilon
 >=gamma-epsilon >=3gamma/4,

d_(m_t)(sigma^(t+1))
 =B_(m_t)(sigma^t)-U_(m_t)(sigma^(t+1))
 <=epsilon.                                               (66.6)
```

Every `sigma^t` remains a deterministic pure-time profile.  Its terminal
outcome is therefore deterministic: either all clocks are Never and the
payoff vector is zero, or the players tied at the least finite clock form one
nonempty first coalition `S` and the payoff vector is `r(S)`.  Hence all
prescribed payoff vectors in this path belong to the finite set

```text
{0} union {r(S) : empty != S subseteq I},                 (66.7)
```

which has cardinality at most `2^n`.  Among
`U(sigma^0),...,U(sigma^(2^n))`, two vectors with indices `r<s` are equal.
The intervening subpath is nonempty, has length at most `2^n`, satisfies
`(66.3)`, and inherits `(66.4)` from `(66.6)`.  QED.

Consecutive payoff vectors cannot be equal because the updater gains at
least `3gamma/4`, but this observation is not needed for nonemptiness.

This theorem solves exactly the absolute-level part of the behavioral port.
It is stronger than selecting one paid recovery: every edge of the returned
subpath is a fixed-gain pure-time recovery with low debt for its updater, and
the endpoint payoff seam is literally zero.  It also requires no tangent
family, curvature selection, or off-minimum endpoint cluster once the
terminal gap is given.

It is **not** a positive answer to the maintained question.  The arrows in
`(66.2)` change complete behavioral strategies; they are not exact
punishment-floor Nash--Bellman edges.  The fixed payoff gain in `(66.4)` is
not literal absorption charge.  No candidate tail, simultaneous exact root,
or floor inequality is produced.  Section 65 remains a sharp warning: even
one such pure-time strategy update can end at the punishment floor with zero
updater debt while every exact outgoing admissible edge has zero charge.
Consequently the current paid gap is now narrower than “find a payoff
return”: it is to Nashify a behavioral exact payoff return **nonlocally and
simultaneously**, retaining one fixed-charge exact edge.

Narrow source comparison found no existing conference statement of this
behavioral exact return.  It uses only the named terminal-gap predicate, the
checked pure-time extremality theorem
`sSup_range_quittingTerminalPayoff_update_eq_pureTime`, cap invariance under
an own-strategy update, and a finite pigeonhole argument on deterministic
terminal coalitions.  Independent review, including the exact-strengthening
addendum, is in
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_66.md`.

### 67. An exact best-response payoff cycle can freeze at every floor clip

Proposition 65 shows that one fixed-gain, zero-debt update need not Nashify at
its reached floor payoff.  The following cyclic version rules out a stronger
shortcut: retaining all nodes of the exact behavioral payoff return and
Nashifying them one at a time by canonical floor clipping.

Take players `1,2,3`, with indices read cyclically, so the successor of `1`
is `2`, of `2` is `3`, and of `3` is `1`.  For each player `i` and coalition
`A` of its opponents, including the empty coalition, put

```text
b_i(A)=1  if A is the singleton containing the cyclic successor of i,
       0  otherwise.
```

For every nonempty quitting coalition `T`, define

```text
r(T)_i = b_i(T)                  if i notin T,
r(T)_i = b_i(T\{i})-1            if i in T.             (67.1)
```

As in Section 27, the punishment vector is exactly zero.  Pure Never
guarantees the nonnegative payoff `b_i(A)` against every first opponent
coalition, while against all-Never opponents the best of Never and quitting
alone is `max(0,-1)=0`.

Consider the deterministic pure-time profiles

```text
sigma^0=(0,1,2),
sigma^1=(3,1,2),
sigma^2=(3,4,2),
sigma^3=(3,4,5),                                  (67.2)
```

where the coordinates are the players' Quit times.  The three first quitting
coalitions are respectively

```text
{1}, {2}, {3}, {1}.
```

Consequently their prescribed payoff vectors are

```text
U^0=U^3=(-1,0,1),
U^1=(1,-1,0),
U^2=(0,1,-1).                                      (67.3)
```

Their unrestricted cap vectors and debts are, in the same order,

```text
B^0=B^3=(1,0,1),       d^0=d^3=(2,0,0),
B^1=(1,1,0),           d^1=(0,2,0),
B^2=(0,1,1),           d^2=(0,0,2).                 (67.3a)
```

Indeed the current owner can obtain `1` by waiting past its successor; the
outsider already receiving `1` cannot improve, and the remaining outsider
can only keep its payoff `0` or join/preempt for `-1`.
In particular, every displayed node has terminal exploitability exactly two;
what fails later is the *global* terminal-gap hypothesis, not a local gap on
the returned cycle.

At step `i`, the unique current singleton owner moves after both opponents.
Before moving it gets `-1`.  Afterwards its cyclic successor is the first
quitter and it gets `1`.  Quitting earlier gives `-1`, tying that successor
gives `0`, and every later time, including Never, gives `1`.  Thus every
update in `(67.2)` is an exact pure-time best response.  It is also an exact
unrestricted behavioral best response: against deterministic opponent clocks,
every behavioral stopping payoff is an expectation of the displayed
pure-time payoffs and is at most one.  Each update gains exactly two and
leaves the updater with terminal debt zero.  Before the update its debt is
exactly two.  The path has the exact payoff return `U^3=U^0`.

Now canonically floor-clip its three payoff values:

```text
V^0=(0,0,1),   V^1=(1,0,0),   V^2=(0,1,0).          (67.4)
```

In fact a stronger root statement holds at every tail `V>=0`.  Conditional
on a nonempty opponent Quit set `A`, player `i` gets `b_i(A)` by forced
Continue and `b_i(A)-1` by forced Quit.  Conditional on no opponent Quit it
gets tail value `V_i>=0` by Continue and `-1` by Quit.  Hence Quit is strictly
dominated by Continue for every player against every product law.  The unique
exact endpoint-Nash root is all-Continue, its absorption charge is zero, and
its Bellman successor is `V` itself.  Induction in the orientation
`tail -> current` shows that every finite exact punishment-floor admissible
path sourced at any `V^j` is the constant zero-charge path at `V^j`.
The same conclusion holds at every cap tail `B^j`, since all those vectors
are also coordinatewise nonnegative.  Since the punishment floor is zero,
the argument in fact covers the entire floor-admissible payoff box: this
reward table has no positive-charge floor-admissible exact edge anywhere.

**Proposition 67 (collective floor-clip Nashification no-go).**  Exact payoff
return, a uniform positive pure-time gain at every unilateral strategy
update, exact best-response status and zero endpoint debt for every updater,
and simultaneous knowledge of every canonical floor-clipped node and cap
node do not produce even one positive-charge exact Nash--Bellman edge.  They
need not produce an exact admissible transition between two distinct clipped
nodes at all.

This example is not a terminal-gap game: all-Never is already an exact
terminal equilibrium.  It is therefore not a negative answer to the paid
near-return question and does not instantiate the positive-minimum frontier.
Its scope is the proposed conversion of Proposition 66's *output fields*,
even augmented by the same fixed local exploitability gap at every returned
node.
Those fields alone cannot be Nashified nodewise.  A successful use of the
actual paid source must add a genuinely global datum which excludes this
uniform joining-dominance pattern and produces a charged floor/root carrier,
then later repays its payoff seam, or construct the exact excursion as one
nonlocal block.  Merely choosing another floor tail would not repair this
example.  Neither canonical floor clipping nor updater optimality can be the
missing datum.

This is ordinary mathematics using the terminal-payoff/cap definitions and
the exact endpoint-root and admissible-path semantics already cited for
Sections 27 and 65.  A narrow conference search found the Section 13
one-stage root best-response cycle, but no deterministic stopping-time cycle
with the simultaneous payoff-return and all-floor-tail freezing properties
above.  Independent falsification review is in
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_67.md`.

### 68. Source correction: the exact return is the checked strict-toggle orbit

The stronger exact deterministic return sought after Proposition 66 does not
require a new cap-range argument.  It is already the checked coalition-toggle
orbit.

For a coalition `S`, let `sigma^S` be the terminal profile in which precisely
the players of `S` Quit at date zero and all outsiders Never Quit.  Against
`sigma^S_{-i}`, every unrestricted deviation by `i` is a mixture of only the
two terminal outcomes obtained by retaining or toggling `i`'s membership:

```text
B_i(sigma^S)
  = max(rbar(S)_i, rbar(S triangle {i})_i),           (68.1)
```

where `rbar(empty)=0`.  Indeed, if an opponent Quits at date zero then every
positive stopping time is equivalent to Never; if no opponent Quits, every
finite stopping time is equivalent to Quit at zero.  Thus the better
membership action is an exact unrestricted best response.

The checked theorem
`QuittingTerminalExploitabilityWitness.exists_strictToggle_gain` in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Collision/Toggles/StrictOrbit.lean`
chooses, at every `S`, a player `m(S)` whose membership toggle gains at least
the terminal gap `gamma`.  Consequently

```text
S_(t+1) = S_t triangle {m(S_t)}                       (68.2)
```

is exactly an asynchronous unrestricted-best-response update of the binary
terminal profile, with

```text
U_m(sigma^(S_(t+1))) - U_m(sigma^(S_t)) >= gamma,
d_m(sigma^(S_(t+1))) = 0.                            (68.3)
```

The checked theorem `exists_strictToggleClosedOrbit_from` in the same file
already proves finite recurrence from every seed.  Starting at `empty`, it
also records the first solo edge and the second collision edge.  Since the
coalition itself recurs, the strategy profile `sigma^S` recurs, and therefore
its entire terminal semantic pair `(U,B)` and debt vector recur exactly.  The
generic orbit has length at most `2^|I|`; in four players the checked simple
cycle extraction in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Collision/Toggles/ReachableSimpleCycle.lean`
has even length between four and sixteen.

This is source correspondence, not a new proposition.  In particular it
supersedes the earlier attempted finite-calendar cap count.  The source files
state the binding qualification explicitly: this is a static membership
toggle orbit through already-terminal coalition profiles, not a history
reached by play, an exact Nash--Bellman path, or an absorption-charge carrier.

For completeness, the elementary off-diagonal ledger from the discarded
cap-count proof still applies to any returned toggle segment.  If its length
is `L` and `n>=2`, the selected coordinate gains at least `gamma` and loses at
least `gamma` debt on each edge, while all endpoint payoff and debt vectors
agree.  Hence some unchanged player loses at least `gamma/(n-1)` payoff on
one edge, and some (not necessarily the same) unchanged player gains at least
`gamma/(n-1)` debt on one edge.  This is only a static cross-player account;
it does not turn a toggle edge into a reached residual or Bellman edge.

Section 67 is a separate interface regression.  Its staggered-clock cycle is
not the binary coalition orbit above, so it should not be advertised as a
counterexample carrying every item of the checked toggle source.  What it
does show is the narrower fact already reviewed there: exact local
best-response recurrence and complete nodewise floor/cap data alone do not
Nashify to positive charge.

### 69. Exact shadowing of the toggle cycle pays its own block denominator

Section 68 identifies the exact behavioral source with the checked static
toggle orbit.  The orbit's selected-coordinate gain automatically creates a
fixed loss in an unchanged payoff coordinate.  Any exact Bellman path which
shadows the orbit must pay that loss by absorption.  Thus charge retention is
not an additional hypothesis once a global exact payoff shadow has been
constructed.

Let `n=|I|`.  Under a terminal exploitability witness of gap `gamma>0`, the
checked theorem `one_lt_card` gives `n>=2`.  Choose a nonempty closed selected
toggle orbit

```text
S_0 -> S_1 -> ... -> S_L=S_0,       L>0,             (69.1)
```

and let `m_t` be its selected toggler.  Put

```text
u_t=quittingSetReward(reward,S_t).
```

The checked edge inequality is

```text
u_(t+1,m_t)-u_(t,m_t) >= gamma.                     (69.2)
```

Fix `epsilon>0`.  An **epsilon exact payoff shadow** of `(69.1)` consists of
floor-admissible states

```text
x_0,x_1,...,x_L
```

and exact floor-admissible paths `pi_t:x_t -> x_(t+1)` such that, writing
`v_t=payoff(x_t)`,

```text
||v_t-u_t||_infinity <= epsilon             for every 0<=t<=L.  (69.3)
```

The final state need not equal the initial state; only both payoff vectors
shadow the same returned coalition payoff.

Let `M=quittingRewardBound reward`.  Under `gamma>0`, `M>0`.  For a path
`pi`, write

```text
Raw(pi)=sum_(edge in pi) absorption(edge),
Agg(pi)=1-product_(edge in pi)(1-absorption(edge)).   (69.4)
```

**Proposition 69 (toggle-shadow block denominator).**  If

```text
epsilon <= gamma/(4*(n-1)),                          (69.5)
```

then the concatenated exact path `Pi=pi_0++...++pi_(L-1)` satisfies

```text
||payoff(x_L)-payoff(x_0)||_infinity <= 2*epsilon,   (69.6)

Raw(Pi) >= gamma/(4*M*(n-1)),                        (69.7)

Agg(Pi) >= gamma/(gamma+4*M*(n-1)).                  (69.8)
```

If `Pi` has at most `H` exact edges, some one of them has literal absorption
at least

```text
gamma/(4*M*(n-1)*H).                                 (69.9)
```

**Proof.**  Since `u_L=u_0`, summing all payoff-coordinate changes around
the static cycle gives zero.  The selected coordinates contribute at least
`L*gamma` by `(69.2)`.  Hence

```text
sum_(t<L) sum_(i!=m_t) [u_(t+1,i)-u_(t,i)] <= -L*gamma.
```

There are exactly `L*(n-1)` displayed off-diagonal entries.  Therefore some
edge `t` and unchanged player `i!=m_t` satisfy

```text
u_(t+1,i)-u_(t,i) <= -gamma/(n-1).                  (69.10)
```

By `(69.3)` and `(69.5)`,

```text
v_(t+1,i)-v_(t,i)
 <= -gamma/(n-1)+2*epsilon
 <= -gamma/(2*(n-1)).                               (69.11)
```

Every admissible payoff lies in the reward box.  On an exact Bellman edge of
absorption `q`, the named one-step estimate
`abs_quittingRootSuccessorPayoff_sub_tail_le_two_mul_absorptionMass` gives

```text
|payoff(current)_i-payoff(tail)_i| <= 2*M*q.         (69.12)
```

Telescope absolute values along `pi_t` and use `(69.11)`.  This gives

```text
gamma/(2*(n-1)) <= 2*M*Raw(pi_t),
```

which proves `(69.7)` for the concatenation.

For numbers `0<=q_k<=1`,

```text
product_k(1-q_k) <= 1/(1+sum_k q_k).                 (69.13)
```

If some `q_k=1` this is immediate.  Otherwise
`1/(1-q_k)>=1+q_k`, and multiplying gives `(69.13)` because
`product_k(1+q_k)>=1+sum_k q_k`.  Thus

```text
Agg(Pi)>=Raw(Pi)/(1+Raw(Pi)),
```

and `(69.8)` follows from `(69.7)`.  Equation `(69.6)` follows directly from
`u_L=u_0` and the two endpoint errors in `(69.3)`.  Finally averaging
`(69.7)` over at most `H` edges proves `(69.9)`.  QED.

**Corollary 69A (the charge field is automatic after global shadowing).**  If
such exact shadows exist at every positive accuracy, then Proposition 26's
reviewed aggregate-block compiler gives an unrestricted-behavior uniform
equilibrium payoff.  Indeed choose

```text
epsilon <= min(eta/2,gamma/(4*(n-1)))
```

for requested endpoint error `eta`; `(69.6)` pays the seam and `(69.8)` gives
one fixed aggregate denominator.  Under a terminal exploitability witness
this conclusion is contradictory, so arbitrary-accuracy global shadowing
cannot coexist with the witness.

If, more strongly, the total number of exact rows in the shadows is bounded
independently of the accuracy, `(69.9)` gives the fixed **single-edge**
threshold required by the maintained
`PAID_ADMISSIBLE_PAYOFF_NEAR_RETURN` question.

**Corollary 69B (the Proposition 66 return has the same reduction).**  The
proof of Proposition 69 uses only a closed finite payoff sequence and a
uniform lower bound on each selected coordinate's forward gain.  Apply it to
the independently reviewed deterministic strategy-update return of
Proposition 66, whose selected gain is

```text
g=3*gamma/4.
```

If, for every positive `epsilon`, its payoff nodes admit concatenable exact
floor-admissible paths whose endpoint states are `epsilon`-close to those
nodes, then for

```text
epsilon <= 3*gamma/(16*(n-1))
```

the concatenation has endpoint seam at most `2*epsilon` and satisfies

```text
Raw(Pi) >= 3*gamma/(16*M*(n-1)),
Agg(Pi) >= 3*gamma/(3*gamma+16*M*(n-1)).              (69.14)
```

Thus Proposition 26 again compiles an unrestricted uniform payoff.  If the
total exact-row count is at most `H`, one row has charge at least

```text
3*gamma/(16*M*(n-1)*H).                               (69.15)
```

The behavioral update cycle already has at most `2^n` update segments, but
that does **not** bound the number of exact Bellman rows needed to connect
two successive payoff nodes.  Consequently `(69.15)` requires a separate
uniform connector-length bound.  The small updater-debt field in Proposition
66 is not used in this conditional implication; its possible role is only in
producing the missing exact connectors.

The scope is exact.  Proposition 69 does not construct one state `x_t` or one
connecting exact path.  It says that a successful nonlocal Nashification of
the checked toggle orbit need not separately preserve the behavioral paid
hazard: the adverse off-diagonal payoff motion forces a fixed exact block
denominator automatically.  What remains is a source-matched construction of
the floor-admissible payoff shadow itself.  Sections 65 and 67 show that
isolated low-debt recovery nodes and their canonical floor/cap tails do not
supply those connections.  The path lengths may diverge; without a uniform
bound the result closes the branch through the aggregate compiler but does
not populate the maintained single-high-edge structure literally.

Sources inspected for this reduction are the checked strict-toggle orbit in
`UniformEquilibrium/Diagnostics/Quitting/Collision/Toggles/StrictOrbit.lean`,
the one-edge motion estimate in
`UniformEquilibrium/Quitting/Debt/Marked/TimeAdvance.lean`, and the reviewed
aggregate block compiler of Proposition 26.  This is ordinary mathematics
and is currently awaiting independent falsification review.

### 70. A floor-safe cap lift or an exact minmax-tight toggle

Section 69 deliberately assumes that the target payoff nodes can be shadowed
by floor-admissible states.  A terminal payoff vector itself need not dominate
the punishment floor.  For the checked finite toggle orbit this feasibility
issue has an exact scalar resolution.

Retain `(69.1)` and write

```text
P_i = quittingPunishmentValue(reward,i),
u_(t,i) = quittingSetReward(reward,S_t,i),
b_(t,i) = quittingStationaryUnilateralCap
            (reward,quittingPureSetRoot(S_t),i).             (70.1)
```

The checked pure-row formula gives

```text
b_(t,i)=max(r_(S_t union {i})(i),r_(S_t minus {i})(i)),
P_i<=b_(t,i).                                               (70.2)
```

In particular `b_t` is in the canonical reward box.  For each player define

```text
theta_i = max_(0<=t<L) R_(t,i),

R_(t,i) = 0                                  if u_(t,i)>=P_i,
R_(t,i) = (P_i-u_(t,i))/(b_(t,i)-u_(t,i))    if u_(t,i)<P_i,

delta_i = 1-theta_i.                                      (70.3)
```

The second denominator is positive by `(70.2)`, and every displayed ratio is
in `(0,1]`.  Put

```text
w_(t,i)=(1-theta_i)u_(t,i)+theta_i b_(t,i),
Lambda=sum_(t<L) delta_(m_t).                              (70.4)
```

**Proposition 70 (finite cap-lift dichotomy).**  The vectors `w_t` lie in the
canonical punishment-floor box, `w_L=w_0`, and every selected edge satisfies

```text
w_(t+1,m_t)-w_(t,m_t) >= delta_(m_t)*gamma.                (70.5)
```

Consequently exactly one of the following alternatives holds.

1. `Lambda>0`.  If the `w_t` admit an `epsilon` exact payoff shadow, with

   ```text
   epsilon <= gamma*Lambda/(4*L*(n-1)),                    (70.6)
   ```

   then its concatenated exact path satisfies

   ```text
   Raw(Pi) >= gamma*Lambda/(4*M*L*(n-1)),
   Agg(Pi) >= gamma*Lambda /
     (gamma*Lambda+4*M*L*(n-1)).                           (70.7)
   ```

   Thus arbitrary-accuracy connectors again trigger Proposition 26.

2. `Lambda=0`.  For every player `i` which occurs as a selected toggler,
   there is an orbit vertex `S_t` with

   ```text
   u_(t,i)<P_i=b_(t,i).                                    (70.8)
   ```

   Against the pure opponent row induced by `S_t`, the other membership
   action of `i` therefore pays exactly `P_i`.  Equivalently this row is an
   attained pure stationary minmax environment for `i`, while the on-path
   membership action is strictly below minmax.

**Proof.**  Formula `(70.3)` was chosen so that each convex combination in
`(70.4)` dominates `P_i`; convexity also keeps it in the reward box.  The
coalition and its pure-row cap return at time `L`, so `w_L=w_0`.

The edge `S_t -> S_(t+1)` changes only player `m_t`'s action.  Its opponents
are identical at the two endpoints, hence its unrestricted cap is identical:

```text
b_(t+1,m_t)=b_(t,m_t).                                    (70.9)
```

Combining `(70.9)` with the checked selected gain `(69.2)` proves `(70.5)`.
If `Lambda>0`, the total selected-coordinate motion is at least
`gamma*Lambda`.  Since the whole vector returns, the sum of all off-diagonal
motions is at most `-gamma*Lambda`.  There are `L*(n-1)` of them.  The proof
of Proposition 69, with this averaged loss and `(70.6)`, gives `(70.7)`.

If `Lambda=0`, nonnegativity of every `delta_i` implies
`theta_(m_t)=1` on every selected edge.  A finite maximum in `(70.3)` equals
one only if some below-floor ratio equals one.  Since `u<P<=b`, that equality
is exactly `b=P`, proving `(70.8)`.  Finally the pure-row cap is the maximum
of the two membership actions; the current one is `u<P=b`, so the other one
attains `P`.  QED.

The dichotomy is sharp at the interface.  If every orbit payoff already
dominates punishment, all `theta_i=0`, `Lambda=L`, and Proposition 70 reduces
to Proposition 69.  At the opposite boundary, `b=P>u` forces the full cap
lift in that coordinate and erases its selected gain.  Section 67 remains a
separate test: its displayed payoff cycle is already floor-safe, yet no local
exact connector exists.  Thus Proposition 70 removes only the hidden
floor-feasibility objection.  It neither constructs exact paths in the slack
arm nor compiles the playerwise minmax-tight rows in the tight arm.

The named source facts are
`quittingStationaryUnilateralCap_pureSetRoot`,
`quittingPunishmentValue_le_pureRowCap`, cap invariance under an own-strategy
update, and the checked strict-toggle orbit.  This is ordinary mathematics
and has passed the independent Ramsey and Euler reviews named below.

**Corollary 70A (coordinatewise floor clipping is the sharp lift).**  The
playerwise cap interpolation above is not needed to detect the live arm.  Put

```text
z_(t,i)=max(u_(t,i),P_i),
g_t=z_(t+1,m_t)-z_(t,m_t),
G=sum_(t<L) g_t.                                           (70.10)
```

Then `z_t` is a returned canonical floor-safe payoff cycle and

```text
g_t=b_(t,m_t)-max(u_(t,m_t),P_(m_t)) >= 0.                 (70.11)
```

If `G>0`, any `epsilon` exact payoff shadow with

```text
epsilon <= G/(4*L*(n-1))                                  (70.12)
```

satisfies

```text
Raw(Pi) >= G/(4*M*L*(n-1)),
Agg(Pi) >= G/(G+4*M*L*(n-1)).                             (70.13)
```

If `G=0`, **every selected edge itself** is floor-tight:

```text
u_(t,m_t)<P_(m_t)=b_(t,m_t)=u_(t+1,m_t).                  (70.14)
```

Indeed the new membership action on a selected edge beats the old one by at
least `gamma`.  The exact two-action cap formula therefore gives
`b_(t,m_t)=u_(t+1,m_t)`, while `(70.2)` gives
`P_(m_t)<=u_(t+1,m_t)`.  This proves `(70.11)`.  If the old payoff is already
above punishment, `g_t>=gamma`; otherwise `g_t=b_(t,m_t)-P_(m_t)`.  Hence
`g_t=0` is equivalent to `(70.14)`.  The returned-vector ledger and the proof
of Proposition 69 give `(70.13)` from a positive sum `G`.

Corollary 70A strictly sharpens the residual split but not the producer.  The
live arm still needs exact connectors.  The remaining tight arm is now a
closed toggle orbit in which every selected membership change lands exactly
at that player's attained punishment value; it is no longer merely a list of
possibly unrelated minmax-tight vertices.  This strengthening passed focused
checks in addenda to both Section 70 feedback files.

### 71. The edgewise minmax-tight arm has no directed square

Corollary 70A leaves a particularly rigid static orbit:

```text
u_(t,m_t)<P_(m_t)=u_(t+1,m_t)                       (71.1)
```

on every selected edge.  The smallest nontrivial cube cycle is incompatible
with `(71.1)` for a purely minmax reason.

**Proposition 71 (no minmax-tight toggle square).**  Let `B` be a coalition
and let `i,j` be distinct labels outside `B`.  There is no directed selected
toggle square

```text
B -> B+i -> B+i+j -> B+j -> B                       (71.2)
```

for which every arrow satisfies `(71.1)` with its displayed toggler.  The
same conclusion holds after rotating or reversing the square and relabelling
`i,j`.

**Proof.**  First suppose `B` is nonempty.  Read the two `i`-edges.  Against
the pure opponent row `B`, joining pays `P_i` and continuing pays strictly
less than `P_i`.  Against the pure opponent row `B+j`, continuing pays `P_i`
and joining pays strictly less than `P_i`:

```text
r_(B+i)(i)=P_i>r_B(i),
r_(B+j)(i)=P_i>r_(B+i+j)(i).                         (71.3)
```

Let every member of `B` Quit surely at the first stage, let `j` Quit with
probability `x` for some `0<x<1`, and let all other opponents Continue.  The
nonempty base makes absorption at the first stage certain whatever `i` does.
Thus `i`'s Quit and Continue values are respectively strict convex
combinations of the two columns in `(71.3)`.  Both are strictly below `P_i`.
The unrestricted cap against this stationary opponent row is their maximum,
contradicting the defining lower bound of the punishment value against every
opponent profile.

Now let `B` be empty and read the two `j`-edges.  Tightness gives

```text
r_{i,j}(j)=P_j>r_i(j),
0=P_j>r_j(j).                                        (71.4)
```

Let the sole potentially quitting opponent `i` use one stationary hazard
`0<x<1`, with every other opponent always continuing.  Against this row,
`j`'s Quit value is

```text
(1-x)r_j(j)+x r_{i,j}(j)<P_j=0,                     (71.5)
```

while its Never value is `r_i(j)<P_j`, because `i` eventually Quits almost
surely.  The exact stationary unilateral-cap formula says that arbitrary
behavioral timing by `j` is bounded by the maximum of these two values.  It
is therefore strictly below `P_j`, again contradicting the punishment lower
bound.  QED.

**Corollary 71A (tight simple cycles start at length six).**  A simple cycle
in a hypercube has even length.  A two-cycle would toggle the same label in
both directions and contradict the two strict payoff improvements directly;
Proposition 71 excludes length four.  Hence an edgewise tight simple selected
cycle has length at least six.  In particular the tight arm is impossible
for two players.

**Corollary 71B (the only surviving six-edge label word).**  If an edgewise
tight simple selected cycle has length six, then, after cyclic rotation and
relabeling, its selected-label word is

```text
i,j,k,i,j,k                                             (71.6)
```

for three distinct players.

Indeed every coordinate is toggled an even number of times.  A six-edge
simple cycle cannot use one label four times and another twice: with only two
separators, two occurrences of the fourfold label would be adjacent, causing
an immediate backtrack.  Hence exactly three labels occur twice.  If their
cyclic word is not `(71.6)`, some label occurs with exactly one intervening
edge, giving a subword `i,j,i`.

The two tight `i`-rows then differ only in opponent `j`.  The proof of
Proposition 71 already contradicts this unless their common opponent base is
empty and the three-edge segment is, up to orientation,

```text
empty -> {i} -> {i,j} -> {j}.                         (71.7)
```

The remaining labels must be `k,j,k` to close the simple six-cycle.  They
produce

```text
{j} -> {j,k} -> {k} -> empty,                         (71.8)
```

which is exactly the forbidden empty-base orientation for `k` in the second
half of Proposition 71.  This contradiction eliminates `i,j,i`, so the two
occurrences of each of the three labels are antipodal in the cyclic word,
which is `(71.6)`.

Corollary 71B classifies only the selected labels, not the six coalition
vertices up to complementing coordinates.  In particular it does not erase
the distinction between a cycle which visits the empty coalition and one
which omits it.

This is a sharp static impossibility, not a chronological compiler.  It
strictly narrows the tight branch of Corollary 70A but says nothing yet about
longer cube cycles.  The proof uses only
`quittingStationaryUnilateralCap_pureSetRoot`, the one-opponent mixed-row cap
formula, and `quittingPunishmentValue_le_stationaryUnilateralCap`.
Proposition 71 and Corollary 71B passed the Euler review and focused addendum
named below.

### 72. A persistent-base tight six-cycle forces a cross-corner premium

The antipodal word from Corollary 71B has one further exact minmax
consequence.  It does not yet construct a connector, but it turns the
edgewise punishment equalities into a uniformly positive payoff at one of
the two omitted cross corners of each active coordinate.

Let a tight six-cycle have selected-label word

```text
i,j,k,i,j,k,                                             (72.1)
```

and let `D` be the intersection of its six coalition vertices.  Assume
`D` is nonempty.  The cycle lies in the three-face obtained by fixing every
member of `D` to Quit, every player outside `D union {i,j,k}` to Continue,
and varying the three displayed coordinates.  For an active label `ell`,
its two selected edges occur at antipodal configurations of the other two
active labels.  Write `delta_ell` for the smaller of its two selected payoff
increments.  The checked strict-toggle inequality gives

```text
delta_ell >= gamma.                                      (72.2)
```

**Proposition 72 (cross-corner premium).**  Under these hypotheses, for each
`ell` in `{i,j,k}`, at one of the two remaining opponent corners and for one
of `ell`'s two membership actions, the corresponding pure coalition payoff
is at least

```text
P_ell + delta_ell/2 >= P_ell + gamma/2.                  (72.3)
```

Here the "remaining opponent corners" are the two configurations of the
other active labels which are not used by `ell`'s two selected edges.  The
high action in `(72.3)` need not itself be the selected action of another
cycle edge, and its coalition may be either a visited or an omitted vertex
of the three-face.

**Proof.**  Fix `ell`, and call its two membership actions `0,1`.  Its two
selected edges use antipodal opponent corners, say `y` and `bar y`.  Since
the coordinate is toggled in opposite directions on its two occurrences,
edgewise tightness and the definition of `delta_ell` give, after possibly
exchanging the action names,

```text
v(1,y)=P_ell,       v(0,y)<=P_ell-delta_ell,
v(0,bar y)=P_ell,   v(1,bar y)<=P_ell-delta_ell.          (72.4)
```

Make every member of `D` Quit surely at date zero, let each of the other two
active labels Quit independently with probability `1/2` at date zero, and
let all remaining opponents Continue.  Absorption occurs at date zero
regardless of `ell`'s action because `D` is nonempty.  Hence the two pure
action values for `ell` are the uniform averages of their four corner
payoffs, and the unrestricted unilateral cap is the larger of those two
averages.  The punishment lower bound against every opponent profile implies
that one action, call it `a`, has four-corner sum at least `4P_ell`.

For either fixed action, its two antipodal selected-corner payoffs in `(72.4)`
sum to at most `2P_ell-delta_ell`.  Therefore action `a` has cross-corner sum
at least

```text
4P_ell-(2P_ell-delta_ell)=2P_ell+delta_ell.              (72.5)
```

At least one of those two cross-corner payoffs is at least
`P_ell+delta_ell/2`, proving `(72.3)`.  QED.

The nonempty persistent base is essential to this argument: it makes the
four-corner experiment a one-stage affine payoff calculation.  With empty
base, Continue at the all-Continue opponent corner exposes a normalized
future continuation value, exactly as in the exceptional arm of Proposition
71.  Proposition 72 is therefore a static finite residual, not a terminal
equilibrium, exact Nash--Bellman edge, or reached payoff return.  The next
missing handoff is to align one forced cross-corner premium with a selected
floor-safe toggle or with a named sure-base/induced-game compiler; no such
alignment follows from the label word alone.

Independent review:
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_72.md`
validates the antipodal/opposite-action identification, the sure-base
four-corner cap, the `delta_ell/2` constant, and the stated static scope.

### 73. The singleton-gap exact prefix has a finite collision budget

The source-matched collision alternative can be made genuinely dynamic.  It
cannot recur indefinitely while the same singleton payoff gap persists:
every collision row spends a fixed amount of the terminal-semantic debt
excess above the positive minimum.  Thus a finite exact floor prefix must
either make a nonlocal payoff excursion or activate a quantitatively interior
owner distinct from the gap player.  This is the first reduction here which
concatenates the
Section 50 roots as literal Bellman predecessors rather than comparing
separately selected rows.

Let `X^0=(U^0,B^0)` be an actual terminal-semantic carrier point with
`U^0>=P`.  Let `D_* > 0` be the minimum terminal-semantic debt sum and put

```text
E_0=D(X^0)-D_* >= 0.
```

Assume that for one player `g` and one `Delta>0`,

```text
U^0_g <= r({g})_g-Delta.                                  (73.1)
```

Put `delta=Delta/2`, let `n=|I|`, and retain the terminal exploitability gap
`gamma>0` and reward bound `M>0`.  Define

```text
c     = delta/(delta+2M),
d     = gamma/(4M),
omega = d^(n-1),
a     = delta*omega,
b     = c*a/(8M).                                         (73.2)
```

All five constants are positive.  Choose an integer `N>=1` with

```text
N*D_* * b > E_0.                                         (73.3)
```

The terminal-gap witness gives `n>=2`, so the denominator `n-1` below is
strictly positive.  It also gives `M>0` and `gamma<=4M`, hence `0<d<=1`.

Starting from `X^0`, whenever the construction has reached `X^t`, choose any
exact product Nash root `q_t` at tail `U^t=(X^t).1` and set

```text
X^(t+1)=Prefix(q_t,X^t).                                  (73.4)
```

Mixed finite-game Nash gives such a root.  Prefix closure keeps every `X^t`
in the actual terminal-semantic carrier, and punishment-floor invariance
keeps every `U^t>=P`.  Hence `(73.4)` is a literal source-matched exact
punishment-floor Bellman edge, not an independently selected reset endpoint.

**Proposition 73 (finite collision-budget escape).**  Before row `N`, one of
the following alternatives must occur.

1. **Nonlocal payoff excursion.**  For some `1<=t<=N`,

   ```text
   U^t_g > r({g})_g-delta,
   U^t_g-U^0_g > delta.                                  (73.5)
   ```

   The exact floor path from `U^0` to `U^t` contains an edge of absorption
   at least `c`.

2. **Crossed interior activation.**  For some `0<=t<N` there is `k!=g` with

   ```text
   q_t(g)<c/2,
   c/(2(n-1)) < q_t(k) <= 1-d.                           (73.6)
   ```

   The row `q_t` itself is an exact floor edge of absorption at least `c`.
   In particular a singleton-owner local face `{g}` has activated an owner
   outside that face, bounded away from both zero and one.

The conclusion is universal over the exact roots chosen in `(73.4)`.

**Proof.**  Suppose neither alternative occurs.  Then for every `t<=N`,

```text
U^t_g <= r({g})_g-delta.                                  (73.7)
```

At each `t<N`, Propositions 50--53 apply with the fixed gap `delta`.  Every
`q_t` has absorption at least `c` and every marginal is at most `1-d`.
Proposition 53 says either `(73.6)` holds or the actual root puts probability
at least `b` on collision outcomes containing `g` with the displayed adverse
joining sign.  The former has been excluded, so in particular

```text
collisionMass(q_t) >= b.                                 (73.8)
```

Apply the checked one-row semantic collision payment to the literal prefix
`X^t -> X^(t+1)`.  Since every carrier point has debt at least `D_*`,

```text
D(X^(t+1)) <= D(X^t)-D_* b.                              (73.9)
```

Telescoping `(73.9)` for `N` rows gives

```text
D(X^N) <= D(X^0)-N D_* b < D_*,                          (73.10)
```

contradicting minimality of `D_*`.  Thus one alternative occurs.  In the
first alternative, choose the first excursion time.  If a crossed row
occurred earlier, the second alternative already holds and supplies its
charged row.  Otherwise every preceding row still satisfies `(73.7)` and is
not crossed, so each has charge at least `c`; `(73.1)` and `(73.5)` give the
strict displacement by `delta`.  The second alternative itself already
includes its charged row.  QED.

**Corollary 73A (recurrent charge already is the near-return).**  Extend the
exact floor prefix indefinitely by arbitrary exact Nash predecessors.  If
there is any `c_0>0` for which infinitely many rows have absorption at least
`c_0`, then the maintained paid payoff-near-return conclusion holds with
fixed threshold `c_0`.

Indeed the tail payoff vectors of those rows lie in the compact canonical
reward box.  For every `eta>0`, two of them, at times `s<t`, are within
`eta` in every coordinate.  The literal exact floor segment from time `s` to
time `t` begins with a row of charge at least `c_0`, so it is exactly the
required varying path.  No recurrence of roots, caps, or semantic debt is
needed.

Consequently, if the maintained conclusion is assumed false, every infinite
exact floor extension from `X^0` satisfies

```text
absorption(q_t) -> 0,                                    (73.11)
```

and eventually

```text
U^t_g > r({g})_g-delta,
U^t_g-U^0_g > delta.                                     (73.12)
```

The first assertion follows because a nonnegative sequence which does not
converge to zero has a positive threshold attained infinitely often.  For
the second, every row whose tail violates `(73.12)` has the persistent
singleton gap `delta`, hence charge at least `c` by Proposition 50; such rows
would contradict `(73.11)`.  Thus the nonlocal excursion arm is now compiled
whenever it regenerates fixed charge.  The exact unresolved case is a
one-way escape: the source-matched orbit permanently leaves the original gap
coordinate and enters the diffuse/all-Continue boundary with vanishing row
charge.

**Corollary 73B (the one-way escape lands on a finite outside/collision
atom).**  Continue under the negation of the maintained near-return
conclusion.  Every infinite exact extension has a strict subsequence along
which

```text
X^(t_j) -> X^infinity,
q_(t_j) -> all-Continue.                                 (73.13)
```

The limit is an actual terminal-semantic carrier point and satisfies

```text
U^infinity_g-U^0_g >= delta,
all-Continue is exact Nash at U^infinity,
D_* <= D(X^infinity) <= D(X^0).                          (73.14)
```

Moreover, some player `i` and terminal outcome `A` satisfy

```text
d_i(X^infinity) >= D_*/n,
reward_i(A) >= U^infinity_i+D_*/n,                       (73.15)
```

where `A` is either Never or a nonempty coalition different from `{i}`.  In
the latter case, a singleton `A={k}` has `k!=i` and is a distinct-owner atom;
otherwise `A` is a genuine collision atom.  The distinct owner is outside a
separated face only when that face has sole owner `i` (or when separate
incidence data exclude `k` from it).

**Proof.**  The carrier is compact, so select a convergent subsequence of
`X^t`.  Corollary 73A gives `absorption(q_t)->0`.  Each marginal Quit
probability is at most the probability that somebody Quits, hence every
coordinate of `q_t` tends to zero and the product roots converge to
all-Continue.  The exact endpoint-Nash graph is closed, proving the middle
claim of `(73.14)`.  The first claim follows from eventual `(73.12)` and
closedness; prefix closure gives carrier membership of the limit.

The one-row semantic collision inequality has a nonnegative left side, so
`D(X^(t+1))<=D(X^t)` on the whole exact prefix.  Continuity and global
minimality give the last two inequalities in `(73.14)`.  All semantic debts
are nonnegative, so finite averaging selects `i` with
`d_i(X^infinity)>=D_*/n`.  The checked finite-terminal-atom bound for a
carrier envelope selects `A` with

```text
B^infinity_i <= reward_i(A).
```

Since `B^infinity_i=U^infinity_i+d_i(X^infinity)`, this proves `(73.15)`.
If `A={i}`, exact all-Continue Nash would give
`reward_i({i})<=U^infinity_i`, contradicting `(73.15)`.  Thus `A` has the
stated form.  QED.

Corollary 73B exhausts the permanent one-way architecture at the level of
actual semantic closure: it cannot terminate at a featureless phantom.  It
must retain a fixed `D_*/n` nonlocal terminal-atom premium, oriented as
Never, another singleton owner, or collision.  The theorem still does not
assert positive probability of that atom in one realizing profile, nor that
its pure replacement is an exact floor root.  Turning `(73.15)` into such a
source-matched positive-mass exact row is the precise last adapter before the
existing outside-owner/collision consumers can apply.

**Corollary 73C (a plateau premium has a carrier-matched positive-mass
realization).**  A positive-mass version follows at behavioral source level,
allowing the finite atom to be reselected at half of the margin in `(73.15)`.
Put

```text
e=D_*/n,                 K=2^n.                           (73.16)
```

There are actual profiles `sigma_j` with
`Sem(sigma_j)->X^infinity`, pure-time unilateral deviations of the selected
debtor `i`, and deviated profiles `tau_j` such that, along one subsequence,

```text
payoff_i(tau_j)-payoff_i(sigma_j) >= e/2,                 (73.17)
Pr_(tau_j)(A) >= e/(8M K),                                (73.18)
reward_i(A) >= U^infinity_i+e/2                          (73.19)
```

for one fixed terminal outcome `A`.  As in Corollary 73B, `A` is Never, a
singleton `{k}` with `k!=i`, or a collision coalition.

**Proof.**  Carrier membership supplies actual `sigma_j` converging jointly
in prescribed payoff and unrestricted cap.  For all large `j`,

```text
B_i(sigma_j) >= B^infinity_i-e/8.
```

Pure-time extremality of the quitting best-response value selects one pure
time (Never allowed) whose unilateral payoff is within `e/8` of that cap.
Since `B^infinity_i>=U^infinity_i+e`, the resulting `tau_j` satisfies

```text
payoff_i(tau_j) >= U^infinity_i+3e/4.                    (73.20)
```

Let `H` be the finite set of terminal outcomes whose `i`-reward is at least
`U^infinity_i+e/2`, and let `p_j` be its total probability under `tau_j`.
All rewards lie in `[-M,M]`, so `(73.20)` gives

```text
U^infinity_i+3e/4
 <= p_j M+(1-p_j)(U^infinity_i+e/2),
e/4 <= 2M p_j.
```

Hence `p_j>=e/(8M)`.  There are exactly `K` terminal outcomes (Never plus
the nonempty coalitions), so one member of `H` has mass at least
`e/(8M K)`; finite pigeonhole freezes the same `A` on a subsequence.  The
