source prescribed payoff also converges to `U^infinity`, so `(73.20)` gives
`(73.17)` after one further tail.  Exact all-Continue Nash excludes
`A={i}`, proving the stated orientation.  QED.

This is now a literal, carrier-matched nonperturbative behavioral exit from
the permanent diffuse plateau: fixed gain and fixed terminal mass occur on
the same one-player replacement while all opponents retain the realizing
profile.  The realizing profiles need not be residuals reached by play from
the original paid profile; only their semantic pairs converge to the
source-matched exact-prefix limit.
It still does **not** identify that mass with one exact Bellman-row charge.
The terminal mass may be spread over calendar times, and the deviating pure
time need not be part of an exact simultaneous floor root.  The capstone
residual is therefore exact and smaller: Nashify this positive-mass
distinct-owner/collision replacement, or charge a finite block containing
it and construct the later exact payoff repayment.

**Corollary 73D (the plateau debtor retains uniform deleted reach to the
paid source).**  Keep the debtor `i` selected in Corollary 73B and put
`e=D_*/n`.  For every row let

```text
O_i(q_s)=product_(k!=i)(1-q_s(k)).                       (73.21)
```

Then the exact source-matched prefix satisfies

```text
d_i(X^(s+1)) <= O_i(q_s)d_i(X^s),
d_i(X^t) <= [product_(s<t) O_i(q_s)] d_i(X^0).           (73.22)
```

Consequently

```text
d_i(X^0) >= e,
inf_t product_(s<t) O_i(q_s) >= e/d_i(X^0) >= e/(2M).   (73.23)
```

Thus the permanent diffuse plateau cannot manufacture its positive debt
locally.  The same debtor already has fixed debt at the original off-minimum
source, and all of that debtor's opponents retain a uniformly positive joint
chance of reaching the supplied source through every finite prefix word.

**Proof.**  The exact semantic identity
`quittingTerminalSemanticDebt_prefix_eq_literalDefect_add_surcharge`
splits the new debt into the root's literal coordinate Nash defect at `U^s`
and its continuation-option surcharge.  The first term is zero because
`q_s` is exact Nash at `U^s`; the checked surcharge bound is at most
`O_i(q_s)d_i(X^s)`.  This proves the first inequality and induction gives the
second.  Along the subsequence of Corollary 73B the left side tends to
`d_i(X^infinity)>=e`.  Every finite product in `(73.22)` is therefore at
least `e/d_i(X^0)`.  Semantic debts are bounded by `2M`, proving `(73.23)`.

Suppose in addition that the **already floor-safe** initial carrier point is
supplied with a
specified realizing sequence

```text
Sem(sigma^r) -> X^0.                                    (73.24)
```

After passing to the subsequence in Corollary 73B, the profiles used in
Corollary 73C can be chosen as literal finite root prefixes

```text
zeta_j = q_(t_j-1) :: ... :: q_0 :: sigma^(r_j),        (73.25)
```

with `Sem(zeta_j)->X^infinity`.  Indeed, for each fixed finite word, semantic
prefixing is continuous and agrees exactly with literal profile splicing.
For each `j`, choose `r_j` inside the common neighborhood for the finitely
many intermediate prefix maps.  All intermediate semantic errors, the sum
of the resulting root-Nash defects, and the floor violation can simultaneously
be made at most `1/j`.  This is only a diagonal choice over a finite word;
no uniform modulus in the word length is assumed.

The same debt recursion with the small literal defects shows directly that
the product of opponent-Continue probabilities in `(73.25)` is eventually
at least (say) `e/(8M)`.  Equivalently, the selected full-replacement profile
is not merely a positive-reach continuation of `zeta_j`: it is reached by
all opponents of `i` with one fixed positive lower bound.  Since
`d_i(Sem(sigma^(r_j)))` tends to `d_i(X^0)>=e`, pure-time extremality together
with the exact stopping-law expectation formula selects two pure times at the
source whose payoff difference is at least `e/2`.  The paid-row decoder and
the exact prefix transport identity therefore give a paid first-disagreement
row for `i` on every sufficiently late `zeta_j`, with transported gain at
least

```text
(e/(8M))*(e/2)=e^2/(16M).                               (73.26)
```

There is presently **no automatic actual-paid specialization** of this
paragraph.  A `FullReplacementCluster` supplies `cluster_mem` and
`fullReplacement_tendsto`, and its mover debt is zero, but it does not supply
`cluster.1>=P`.  Proposition 73 and the present corollary require that floor
inequality before the first exact root is chosen.  Thus one may set
`X^0=endpoint.cluster` only after proving a separate floor-entrance theorem.
Conditional on that extra theorem, the zero mover debt would force the debtor
`i` in `(73.23)` to differ from the reset mover, and the displayed high/low
pure-time transport would apply to the full-replacement realizing sequence.
Without it, neither the distinct-observer specialization nor the eventual
paid-row conclusion is claimed here.

This removes the last **behavioral provenance and reach** ambiguity in the
one-way escape: a fixed paid row is carried on literal prefixes of the
original full-replacement source.  It deliberately does not call `(73.25)`
an exact root stack.  Its roots are exact at the semantic suffixes `X^s`,
while their literal profile suffixes are only arbitrarily close.  Upgrading
these simultaneous vanishing errors to exact punishment-floor roots without
losing `(73.26)` is now the sole Nashification obstruction; Sections 27, 33,
and 67 show that such an upgrade is not a consequence of proximity alone.

**Corollary 73E (the permanent diffuse escape is summable or reaches a
singleton support-entry wall).**  Retain the debtor `i` of Corollary 73D and
write

```text
h_t=q_t(i),                 H_t=1-O_i(q_t).              (73.27)
```

The lower product bound `(73.23)` implies

```text
sum_t H_t < infinity,
sum_t q_t(k) < infinity       for every k!=i.            (73.28)
```

There are exactly two clock cases.

1. If `sum_t h_t<infinity`, then the entire exact prefix charge is summable:

   ```text
   sum_t absorption(q_t)<infinity.                       (73.29)
   ```

2. If `sum_t h_t=infinity`, then the whole prescribed-payoff orbit, not only
   a subsequence, converges to the singleton reward vector

   ```text
   U^t -> r({i}),                 r({i})>=P.              (73.30)
   ```

   At this floor-safe vector either the maintained payoff-near-return family
   already exists as a one-edge self-loop of fixed positive charge, or some
   distinct owner `k` satisfies the strict support-entry wall

   ```text
   r_k({k})=r_k({i}) < r_k({i,k}).                       (73.31)
   ```

**Proof.**  A positive infinite product of numbers in `[0,1]` forces the sum
of their deficits to be finite, proving the first part of `(73.28)`; each
`q_t(k)` with `k!=i` is bounded by `H_t`.  Also

```text
absorption(q_t)=1-(1-h_t)O_i(q_t) <= h_t+H_t,
```

which proves `(73.29)`.

For the second clock case, compare `q_t` with the product row having the same
`i`-hazard `h_t` and every opponent continuing.  Coupling on the event that
an opponent Quits gives, coordinatewise,

```text
|U^(t+1)-[(1-h_t)U^t+h_t r({i})]| <= 2M H_t.             (73.32)
```

The errors in `(73.32)` are summable, `h_t->0`, and `sum h_t=infinity`.
The elementary affine-recursion formula (split its error sum at a late fixed
cutoff) therefore gives `(73.30)`.  The floor inequality passes to the limit.
Exact all-Continue Nash at that same limit gives

```text
r_k({k}) <= r_k({i})       for every k.                 (73.33)
```

For `0<a<1`, let `q^a` have only `i` Quit, with probability `a`, and use tail
`r({i})`.  Its successor is literally `r({i})`; player `i` is indifferent,
and outsider `k` has forced-Quit value

```text
(1-a)r_k({k})+a r_k({i,k})                              (73.34)
```

against Continue value `r_k({i})`.  If every outsider tied in `(73.33)` also
satisfies `r_k({i,k})<=r_k({i})`, then finiteness and continuity at `a=0`
select one `a>0` for which `(73.34)` is at most `r_k({i})` for all `k`.
Thus `q^a` is an exact floor-Nash self-loop at `r({i})`, of charge `a`; using
that same edge for every tolerance answers the maintained question.  If no
such `a` exists, a tied outsider violates the displayed collision inequality,
which is exactly `(73.31)`.  QED.

Corollary 73E consumes the entire nonsummable one-owner clock except for one
explicit outside-owner wall.  The wall is a strict table-level support-entry
datum, not yet an exact two-owner root or a chronological return.  The sole
diffuse architecture with neither the near-return nor `(73.31)` is therefore
the summable-total-charge branch `(73.29)`, where the original fixed charged
edge still requires a genuinely nonlocal payoff repayment.

This proposition does not yet return the payoff.  Its role is a
well-founded, source-matched dispatch of the two architectures required by
the maintained question.  Repeated nonperturbative collision is no longer a
free residual: while the singleton gap persists it strictly decreases the
explicit obstruction `D-D_*` by `D_* b` per row.  The only survivors are a
fixed `g`-coordinate excursion which later needs exact repayment, or a
quantitatively interior crossed-owner row.  In a one-owner separated face the
latter is exactly the required outside-owner activation; with a larger face,
additional incidence information is still needed to prove that `k` lies
outside the separated owner set.

The inputs are the reviewed Propositions 50--53, punishment-floor invariance
from `PunishmentFloorForward.lean`, prefix closure from
`TerminalSemanticPair.lean`, and
`QuittingPunishmentFloorFinitePrefix.collisionMass_mul_debtSum_le_debtDrop`
from `TightFaceCollisionSemanticDebt.lean`.  The conditional part of
Corollary 73D additionally uses
`quittingTerminalSemanticDebt_prefix_eq_literalDefect_add_surcharge`, its
checked opponent-survival surcharge bound, the exact stopping-law expectation
formula in `BehaviorStoppingPayoff.lean`, and the paid-row prefix transport of
Section 37.  This is ordinary mathematics.
Independent falsification review of Proposition 73 is in
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_73.md`;
the addenda there also validate Corollary 73A's compact recurrence consumer,
Corollary 73B's plateau/finite-envelope conclusion, and Corollary 73C's
carrier-matched positive-mass realization.  These statements do not finish
the one-way escape alternative.  Corollary 73D is a new conditional
provenance refinement and Corollary 73E is a new clock/support-entry
dichotomy; both await independent review.  The earlier claimed direct
substitution `X^0=endpoint.cluster` has been retracted because the endpoint
structure has no punishment-floor field.

### 74. What the actual large-base paid chain does—and does not—supply

Fermat's checked four-player proof mining puts the same terminal-witness
reward table into one of three strict-toggle residuals.  In the large-base
arm, the persistent and free labels form a `2+2` partition and the concrete
large-base gap enters the paid pure/mixed deletion dispatch.  This is genuine
actual-table provenance, but it is not the paid source used by the maintained
near-return consumer.

The distinction is literal.  A large-base source row has two persistent base
players Quit surely.  At the selected paid cell, one base label `c` has

```text
Continue payoff - Quit payoff >= gamma > 0.             (74.1)
```

Thus this source row is deliberately **not** a full-game exact Nash root.
The normality-sharpened output records strict failures of the candidate
deleted singleton-base or sure-exit compilers.  It stores reward-table cells,
binary Nash data, and toggle/floor residuals; it stores no terminal-semantic
tail, no prescribed vector above punishment, and no sequence of exact
Nash--Bellman predecessors.  In the pure arm the final
`HasSupportTwoNormalPaidChainResidual` predicate even forgets the original
quantitative paid inequality after using it to select the cell, although the
upstream large-base gap may be rerun to recover a paid pure/mixed alternative.

The contrast with the curvature paid source is therefore not terminological.
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow` is carried by the literal behavioral
profiles `frontier.fullReplacementProfile mover rank`, and its endpoint
cluster is an actual semantic carrier limit.  Even that stronger object has
no prescribed-payoff floor field.  Proposition 73 begins only after an
actual carrier point `X^0` with `U^0>=P` has separately been supplied.

There is nevertheless one sharp, source-native consequence of the original
large-base paid cell.  It shows exactly why the missing adapter must be
nonperturbative.

**Proposition 74 (quantitative root separation from a paid large-base
cell; unreviewed).**  Let rewards and the tail coordinates lie in `[-M,M]`,
with `M>0`.  Let `p` be the product row at an upstream paid large-base cell:
`c` and the other base label `d` Quit surely, the two free labels use their
selected pure or mixed binary-Nash rates, and

```text
Delta_c(p) := Quit_c(p_-c)-Continue_c(p_-c) <= -gamma.   (74.2)
```

The value in `(74.2)` is independent of the tail because `d` Quits surely.
For any product row `q` which is exact endpoint Nash at any tail in the
canonical box, one has the exact alternative

```text
q_c=0
or
sum_(j!=c) |q_j-p_j| >= gamma/(4M).                      (74.3)
```

In particular, on four players every exact root is at sup-norm distance at
least `gamma/(12M)` from `p` unless the paid base label has moved all the way
from sure Quit to sure Continue.

**Proof.**  For a fixed tail `U`, force `c` to Quit or Continue and couple the
two opponent product laws with parameters `p_-c` and `q_-c`.  Their mismatch
probability is at most

```text
sum_(j!=c) |q_j-p_j|.
```

The pointwise forced-action difference lies in `[-2M,2M]`, so the two
expected endpoint differences differ by at most four times `M` times that
sum.  The endpoint difference at `p` is `(74.2)` for every `U`.  If `q_c>0`,
exact endpoint Nash says that the supported Quit action has endpoint
difference at least zero.  Hence the two endpoint differences differ by at
least `gamma`, proving the second arm of `(74.3)`.  If `q_c=0`, the first arm
holds.  The four-player sup-norm bound follows by averaging over the three
opponents.  QED.

Proposition 74 is only a root-space separation theorem.  It does not turn the
large-base source into a floor state, does not orient the successor payoff,
and does not identify its labels with the mover/observer or the plateau
debtor of Proposition 73.  Consequently the conjunction of Fermat's checked
large-base residual and Corollaries 73A--73C on the same reward table gives no
exact stack by itself.  The missing common adapter is now exact and cannot be
hidden under the word “paid”:

1. produce a floor-safe semantic realization of the large-base paid cell (or
   of one of its source-retaining deleted cells) together with a simultaneous
   exact root; and
2. retain either the fixed marginal displacement `(74.3)` as literal charged
   collision/outside-owner motion or a later exact payoff repayment.

Without item 1, the large-base chain does not instantiate Proposition 73.
Without item 2, its fixed strategic displacement is another nonlocal source
move, not the requested payoff near-return.  Thus this audit closes the
direct-identification architecture but does not close the paid branch.

The checked declarations inspected for this boundary are
`paidPure_or_paidMixed_of_actual_largeBase_gap_labels`,
`PurePaidBaseLeaveSource`, `HasSupportTwoNormalPaidChainResidual`, and
`HasLargeBasePaidChainResidual` in the strict-toggle large-base modules;
`QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow`, `FullReplacementCluster`, and
`PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissiblePayoffNearReturnConsumer` in the curvature
paid-source modules.  Fermat Sections 5 and 7 supplied the route map, not an
additional theorem.  The reviewed ordinary-mathematics Proposition 29.2 in
Euler's singleton-dispatch note independently confirms the upstream boundary
cell orientation `(74.2)` while explicitly leaving its connection to the
full deleted-game Nash carrier open.

### 75. The large-base reselected source has only one possible floor deficit

The reviewed large-base deletion capstone gives a stronger semantic object
than the original paid boundary cell.  Keep its retained base label `d`, put
the other three labels in `F`, and let `z` be any exact Nash point of the
complete induced binary game on `F` with persistent singleton base `{d}`.
The terminal witness supplies a constant `delta>0` such that the
punishment-priced owner excess satisfies

```text
K_d(z) >= delta                                           (75.1)
```

at every such `z`.  Extend `z` to the ambient product row `q` by making `d`
Quit surely, and let `sigma` be the literal stationary profile which repeats
`q`.  Since `d` Quits at the first date with probability one, the prescribed
payoff vector is the one-row payoff of `q`; write

```text
X=(U,B)=Sem(sigma).
```

**Proposition 75 (one-coordinate floor localization; reviewed).**  For
every free label `j in F`,

```text
U_j=B_j>=P_j,                 d_j(X)=0.                  (75.2)
```

For the retained owner,

```text
B_d>=P_d,
d_d(X)=B_d-U_d >= K_d(z) >= delta.                       (75.3)
```

Consequently the prescribed vector has at most one punishment-floor
violation:

```text
max_j (P_j-U_j)_+ = (P_d-U_d)_+.                         (75.4)
```

There are then two exact source alternatives.

1. If `U_d>=P_d`, `X` is already an actual floor-safe semantic carrier with
   a unique positive debtor `d` and debt at least `delta`.
2. If `U_d<P_d`, take an actual cap-attaining best response of `d` against
   `sigma_-d` and call the resulting unilateral replacement `tau`.  Then

   ```text
   payoff_d(tau)=B_d(sigma)>=P_d,
   d_d(Sem(tau))=0,
   payoff_d(tau)-U_d>=delta.                             (75.5)
   ```

   Thus the owner crosses its floor with fixed source-matched gain; every
   floor violation which remains after the replacement belongs to one of the
   three free labels.

**Proof.**  Fix `j in F`.  Whatever complete behavioral strategy `j` uses,
`d` still Quits at the first date.  Hence only `j`'s first binary action can
affect its payoff.  The induced-game Nash property of `z` says that the
prescribed mixture attains the larger of those two action values.  Therefore
the unrestricted behavioral cap is exactly the prescribed payoff,
`B_j=U_j`.  The punishment value is bounded above by the cap against every
fixed opponent profile, so `P_j<=B_j=U_j`.  This proves `(75.2)`.

For `d`, put `Q=U_d`, let `beta` be the probability that every free player
Continues in one row, and let `N` be `d`'s payoff from always Continuing
against their stationary product profile (with the usual Never payoff zero
when `beta=1`).  The stationary unilateral cap is

```text
B_d=max(Q,N),                                             (75.6)
```

and the punishment upper leg gives `P_d<=max(Q,N)`.  The floor-priced
one-row Continue value is

```text
C_d(P_d)=(1-beta)N+beta P_d,                              (75.7)
```

including the boundary `beta=1`.  By definition,
`K_d(z)=C_d(P_d)-Q`.  If the maximum in `(75.6)` were `Q`, then both
`N<=Q` and `P_d<=Q`, so `(75.7)` would give `C_d(P_d)<=Q`, contradicting
`(75.1)`.  Hence `B_d=N>=P_d`.  Equation `(75.7)` now gives

```text
K_d(z)=C_d(P_d)-Q <= N-Q=B_d-U_d,
```

which proves `(75.3)` and `(75.4)`.  Finally the stationary cap is attained
by an actual unilateral behavioral strategy.  Updating only `d` to that
strategy leaves its opponents, and hence its cap, unchanged; its new
prescribed payoff equals the old cap.  This proves `(75.5)`.  QED.

This is the strongest honest connection presently available between the
checked large-base `2+2` residual and the paid semantic route.  It produces
an actual carrier source and localizes floor entrance to one scalar.  It does
**not** produce an exact Nash--Bellman row: `(75.3)` says the displayed sure
owner row is exploitably non-Nash.  In alternative 1 it supplies the
floor-safe-carrier part of Proposition 73, but not Proposition 73's singleton
reward gap `(73.1)`.  In alternative 2 the full replacement repairs the
owner and may lower the three free prescribed coordinates, so a simultaneous
floor return still requires the nonlocal repayment mechanism of Sections
56--60.  The remaining large-base obligation is therefore no longer an
unspecified four-coordinate floor problem: it is exact Nashification from a
unique-debtor floor carrier, or repayment of the free-coordinate damage made
by the cap-attaining owner replacement.

The proof uses the checked induced-Nash action inequalities in
`quittingPersistentBaseRoot_free_purePayoff_le`, the stationary cap identity
and `quittingPunishmentValue_le_stationaryUnilateralCap`, and the exact
definition of `quittingSingletonBaseOwnerFloorExcess`.  The large-base
selection and uniform `delta` are the independently reviewed Proposition
29.2 of Euler's singleton-dispatch note; this proposition is ordinary
mathematics and awaits its own independent check.

**Corollary 75A (the owner repair transfers the terminal gap outside;
reviewed).**  Let `Gamma>0` be the terminal exploitability margin.  Take
the cap-attaining owner replacement `tau` from the proof, whether or not
`U_d` was initially below its floor.  Since the owner's prescribed payoff
equals its unchanged cap,

```text
d_d(Sem(tau))=0.
```

The terminal gap applies to the actual profile `tau`, so some free label
`j in F` satisfies

```text
d_j(Sem(tau)) >= Gamma.                                  (75.8)
```

Consequently the large-base source has the exact semantic alternative

```text
payoff(tau)>=P,
  with a fixed positive debtor outside the repaired owner;

or

there exists j in F with payoff_j(tau)<P_j.              (75.9)
```

The first arm is an actual floor-safe outside-debtor carrier.  The second is
a source-matched off-diagonal floor loss caused by the owner's fixed-gain
repair.  Proof of `(75.8)` is immediate from terminal exploitability and the
zero owner debt; if the prescribed vector is not above punishment, its
violating coordinate cannot be `d` by `(75.5)`, proving `(75.9)`.

This is a genuine nonperturbative debt excursion from the selected
large-base source, but `sigma -> tau` is a unilateral strategy replacement,
not an exact reached Bellman edge.  The remaining repayment arm is now
precise: Nashify the floor-safe outside-debtor profile in the first case, or
construct exact floor edges which repay the explicit free-coordinate loss in
the second.  No label alignment with the independent curvature endpoint or
with Corollary 73B's plateau debtor is inferred.

Independent falsification review of Proposition 75 and Corollaries 75A--75B is in
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_75.md`.
It validates the induced-Nash unrestricted caps, the punishment upper leg,
the `beta=1` owner boundary, cap attainment, and the outside-debt/floor-damage
split, together with the stationary two-endpoint decoder below.

**Corollary 75B (large-base repair produces a literal paid row;
reviewed).**  Choose the free debtor `j` from `(75.8)`.  The owner repair
may be taken to be Always Continue, because the proof of Proposition 75 gives
`B_d=N>Q`.  Hence `tau` is again a stationary product profile.  Against its
fixed stationary opponents, let `Q_j` be `j`'s immediate-Quit value and let
`N_j` be its Never value.  The prescribed payoff of `j` lies in the closed
segment between these two endpoints, while

```text
B_j(tau)=max(Q_j,N_j).
```

Therefore

```text
|Q_j-N_j| >= B_j(tau)-payoff_j(tau) >= Gamma.            (75.10)
```

Orient the two pure times `some 0` and `Never` from the lower endpoint to the
higher endpoint.  The exact first-disagreement decoder then gives

```text
Nonempty (QuittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow reward tau j Gamma),
j != d.                                                  (75.11)
```

For completeness, the segment statement is the stationary one-player
fractional formula.  If the opponents' one-row absorption probability is
positive, the stationary payoff is

```text
theta Q_j+(1-theta)N_j,       0<=theta<=1;
```

the zero-absorption boundary is directly either eventual solo Quit or Never.
Thus `(75.10)` is exact, and
`exists_quittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow_of_pureTimePayoff_sub` proves
`(75.11)`.

In the first arm of `(75.9)`, this is a paid first-disagreement row carried by
an **actual floor-safe** profile and with observer distinct from the repaired
large-base owner.  This is a real floor entrance missing from the generic
full-replacement endpoint cluster.  It is still not the maintained return:
one standalone row has no simultaneous exact Bellman root or later payoff
repayment, and it is not asserted to be a rank of the independent tangent
frontier.  In the second arm, the same paid row is present but its source has
an explicit free-coordinate floor violation which must first be repaid.

### 76. The accepted paid row either enters the exact singleton stack or carries a fixed nonlocal atom

The accepted large-base handoff ends with an actual stationary profile
`tau`, a free debtor `j`, and the two pure-time endpoints `some 0` and
`Never`.  The prescribed payoff is `U_j(tau)`, the larger endpoint is the
unrestricted cap, and

```text
max(Q_j,N_j)-U_j(tau) >= Gamma.                         (76.1)
```

Here `Gamma>0` is the terminal exploitability margin.  Let

```text
M=max(1,max_(A,i)|r_A(i)|),       K=2^|I|,
alpha=Gamma/(4*M*K).                                  (76.2)
```

Choose the high endpoint and let `nu` be the corresponding literal unilateral
replacement of `j` in `tau`.  Thus `nu_j` is either immediate Quit or Never,
and every opponent strategy is exactly the one in the accepted large-base
source.

**Proposition 76 (source-native paid-endpoint atom; reviewed).**  There is
one terminal outcome `A`--Never or a nonempty quitting coalition--such that

```text
Pr_nu(A) >= alpha,
r_A(j) >= U_j(tau)+Gamma/2,                            (76.3)
```

where the reward of Never is zero.  Its incidence is forced by the paid-row
orientation:

1. if the high endpoint is immediate Quit, then `j in A`; hence `A={j}` or
   `A` is a genuine collision containing `j`;
2. if the high endpoint is Never, then `j notin A`; hence `A` is Never, a
   singleton owner `{k}` with `k!=j`, or a collision coalition disjoint from
   `j`.

In particular the accepted paid source cannot lose its fixed gain by
diffusing it over terminal outcomes or by changing the opponents: the gain
contains a fixed source-matched terminal atom with one of exactly these five
incidence types.

**Proof.**  Let `H` be the terminal payoff of the selected high endpoint, so
`H>=U_j(tau)+Gamma`.  Let `G` be the finite set of terminal outcomes whose
`j`-reward is at least `U_j(tau)+Gamma/2`, and put
`p=Pr_nu(G)`.  Every terminal reward and `U_j(tau)` lie in `[-M,M]`.  Splitting
the terminal expectation over `G` and its complement gives

```text
U_j(tau)+Gamma
 <= H
 <= p*M+(1-p)*(U_j(tau)+Gamma/2).
```

Consequently

```text
Gamma/2 <= 2*M*p,
p >= Gamma/(4*M).                                      (76.4)
```

There are at most `K` terminal outcomes, so one `A in G` has probability at
least `p/K>=alpha`.  This proves `(76.3)`.  Under immediate Quit by `j`,
absorption occurs in the first row and its quitting coalition contains `j`.
Under Never by `j`, no terminal quitting coalition contains `j`.  The listed
incidence alternatives are therefore exhaustive.  QED.

**Corollary 76A (immediate Quit gives the exact singleton stack or fixed
collision mass; reviewed).**  Suppose the first arm of `(75.9)` holds, so
`Sem(tau)=(U,B)` is an actual floor-safe carrier point, and suppose immediate
Quit is the high paid endpoint.  There is then an exact alternative.

1. The paid singleton itself has the half-gap

```text
r_{\{j\}}(j)-U_j >= Gamma/2.                            (76.5)
```

   Proposition 73 applies to `X^0=Sem(tau)`, gap player `g=j`, and
   `Delta=Gamma/2`.  Starting from this literal accepted source, arbitrary
   exact Nash predecessors form genuine punishment-floor Bellman edges.
   Before the reviewed finite collision budget is exhausted they produce
   either a nonlocal `j`-payoff excursion or a quantitatively interior owner
   `k!=j`; every such finite prefix contains an edge whose absorption is
   bounded below by the fixed constant from `(73.2)` with `delta=Gamma/4`,
   namely

```text
c=(Gamma/4)/(Gamma/4+2*M)=Gamma/(Gamma+8*M)>0.          (76.6)
```

   Infinite regeneration of that charge is already the maintained near-return
   by Corollary 73A.

2. The paid singleton fails `(76.5)`.  Then every outcome in the good set
   `G` from the proof of Proposition 76 is a collision containing `j`.
   Therefore the **total first-row collision mass** of the literal receiving
   profile satisfies

   ```text
   Pr_nu(|A|>=2) >= Gamma/(4*M),                         (76.7)
   ```

   and one such collision has the atom bound `(76.3)`.

**Corollary 76B (immediate-Quit exact-edge versus source-collision
dichotomy; reviewed).**  Keep the floor-safe immediate-Quit orientation of
Corollary 76A.  Let `p` be the stationary one-row product root of `tau`, let
`x=p_j`, and put

```text
h=1-product_(k!=j)(1-p_k),
c_0=Gamma/(8*M*(|I|-1)).                                (76.8)
```

The terminal-gap witness implies `|I|>=2`, and bounded payoffs imply
`Gamma<=2*M`, so `0<c_0<=1`.  Choose **any** exact product Nash root `q` at
the floor tail `U`.  Then

```text
h>=c_0
or
absorption(q)>=c_0.                                    (76.9)
```

In the first arm, `h` is exactly the first-row collision probability in the
literal receiving profile where `j` Quits immediately.  In the second arm,
prefixing `Sem(tau)` by `q` is a genuine exact punishment-floor Bellman edge
of fixed charge at least `c_0`.

**Proof.**  Let `Q` and `C` be `j`'s one-row forced-Quit and forced-Continue
values at the stationary row `p` with tail `U`.  Stationarity gives

```text
U_j=x*Q+(1-x)*C.
```

Since immediate Quit is high, `Q-U_j>=Gamma`; hence `x<1` and

```text
Q-C=(Q-U_j)/(1-x)>=Gamma.                              (76.10)
```

If `q_j=1`, its absorption is one and the second arm holds.  Otherwise
Continue has positive support in the exact root, so its endpoint condition
gives `Quit(q)-Continue(q)<=0`.  Coupling the opponent product laws of `p`
and `q` changes each forced-action payoff by at most
`2*M*sum_(k!=j)|q_k-p_k|`.  Applying this to the difference of the two
actions and using `(76.10)` yields

```text
sum_(k!=j)|q_k-p_k| >= Gamma/(4*M).                    (76.11)
```

Suppose the first arm of `(76.9)` fails.  Every `p_k<=h`, so

```text
sum_(k!=j)p_k < (|I|-1)c_0=Gamma/(8*M).
```

Equation `(76.11)` and
`sum |q_k-p_k|<=sum q_k+sum p_k` then give

```text
sum_(k!=j)q_k >= Gamma/(8*M).
```

Some opponent marginal is at least `c_0`, and joint absorption dominates
every marginal.  Thus `absorption(q)>=c_0`, proving `(76.9)`.  Exact root
existence is the finite mixed-Nash theorem; floor forward-invariance makes
its literal prefix an admissible edge.  QED.

The non-singleton alternatives in Proposition 76 are exactly nonlocal
source-matched exits, not local floor identities: the fixed first-row
collision mass `(76.7)`, a distinct outside owner, a collision avoiding `j`,
or Never.
They retain probability at least `alpha` and payoff premium at least
`Gamma/2` on the literal receiving endpoint `nu`.  What is still missing is
to make that event part of a simultaneous exact floor root and later repay
its absolute payoff displacement.  In the second arm of `(75.9)` the same
atom conclusion holds, but the already localized free-coordinate floor
damage must also be repaired before any admissible edge can start.

This section is not a new terminal-envelope lemma: its expectation estimate
is the one already used in reviewed Corollary 73C.  Its new content is the
exact source alignment with the accepted large-base stationary paid row and
the complete incidence split of its two pure endpoints.  It does not identify
behavioral event mass with exact-edge absorption and does not claim a payoff
near-return outside the singleton subbranch and the later recurrence consumer.
The declarations inspected are
`sSup_range_quittingTerminalPayoff_update_eq_pureTime` and
`exists_quittingPaidFirstDisagreementRow_of_pureTimePayoff_sub`, together with
the exact terminal-outcome envelope used in Corollary 73C.  Independent
falsification is in
`feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER__SECTION_76.md`.
The audit validates the expectation and pigeonhole constants, the five-way
endpoint incidence split, the Proposition 73 handoff, the total collision
bound, and the `4M` coupling/root-support estimate.  It confirms that the
non-singleton event mass is not an exact Bellman-edge charge.

### Closed architecture audit: the exact minimum spine never enters the floor box

The minimum-terminal-semantic inverse limit initially looks like a complete
connector: on its fixed-owner solo branch one may choose one controlled rate
`q>0` and iterate the same exact Nash prefix forever.  The checked iteration
keeps every state in the minimum-debt carrier, every edge has literal
absorption `q`, and the prescribed vectors converge.  Thus consecutive late
states have arbitrarily small payoff seams while retaining fixed charge.

This does **not** produce even one admissible state for the maintained
relation.  On the singleton-tight positive-debt face, every iterate has

```text
U_owner = r({owner})_owner,
P_owner-r({owner})_owner = D_min > 0.
```

The first identity is preserved by
`quittingSingletonTightMinimumFace_iterate`; the second is exactly
`singletonTight_debt_eq_punishmentGap`.  Hence every state in the apparent
charged recurrence violates `U_owner >= P_owner` by the same fixed amount.
Taking later iterates, compact limits, or shorter seams cannot repair it.
The checked terminal alternatives from this face are instead
`singletonTight_atomicHandoff_or_playerDeletion`: an outside-owner atomic
toggle or a strict decrease in player type.  Neither is itself the paid
floor-path connector.

**Global homotopy audit (also closed as a new route).**  A natural attempt is
to connect a charged exact root at a floor tail to the stationary boundary by
a discounted/anchored Nash homotopy.  Along such a branch the associated
one-edge Bellman seam tends to zero.  If the root absorption stays bounded
below, that one edge is already the desired payoff near-return; if it tends
to zero, however, the construction is exactly the existing analytic
projective packet/target-selection boundary.  In particular
`TargetMismatch.lean` gives a genuine analytic discounted branch for which
the limiting packet value is not the selected terminal target.  The homotopy
does not retain the paid full-replacement source and its parameter slices are
not consecutive reached Bellman states, so it supplies neither source-matched
collision debt nor a connector.  This is therefore not counted as a new
reduction.  The live capstone remains: obtain an actual floor entrance from
the off-minimum paid source and then a nonlocal exact repayment, or activate
an outside owner in a way that itself compiles the paid branch.

This closes the minimum-spine architecture only.  It is not a negative answer
to the maintained question and is not an export candidate.  Any successful
paid construction must use exactly the mechanisms which this solo spine
lacks: outside-owner activation or a source-matched nonperturbative
collision/debt excursion whose later floor-safe payoff repays the initial
seam.

## Proved ordinary-mathematics claims

- Proposition 73 passed independent falsification.  From
  an actual floor-safe singleton-gap carrier point, a literal exact prefix
  cannot remain in the half-gap region and avoid a quantitatively interior
  owner distinct from the gap player for more than
  `(D(source)-D_min)/(D_min*b)` collision rows.
  Each such row spends at least `D_min*b` of semantic debt, so the finite
  terminal alternatives are a fixed payoff excursion or crossed owner
  activation, and the prefix already contains a fixed-charge edge.
- Corollary 73A passed independent micro-review and gives the exact recurrence
  consumer: infinitely many rows
  above any fixed charge threshold produce the maintained varying payoff
  near-return by compactness.  Therefore a counterexample extension must
  have charge tending to zero and permanently retain the fixed gap-coordinate
  displacement from Proposition 73.
- Corollary 73B passed independent micro-review.  Every such permanent
  one-way orbit has a subsequential actual carrier all-Continue plateau with a
  fixed `D_min/n` Never, distinct-owner, or collision atom above one debtor's
  prescribed payoff.  Positive source mass and exact floor-root realization
  of that atom are not inferred.
- Corollary 73C passed independent micro-review.  A pure-time
  unilateral near-best response along actual profiles realizing the plateau
  retains gain `D_min/(2n)` and one fixed terminal-outcome mass at least
  `D_min/(8Mn*2^n)`.  This preserves the entire opponent environment of each
  plateau-realizing profile but does not make it an original paid residual,
  an exact floor root, or literal exact-edge charge.
- Corollary 73D is an unreviewed conditional provenance refinement.  Exact coordinate
  debt contraction forces the plateau debtor's deleted-player survival back
  to the original source to stay at least `D_min/(2Mn)`.  For the supplied
  realizing sequence of an **already floor-safe** initial carrier, the
  plateau profiles can be chosen as
  literal finite prefixes with vanishing aggregate root defect and floor
  error, and a source pure-time pair transports to a fixed paid row of gain
  at least `(D_min/n)^2/(16M)`.  Exact Nashification of those approximate
  literal stacks is not inferred.  A general full-replacement cluster has no
  checked prescribed-payoff floor field, so the actual paid endpoint cannot
  yet be substituted for that initial carrier.
- Corollary 73E is an unreviewed clock dichotomy.  The permanent plateau has
  summable hazards for every player except possibly its fixed debtor.  A
  nonsummable debtor clock converges to the singleton reward vector and gives
  either a fixed-charge exact self-loop or the strict outside-entry wall
  `r_k({k})=r_k({i})<r_k({i,k})`.  With no such wall, only summable total
  charge and nonlocal payoff repayment remain.
- Proposition 74 is an unreviewed source-separation estimate.  The upstream
  large-base paid cell cannot be repaired to any exact bounded-tail product
  root while retaining positive paid-owner hazard unless its opponent
  marginal vector moves by total `l1` distance at least `gamma/(4M)`.
  This certifies a source-native nonperturbative root displacement, but no
  floor entrance, payoff orientation, or chronology.
- Proposition 75 and Corollaries 75A--75B passed independent review.  A Nash point
  of the reselected three-free-player game gives an actual stationary source
  in which all free coordinates are at their unrestricted caps and above
  punishment, while the sole sure owner has debt at least the uniform
  owner-floor gap.  Thus only that owner can violate the floor; its
  cap-attaining full replacement crosses its own floor with fixed gain and
  zero own debt, leaving only off-diagonal floor damage.
- Corollary 75B is a reviewed decoder addendum.  The repaired stationary
  source's outside debtor has immediate-Quit and Never values separated by
  the terminal gap, hence carries a literal paid first-disagreement row with
  observer distinct from the repaired owner.
- Proposition 76 and Corollaries 76A--76B are reviewed.  The high pure endpoint of
  that same literal row carries one fixed terminal outcome with probability
  at least `Gamma/(4M*2^n)` and payoff premium at least `Gamma/2`.  The
  immediate-Quit orientation yields the paid singleton or a collision
  containing it; the Never orientation yields Never, a distinct owner, or a
  collision avoiding it.  In the floor-safe paid-singleton subbranch,
  reviewed Proposition 73 now supplies a source-native exact floor stack with
  fixed charge.  The non-singleton atoms are still behavioral event mass, not
  exact Bellman-edge charge.  In the immediate-Quit orientation, every exact
  root at the same floor tail additionally satisfies the source-collision
  versus exact-edge dichotomy `(76.9)` with threshold
  `Gamma/(8M(n-1))`.
- Proposition 59's source-matched full-reset dichotomy: the terminal gap at
  the simultaneous selected replacement, compared with each player's
  same-own-strategy vanishing-debt anchor, forces either fixed prescribed
  payoff loss or fixed cap escalation and hence fixed opponent-environment
  discrepancy.  The reached word consists of literal unilateral updates but
  is not an exact Nash--Bellman path.
- Proposition 60's bounded reached-transfer theorem: a tolerance-independent
  finite walk of approximate best responses under the terminal gap contains
  a fixed outsider payoff loss.  Best-responding next for that outsider gives
  either a low-debt negative re-entry or a fixed-gain paid recovery row.  Both
  propositions were independently validated in the Ramsey Sections 59--60
  feedback named above.
- Proposition 61's uniform semantic-to-edge seam: under the terminal gap, an
  exact floor root evaluated at a lifted tail differs from the literal
  semantic prefix by at least `kappa` times the tail displacement.  In
  particular cap seeding costs at least `kappa*gamma`, and floor clipping
  costs at least `kappa*floorDef(U)`.  The independent Ramsey Section 61
  feedback validates the theorem and its exact scope.
- Proposition 62's multi-debtor local-charge exclusion: along any actual
  source converging to a minimum with two positive debtors, exact roots at
  tails converging to the prescribed source payoff have absorption tending
  to zero.  The independent Ramsey Section 62 feedback validates the compact
  limit and conditional scope.
- Proposition 63's paid-coordinate localization: the curvature-selected paid
  observer either retains endpoint debt at least half its curvature and has a
  receiving pure-time recovery of at least one quarter curvature, or is a
  positive debtor at the minimum base with tangent at most minus half the
  curvature.  The quantitative constants are respectively `E/(2N)`,
  `E/(4N)`, and `-E/(2N)` for endpoint excess `E` and opponent count `N`.
- Corollary 63A and Propositions 64--65 give the reviewed arm-specific
  boundary: endpoint debt yields a tolerance-dependent reached recovery with
  fixed gain and vanishing updater debt, but those local fields can still
  Nashify only to zero charge; a negative normalized tangent can coexist with
  an exactly flat, ray-minimum-compatible full replacement whose entire
  monotone debt descent is only `lambda`.
- Proposition 66 gives an exact behavioral payoff return at every tolerance:
  the endpoint payoff vectors are equal, every intervening unilateral
  strategy update is a pure-time recovery of at least `3gamma/4`, and its
  updater's endpoint debt is at most `min(eta,gamma/4)`.  It is not a reached
  residual chronology, exact Bellman path, or absorption-charge producer.
- Proposition 67 gives an exact three-player regression for the attempted
  nodewise conversion of Proposition 66: a deterministic best-response cycle
  has gain two, zero updater debt and exact payoff return, while every
  floor-admissible tail has only the all-Continue root and only constant
  zero-charge outgoing admissible paths.  It is a local-interface no-go, not
  a terminal-gap or paid-frontier instance.
- Section 68 is a source correction, not a new theorem.  The exact binary
  strategy and terminal-semantic return is already the checked strict-toggle
  orbit in `StrictOrbit.lean`; the four-player simple-cycle bounds are checked
  in `ReachableSimpleCycle.lean`.  The elementary off-diagonal compensation
  ledger remains valid, but it is static toggle data rather than Bellman
  chronology or charge.
- Propositions 69--72 give the reviewed capstone reduction around that static
  orbit.  A global exact payoff shadow automatically pays fixed aggregate
  charge; floor clipping leaves either positive returned gain or an
  edgewise punishment-tight orbit; tight squares are impossible; and a
  persistent-base tight six-cycle forces a cross-corner premium of at least
  `gamma/2`.  None of these statements produces the missing source-matched
  exact continuation from the already charged current payoff back to its
  own tail.

- The Section 25A scope correction: the external opponent-block estimate is
  valid for two finite pure Quit times but false for the actual interface when
  the later time is `Never`; Section 3 supplies an exact zero-opponent-hazard
  paid row.
- Proposition 26: fixed whole-block absorption plus arbitrarily small endpoint
  payoff seams compiles to single-seam projective lassos at every error and
  hence to an unrestricted-behavior uniform payoff.  This is a direct
  specialization of named existing lasso identities, not a new producer; it
  was independently validated in
  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_EULER.md`.
- The Section 27 three-player regression: a gain-one paid row with unit
  opponent block absorption, exact receiving-witness optimality, prescribed

- The Section 28 capacity tests: fixed-charge replication `(R)` is bounded by
  the common prefix budget under a terminal witness; every fixed-aggregate
  block spends at least its aggregate absorption in raw charge, so literal

- Proposition 30's selected-ray incidence no-go: all local curvature,
  flat-column, source-excess, endpoint-separation, and moved-player debt data

- Proposition 31's exact global-minimum transfer identity and fixed
  subsequential recipient: the mover's nearly complete reset forces at least

- Proposition 32's diagonal-cap regression: the same floor-safe semantic pair
  supports a charge-one exact self-loop at its prescribed vector `U`, while
  its cap vector `B` admits only the all-Continue exact root.  This is an

- Proposition 33's sharp perturbation form: tails `U^epsilon -> U` from inside

- The Section 34 source synthesis: the existing constrained reset-face
  minimizer upgrades Proposition 31 to the exact transfer (34.1) and reduces

- Proposition 35's two-player timing regression: identical terminal semantic
  pairs and terminal coalition laws can assign gains one and zero to the same
  ordered pure-time witnesses.  Terminal-law retention therefore does not by

- Proposition 36's executable paid-port dichotomy: mixing only the observer's
  two witnesses retains the complete paid environment, makes total semantic

  does not retain zero mover debt or construct a floor edge.
- Proposition 37's exact paid-row prefix identity: literal reached prefixing
  scales the row gain by observer-opponent survival and otherwise preserves

  `B`, not loss of the row under an actual prefix.
- Proposition 38's exact cap-to-prescribed root criterion: tail lowering adds
  `O_i(q)d_i` to player `i`'s endpoint gap and changes the successor by
  `C(q)d_i`.  A sure-Quit cap root transports automatically with unchanged
  successor; without sure Quit, every active coordinate of a transported
  root has zero semantic debt and every inactive positive-debt coordinate
  must pay the displayed cap-slack toll.  This assembly was independently
  validated in the Ramsey Section 38 feedback.
- Proposition 39's dual upward-tail/floor-clipping criterion: tail lifting subtracts
  `O_i(q)e_i` from the endpoint gap.  Pure continuers transport
  automatically, mixed players must have zero lift whenever opponent
  survival is positive, and a pure quitter must pay the lift from strict Quit
  slack.  A sure quitter with a floor-safe own coordinate therefore gives a
  charge-one floor edge; Section 33 makes the missing own-coordinate/slack
  condition sharp.  This assembly was independently validated in the Ramsey
  Section 39 feedback.
- Proposition 40's exact sure-root re-entry criterion: a root with at least
  two sure quitters automatically becomes a charge-one self-loop at its
  successor.  With a unique sure quitter `k`, the same is true exactly when
  `K_k<=(1-O_k)Q_k`; failure is the literal collision-Continue premium (40.5).
  The current payoff is floor-safe by the exact predecessor bound.  This
  ordinary-mathematics result was independently validated in
  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_40.md`.
- Proposition 41's normalized-delivery criterion: a fixed positive root
  repeats to its stationary absorbing delivery exactly under the three
  support inequalities `(41.2)`, and the entire interpolating Bellman orbit
  stays above punishment.  In particular a wholly mixed moving root cannot
  repair itself by repetition.  This ordinary-mathematics proposition was
  independently validated in
  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_41.md`.
- Proposition 42's rigid-completion regression: the explicit nonnegative
  three-player table has a unique matching-pennies outsider completion under
  its sure quitter and a strict collision premium `3/20`.  Arbitrary
  outsider-Nash reselection with the sure quitter frozen is therefore not a
  universal repair.  This ordinary-mathematics proposition was independently
  validated in
  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_42.md`.
- Proposition 43's conditional-tail desaturation: a positive unique-sure
  premium has a canonical switch tail; if every mixed outsider's conditional
  indifference threshold clears punishment, halving the sure owner while
  keeping outsiders fixed gives an exact floor edge of charge at least

  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_43.md`.
- Proposition 44's threshold-failure regression: modifying only the
  sure-owner-unreached rows of Section 42 preserves its exact unique-sure root,

  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_44.md`.
- Proposition 45's outsider-Nash switch-tail compiler: any positive-survival
  outsider Nash completion whose owner switch value lies in the canonical
  interval `[P_k,M]` gives an exact changed-support floor edge with charge at
  least the chosen owner hazard.  The rational Section 44 repair has charge

  `(44/39,1/4,21/52)`, but no payoff return.  This ordinary-mathematics

  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_45.md`.
- Proposition 46's local regularity theorem: strict inactive outsider signs,
  an invertible active-gap Jacobian, and strict switch membership in

- Proposition 47's outsider-closed normal form: the regular continuation is
  uniform over the full boxed outsider-tail rectangle, and Brouwer closes all

  `[K-(1-O)Q]/O` in the owner coordinate; in the positive-premium arm it is at
  least `mu_0/2`.  This supplied-root result was independently validated in

- Proposition 48's repayment ledger: returning within `eta<a` after an
  owner-only seam `a` forces later negative owner motion and aggregate literal
  exact-edge charge at least `(a-eta)/(2M)`.  In the premium normalization,
  accuracy `mu_0/4` costs at least `mu_0/(8M)`.  This does not force one large
  later edge or control behavioral block hazard; it was independently
  validated in the Ramsey Section 48 feedback.
- Proposition 49's actual terminal-gap exclusion: no exact endpoint-Nash root
  at any boxed tail above punishment can contain a sure quitter, and
  compactness gives one game-dependent marginal ceiling `rho<1` for every
  such root.  On a forced-owner outsider Nash face,
  `O(H-P)=B<=-gamma` when `O>0`, while `O=0` makes the required owner equality
  impossible.  This direct adapter/compact reformulation overlaps the checked
  instant-punishment branch exclusion and was independently validated in the

- Proposition 50's singleton-gap first edge: a fixed boxed singleton gap
  `delta` gives absorption at least `delta/(delta+2M)` for every exact root;
  combined with the marginal ceiling, one selected active coordinate is
  bounded away from both zero and one and all joint/deleted Continue products

  validates the constants and limits the conclusion to one row.
- Proposition 51's explicit terminal-gap ceiling: every marginal of every
  exact boxed floor root satisfies `q_i<=1-gamma/(4M)`.  The proof forces a
  near-sure marginal to sure Quit, splices a genuine punishment continuation,

  not orient a later payoff transfer.
- Proposition 53's signed first-edge dichotomy: every singleton-gap root has
  a uniformly negative counterfactual joining account.  Either it is realized

  has a uniformly positive marginal while the gap owner remains latent.
  Ramsey's Sections 53--54 feedback validates the endpoint decomposition,
  bad-event estimate, collision product, and averaging constants; no later

- Proposition 54's common-level invariance regression: shifting one player's
  payoff by a common level on every terminal event containing an opponent
  preserves the endpoint difference, joining account, root, and charge while
  moving the Bellman successor by opponent absorption times that level.  The
  same feedback validates these identities and the explicitly local
  robustness scope; the reward bound, constants, and full paid frontier need
  not be invariant under the table shift.
- Proposition 55's literal-source dichotomy: a Continue-supported exact root
  at an actual singleton-gap payoff has gap-owner opponent absorption at least
  `delta/(delta+2M)` and transports that owner's semantic debt exactly by the
  complementary survival factor.  Either this spends at least
  `delta^2/(2(delta+2M))` of actual total-debt excess, or the original source

  `feedback/CODEX_CEDAR__PAID_ROW_REENTRY__BY_CODEX_RAMSEY__SECTION_55.md`;
  its second arm is behavioral hazard, not exact-edge charge.
- Proposition 56's absolute-level obstruction: a payoff loss `a` followed by
  target debt at most `eta` forces uniform opponent-environment discrepancy at

  `(a-eta)/(2M)`.  The actual reverse full reset has zero such discrepancy and

  producer of that movement.
- Proposition 57's corrected cap-capacity theorem: along a literal serial
  chain of unilateral resets, a fixed off-diagonal debt transfer `h` at every
  step forces an outsider payoff loss greater than `h/(2(n-1))` within any
  chain longer than `4nM/h`.  The proof uses invariance of the updater's cap
  and the bounded aggregate cap.  Ramsey's Section 57 feedback validates the
  corrected theorem and records that the independent base-to-endpoint paid

- Proposition 58's exact common-base seriality regression: both independent
  best-response resets can have positive own gain, zero own endpoint debt,

  their reached composition has zero outsider transfer and lands at an exact

  strict local-interface scope.
- The three elementary consequences in section 2, directly from the paid-row
  fields and probability nonnegativity.
- The two exact two-player row examples in section 3.
- The exact two-reset debt-instability regression in section 7.
- The audit in section 8 that the directed-transport consequences require an

- The compact payoff-closing positive-edge exactification proposition and its

- The orientation-wise uniform Nash-residual lower bounds for the natural

- The quantitative `theta/(4M)` earlier-branch Nash-correction lower bound,

- The two-player nonconvex Nash-root/Nash-payoff example, the nonconvex charge

- The strict two-player best-response-cycle regression in section 13, showing

- The declaration-level actual-source audit in section 14: mover debt control

- The exact nonperturbative joint repair in section 15: the section 13 game has
  fully mixed Nash root `(1/2,1/3)`, successor `(2/3,0)`, and charge `2/3`;
  at tail `(2/3,0)` its interior root instead has `(x,y)=(1/2,1/7)` and

  `x(z)=1/2`, `y(z)=(1-z)/(3-z)` has successor `T(z)=2/(3-z)` on

- The exact punishment values of the section 13 table are `(1,0)`, so that

- The alternating-sign two-player root formula
  `y*=A/(A+B)`, `x*=C/(C+D)` under the four strict endpoint signs.
- The source-matched forced-Q separation in section 16: any diagonal
  vanishing-error earlier paid subsequence has a forced-Q cluster row `p*`
  with `B(p*)>=0` and `D(p*)>=gamma`, while every forced-Q outsider-Nash row
  has `B<=-gamma` by the named checked atomic-completion theorem.  Mixed Nash
  existence for the auxiliary finite forced-owner game is ordinary

- The coupling estimate in section 16:
  `|B(y)-B(z)|<=4M||y-z||_1`, hence every forced-Q outsider-Nash completion is
  at least `gamma/(4M)` in outsider `l1` distance from the paid limit and one
  coordinate moves by at least `gamma/(4M(|I|-1))`.
- The one-outsider dichotomy in section 17: the actual forced-Q separation
  produces either a pure transferred-anchor Nash root or an interior mixed
  root over the punishment-value tail, hence a positive exact
  floor-admissible edge.  This is ordinary mathematics and still lacks a
  return.
- The exact three-player static no-go in section 18: the displayed reward
  table has punishment vector `(1,1,1)`, forced-owner paid/completion gaps
  `1` and `-2`, and unique endpoint-Nash root all-Continue at that tail.  This
  refutes a charged-root implication from the extracted forced-face data, not
  from the full terminal-gap hypothesis.
- The universal punishment-floor Nash dichotomy in section 19: a finite Nash
  row at tail `P` either has positive charge and is an exact floor-admissible
  edge, or it is all-Continue and the terminal gap selects a positive-solo
  owner with `P_o=r({o})_o>=gamma` and a joining obstruction at every positive
  solo rate.  This is an ordinary-mathematics assembly of named checked
  ingredients, not a new Lean declaration and not yet a return producer.
- The Section 18 dynamic-tail repair: at tail `(1,3,3)`, any singleton rate
  `0<x<=2/3` for owner `1` is an exact positive stationary self-loop.  This
  shows that the frozen-tail static obstruction does not survive the
  universal-joining alternative.
- The tight collision-cycle dichotomy in section 20: if the root game at tail
  `P` has no positive endpoint-Nash row, every punishment-tight owner has a
  distinct punishment-tight successor with strictly positive singleton-
  collision increment.  The finite tight set therefore contains a periodic
  collision cycle with a positive minimum edge margin `kappa`.  This is an
  ordinary-mathematics support reduction, not a Bellman-state return.
- The universal one-edge chronology identities in section 21: a tight
  collider requires successor-tail excess
  `p/(1-p)` times its collision increment; it cannot be the immediately
  following owner, reverse adjacency works in that coordinate exactly when
  the joint payoff is at most its punishment floor, and a strong joint payoff
  forces current labelled excess at least `p(r({o,j})_j-P_j)`.  These are
  ordinary Bellman/endpoint expansions and do not yet telescope to a return.
- The exact section 22 dispatch
  `collisionIncrement = preemptionGap + strongCollisionExcess`: every edge of
  the tight cycle carries either preemption margin at least `kappa/2` or
  current-excess forcing at least `p*kappa/2`.  This is a finite quantitative
  localization; the signed telescope across mixed edge types remains open.
- The exact consumer audit in section 23: the present collision and window
  reductions instantiate neither `VanishingDebtAtomChronologicalConsumer` nor
  `PaidFirstDisagreementAdmissibleReturnConsumer`; vanishing support-paid
  windows would instead be a distinct support-witness endpoint route.
- The section 24 seam-or-charge dichotomy.  A preemption-colored solo edge has
  one-phase support-paid normalized seam greater than `kappa/2`, and its
  exact repeated-row support error is at least `kappa/2` even after action-gap
  compensation.  A strong edge followed through the collider's next interior
  solo phase has either defect at least `kappa/(2K)` or intervening absorbed
  mass greater than `K` times the forcing hazard.  The scalar `A=-1,C=2` test
  shows why the strong case cannot use the one-phase passive estimate.
- The source-level audit in Section 25: checked payoff-cell preemption edges
  do not concatenate; table-justified observer-switch charging has
  nonpositive cyclic weight; and the paid profile spine has nonzero root
  defects at the dispatched row, so it is not an exact admissible chronology.
- The bounded-length compact exactification lemma in section 4.
- Necessity of some bounded-length or closing input for this compactness proof,
  witnessed by irrational circle rotation in section 5.
- The quantitative bounded-loop residual alternative in section 6.
- The paid-row fields alone cannot be *definitionally* reinterpreted as an
  admissible edge: their simultaneous-optimality, floor, bounded-state, and
  finite-return fields are absent.  This is an interface audit, not yet a
  mathematical independence counterexample.

## Unproved claims and possible bridge

- Floor entrance is prior to repayment.  Neither the curvature
  `FullReplacementCluster` nor Fermat's large-base paid-chain residual gives
  a prescribed vector above punishment.  Produce that floor-safe semantic
  realization before substituting either source into Proposition 73; same
  reward table and the shared adjective “paid” do not supply the field.
- Proposition 75 reduces the large-base version of that entrance to one
  scalar.  Close either its floor-safe unique-debtor arm by producing a
  charged exact root without assuming a singleton gap, or show that the
  owner's cap-attaining replacement repays every free-coordinate floor loss
  along a finite exact path.
- After Corollary 73E, the permanent diffuse arm has only two exact residuals:
  compile the strict tied-outsider wall `(73.31)` into the support-entry exit,
  or repay the original fixed payoff excursion along a summable-total-charge
  prefix.  The nonsummable one-owner/no-entry arm already gives a fixed-charge
  exact self-loop and is closed.
- Compile one of Proposition 73's two finite terminal alternatives.  In the
  excursion arm, construct a later exact floor prefix which repays the fixed
  `g`-coordinate rise.  In the crossed arm, prove that the interior second
  owner lies outside the separated paid face (automatic only for a
  singleton-owner face) and invoke an actual outside-owner compiler.  The
  collision/debt arm itself is now well-founded and is no longer an
  indefinitely repeatable residual.
- Current exact producer: construct arbitrarily accurate concatenable exact
  floor-admissible payoff shadows of the finite Proposition 66 update return.
  Proposition 69 and Corollary 69B then force a fixed aggregate absorption
  denominator automatically.  To answer the maintained one-edge structure
  literally also bound the total Bellman-row count; without that bound the
  reviewed aggregate compiler still closes the branch at existence level.
- Floor-tight alternative from Proposition 70: turn the finite family of
  pure opponent rows attaining each selected toggler's punishment value into
  an exact nonlocal floor path, or exhibit a table-level obstruction showing
  why these separately attained punishments cannot be assembled.  No such
  simultaneous assembly is claimed.
- Corollary 70A and Proposition 71 reduce that alternative further to a
  simple cube cycle of length at least six on which every selected move lands
  exactly at punishment.  The next finite question is whether an
  adjacent-opponent mixture along a longer cycle forces another cap below
  punishment, or whether a genuine six-cycle table survives.
- Main surviving producer: from the full separated full-replacement cluster,
  construct at every payoff tolerance one exact floor-admissible block with a
  uniform positive lower bound on aggregate absorption.  Proposition 27 shows
  that no theorem using only one paid row, its prescribed payoff, its block
  hazard, and receiving-witness optimality can do this.
- Alternative contradiction: show that the Section 27 strict-dominance
  erasure pattern is incompatible with the coordinatewise debt-change and
  exact mover-coordinate fields of the actual off-minimum endpoint cluster,
  or charge the resulting dominated Quit mass to a different source-matched
  exact block.  No such implication is proved.
- Candidate producer: build a uniformly bounded approximate detour satisfying
  section 4 from the actual full-reset subsequence.  Neither temporal dispatch
  nor the stopping-law endpoint currently supplies it.
- Candidate intermediate lemma: control the reset mover's semantic debt after
  replacing the paid observer by its selected receiving pure-time witness, as
  isolated in section 7.
- Alternative intermediate lemma: derive a scalar debt return, or a compactly
  closable approximate return, from the source/full-reset endpoint relation.
  The paid-row chronology alone does not state this.
- Sharper alternative: produce exact admissible edges from the actual paid
  subsequence with charge bounded below and current/tail payoff distance
  tending to zero.  A full equality of stored tail/current simplexes is not
  needed.
- Any Nashification of the literal reached roots must move by a nonvanishing
  amount; prove that a cap--Nash selection after this move retains a uniform
  positive absorption charge and enters the payoff-closing regime.
- Sufficient nonlocal datum: a continuous uniformly absorbing exact-Nash
  selection on a forward-invariant compact convex payoff region.  The paid
  carrier does not currently supply a selection or the invariant region.
- Weaker sufficient datum: a forward-invariant compact convex payoff/root
  carrier with one quit coordinate uniformly positive and with nonempty convex
  best-response intersections.  The paid seed does not make this carrier
  invariant, especially after the outsider correction.
- Alternative datum: observer-optimality invariant under all outsider
  corrections, or a finite acyclic rank for transferring the positive-quit
  anchor.  Neither is present in the paid carrier.
- Actual-source form of the same gap: prove two-reset stability from the
  tangent-family selection, extend support rank to off-minimum corrections, or
  construct a nonperturbative floor carrier directly from the endpoint
  cluster.  No named declaration currently supplies any of these.
- Joint-phase alternative: derive the two reverse face signs completing the
  section 15 rectangle, then couple its charged Nash root to a payoff-closing
  fixed point or finite exact floor-admissible return.  The current dispatch
  provides only the two forward comparisons.
- Sharpened actual joint-phase alternative: use Section 16's forced-Q
  separation together with a new owner-Continue boundary-degree condition to
  produce a joint Nash root, then prove floor-safe payoff closure.  The
  observer reversal no longer needs to be postulated separately.
- Chronological colored-cycle alternative: obtain an actual reached serial
  owner word from the paid source.  Section 24 then forces either a
  support-paid defect or charge amplification before each strong collider is
  reactivated.  No current declaration aligns the static label cycle with
  such reached phases, so this is not yet an exact consumer.
- The quitting specialization has an explicit residual formulation above, but
  no Lean wrapper is claimed here.  Its formalization would use the present
  closed-graph theorem and continuity of absorption mass.

## Checks and open objections

- Orientation checked: the charged relation runs from `tail` to `current`,
  although `IsQuittingNashBellmanEdge` lists `current` first.
- Positive paid gain is not positive absorption charge; those quantities live
  in different interfaces.
- The two small examples deliberately test only the paid-row interface.  They
  do not refute the stronger frontier-to-return producer, which also sees the
  witness, frontier, mover, endpoint cluster, and strict total-debt separation.
- Eventual nonemptiness of paid rows does not choose one row coherently across
  ranks.
- No fixed payoff target has been constructed.
- No Lean theorem, adapter seal, or downstream-consumer seal is claimed for the
  work in this note.

## Feedback wanted

Sections 38--65 are independently reviewed.  Euler's Section 66 review and
addendum validate the exact deterministic strengthening with the
strategy-update terminology now used.  On the floor-safe uniform
singleton-gap arm, the bounded-away-from-sure charged first edge is now
constructed.  Sections 59--60 now also construct macroscopic source-matched
opponent movement and, independently of reset stability, a bounded reached
behavioral walk ending in either a low-debt negative transfer or a fixed-gain
paid recovery row.  Section 66 closes the absolute-level recurrence problem
for behavioral profiles: at every tolerance, a finite pure-time unilateral
strategy-update subpath has equal endpoint payoff vectors, fixed gain on
every step, and small updater debt.  The one exact remaining gap is therefore
to convert such a behavioral payoff return into a path of simultaneous exact
punishment-floor Nash--Bellman roots while retaining fixed absorption charge.
Section 61 shows that the first charged edge can be given an
actual semantic continuation, but any later cap-seeded or floor-clipped root
has a payoff seam at least `kappa` times its tail lift; cap seeding costs the
fixed amount `kappa*gamma`.  Section 27 rules out direct prescribed-tail
Nashification from paid gain and low mover debt alone, while Section 33 rules
out transporting charge by tail proximity alone.  Section 62 further proves
that at a minimum with two positive debtors, every exact root at a tail
converging to that minimum's prescribed source has vanishing charge.  The
paid rows instead converge to a strictly off-minimum cluster, so this does
not exclude a local prescribed-tail root there; it only prevents treating
the original multi-debtor minimum as the charged local return port.  The live
paid obligation remains a root near the reached off-minimum prescribed payoff
or additional exact edges repaying the seam.  Section 63 now aligns the paid
observer with one of two genuine debt ports: a fixed positive-debt recovery at
the endpoint, or a negative tangent at the minimum base.  The first still
needs simultaneous exact floor-root Nashification; the second still needs a
reached serial realization.  Terminal law, cap vector, behavioral hazard, and
one-coordinate low debt are insufficient.  The exact surviving question is
whether the paid source/global frontier can Nashify this strategy-update cycle
into a punishment-floor exact path without losing fixed absorption charge.
